## PLAN-PAID-001 / SIGNUP-STEP-001 / GRANT-001 — nothing is free, nothing activates unpaid, and every exception is written down

### The free plan is withdrawn

| Requirement | Where it is enforced | How it is proven |
| --- | --- | --- |
| «البداية» costs `99 SAR` a month | `SubscriptionPlanSeeder` + migration `2026_08_04_100000_price_the_starter_plan` | `PlanCatalogueTest` «the entry plan is priced on both terms» |
| A paid annual option, managed from `/admin` | `subscription_plans.price_annual`, edited by `PATCH /admin/plans/{plan}` and the console's `PlanPrices` control | `platform-control.spec.ts` «the monthly and annual prices are editable and reach the public catalogue» |
| The annual price is shown clearly before payment | `PlanChooser` quotes the whole annual amount on the annual term; `GET /plans/{code}/quote` returns `due_now` | `registration-onboarding.spec.ts` «…quotes both terms before payment»; `RegisterPage.test.tsx` «shows the annual amount when the annual term is chosen» |
| The plan includes campaign tracking and reports | `features.campaign_tracking` / `features.reports` on the catalogue row — data, not marketing copy | `PlanCatalogueTest` «the entry plan is priced on both terms» asserts both flags |
| No free tier can creep back | — | `PlanCatalogueTest` «no offered plan is free»; `platform-control.spec.ts` «nothing on sale is free» |
| An application must name a plan and a term | `RegisterRequest` — `plan_code` and `billing_interval` are `required` | `PaidRegistrationTest` «an application with no plan is refused»; `RegisterPage.test.tsx` «refuses to apply until a plan is chosen» |

### Activation is a consequence of money, never of a browser

| Requirement | Where it is enforced | How it is proven |
| --- | --- | --- |
| No activated workspace before verified payment | `config/accounts.php` default `requires_payment: true`; `ProvisionWorkspace` refuses without a settled charge | `PaidRegistrationTest` «a verified application waits at the payment gate with nothing created» — 0 tenants, 0 users, 0 memberships, 0 subscriptions |
| Only a trusted payment record or a valid webhook activates | `ApplySubscriptionPaymentEvent::settle()` is the single call site of `AdvanceRegistration::paymentConfirmed()` | `PaidRegistrationTest` «an unverified webhook cannot activate an account», «a short payment does not activate an account» |
| Returning from the payment page activates nothing | There is no endpoint a browser can call to declare itself paid; the status page reads state from the server | `PaidRegistrationTest` «opening a checkout activates nothing»; `registration-status` reads `data-state` |
| Awaiting Credentials when Moyasar and Stripe are absent | `SubscriptionCheckout::open()` records `awaiting_credentials`; `GET /payments/providers` reports it | `PaidRegistrationTest` «with no gateway the checkout is honest and activates nothing»; `PaymentActivationSecurityTest` |
| Sandbox, Awaiting Credentials and Live are told apart | `GET /payments/providers` returns three states; `AccountStatusPage` renders the sandbox warning | `PaymentActivationSecurityTest` «the sandbox gateway is never reported as live», «…is inert in production» |
| A retried webhook does not charge or provision twice | `payment_webhook_events.event_id` unique + the idempotency key on the charge | `PaidRegistrationTest` «a redelivered webhook changes nothing» |
| After payment: account → subscription → workspace → first project → membership → role → permissions → portal | `ProvisionWorkspace` (workspace, client space, first project, role, membership) then `SubscriptionLifecycle::beginSubscription()` | `PaidRegistrationTest` «a confirmed payment runs the whole provisioning chain»; walked live end to end |

### The account step is a gate

| Requirement | Where it is enforced | How it is proven |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Every account field, password strength included, is validated on step one | `features/auth/registerValidation.ts`, called before `setStep(2)` | `registerValidation.test.ts` (16 tests); `RegisterPage.test.tsx` «will not move to the packages step while a field is invalid» |
| The error appears beside the field, before the packages step | `err()` feeds each control; the summary is filtered to the step in view | `RegisterPage.test.tsx` «names a weak password beside the password field, on the step that has one» |
| No error from a previous step on the packages step | `summaryErrors` is built per step; a server refusal about an account field sends the form BACK | `RegisterPage.test.tsx` «shows no account-step error on the packages step», «shows an ErrorSummary on a failed submit…» |
| Progress survives going back | Non-secret fields autosave (`useFormDraft`); the panel stays mounted so secrets survive in memory | `RegisterPage.test.tsx` «keeps the whole form across a round trip»; e2e «…not beside the price list» |
| One validation authority | `<form noValidate>` — the browser's bubbles no longer preempt our rules | the malformed-address case, which the native check used to swallow |

### An administrative exception, recorded and revocable

| Requirement | Where it is enforced | How it is proven |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Grant or remove extra permissions for one account | `account_grants` + `AccountGrants`; `POST`/`DELETE /admin/tenants/{tenant}/grants` | `AccountGrantTest` (12 tests); `platform-control.spec.ts` «granting needs a reason, and revoking needs its own» |
| Services and features tied to each plan | boolean features are switches on `/admin/billing`; `PATCH /admin/plans/{plan}` accepts `features` | `BillingPage.test.tsx` «turns a plan's services on and off» |
| Grant a subscription or full access free to a specific account | grant kinds `plan` and `full_access` | `AccountGrantTest` «full access is still bounded by the portal», «the console grants revokes and records the actor» |
| Revocable without changing other accounts | one row per account; revocation stamps that row only | `AccountGrantTest` «revoking one grant does not touch another account» |
| Suspend and reactivate | `PATCH /admin/tenants/{tenant}/status` (unchanged; data is preserved on suspension) | existing `TenantsPage` tests and `admin-console.spec.ts` |
| Actor, reason and date on every change | grants refuse a blank reason; the plan editor refuses to save until one is typed, and it is written to the `platform.plan.updated` audit row | `BillingPage.test.tsx` «…saves only a real change with a reason»; `platform-control.spec.ts` reads the audit back |
| Actor, reason and date on every grant | `AccountGrants::grant()`/`revoke()` refuse a blank reason and write an `AuditLog` row | `AccountGrantTest` «the console grants revokes and records the actor» asserts both audit rows |
| Fail-closed; nobody grants themselves anything | the routes sit behind `platform`; `AccountEntitlements` unions grants and can only widen | `AccountGrantTest` «a tenant owner cannot grant themselves anything»; `platform-control.spec.ts` «the console refuses an agency owner, at the page and at the API» |
| A grant cannot exceed the plan's portal | `nav()` intersects granted sections with `Portal::sections()` | `AccountGrantTest` «a grant cannot reach outside the portal», «a grant does not survive having no portal» |

### The sandbox gateway, and why it exists

`SandboxPaymentProvider` is a real adapter: it signs a webhook, verifies the signature in constant
time, and its event travels the same `ApplySubscriptionPaymentEvent` path Moyasar's does — including
the amount re-check and the idempotency key. It exists because PLAN-PAID-001 made every workspace
depend on a confirmed payment, which left an installation with no gateway credentials unable to walk
its own registration journey.

The alternatives were all worse: a "mark as paid" button, a policy that skips the gate off-production,
or a test that writes the paid row directly — each proves the product can be activated by something
other than money, which is the one thing this path exists to prevent.

It is inert in production twice over: the routes are not registered there, and `isConfigured()`
returns false on the environment name regardless of configuration. Its state is reported as
`sandbox` rather than `live` everywhere it surfaces, and the applicant sees «وضع تجريبي (Sandbox)»
above the Pay button.

## LOGIN-PATHS-001 / PHONE-VERIFY-001 / PHONE-SA-001 / PLATFORM-ORDER-001

### Two ways in, on one page

| Requirement | Where it is enforced | How it is proven |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `/login` keeps the marketing panel and gets a larger, balanced box | `LoginPage` renders through `AuthShell` — the component registration uses, not a copy | `auth-redesign.spec.ts` layout budgets; `auth-visual.spec.ts` baselines |
| Path 1 — email and password | `login-path-email` → `login-identify` → `login-password` | `login-paths.spec.ts` «both paths are offered…»; `LoginPage.test.tsx` |
| Path 2 — mobile number and a one-time code | `login-path-phone` → `login-phone` → `login-code` | `login-paths.spec.ts` «the national form signs in…» |
| Choosing a method is not choosing a portal | `login()` and the phone verify both claim `portal: null`; the destination comes from memberships | `login-paths.spec.ts` asserts every `login-portal-*` has count 0 |
| A contact with no password still gets a code | the email path asks `POST /auth/method` before rendering a password field | `SignInMethodTest`; `login-unified.spec.ts` code branch |
| A phone code opens a PLATFORM session, not a portal one | `POST /auth/phone/{start,verify}` — its own endpoints, because `/client/login/verify` opens a portal session | `PhoneSignInTest` (16 tests) |
| A phone code cannot be replayed | the challenge is `consumed_at`-stamped on use | `PhoneSignInTest` «a code cannot be used twice» |
| A portal code is not a platform credential | `purpose` must be `phone_sign_in` | `PhoneSignInTest` «a portal code cannot be replayed…» |
| No number enumeration | `start` answers identically for a number nobody holds | `PhoneSignInTest` «an unknown number is answered identically», «…creates no session» |
| A suspended account stays out on both paths | `PhoneSignInController::assertMaySignIn` uses the same `AccountSuspension` helper as the password path | `PhoneSignInTest` «a disabled account cannot sign in by phone» |

### A verified mobile number, for every account

| Requirement | Where it is enforced | How it is proven |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Mandatory for every account, including email registration | `config/accounts.php` → `requires_mobile: true` | `MobileVerificationGateTest` «a paid application with an unverified number creates nothing» |
| A number is required at registration | `RegisterRequest` → `phone` is `required` | `MobileVerificationGateTest`; `registerValidation.test.ts` |
| No activation without it | `AdvanceRegistration` holds at `MobileVerificationRequired`; `ProvisionWorkspace` never runs | `PaidRegistrationTest`; `registration-onboarding.spec.ts` walks the gate |
| The code is sent on arriving at the gate | `AdvanceRegistration::moveTo()` issues the challenge | the applicant lands on a code field with a code already sent |
| A wrong code clears nothing | `verifyMobile` refuses; `mobile_verified_at` stays null | `MobileVerificationGateTest` «a wrong code does not clear the mobile gate» |
| The administrator's shortcut cannot skip it | `RegisterTenantAction` refuses while any gate is open | `MobileVerificationGateTest` «…refuses while the mobile gate is on» |

### Saudi Arabia is the default

| Requirement | Where it is enforced | How it is proven |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `+966` is the default dial code | `PhoneField` → `DEFAULT_DIAL_CODE`; `PhoneNumber::DEFAULT_COUNTRY` | `login-paths.spec.ts` «the country opens on +966 and offers others» |
| Another country can be chosen | the `DIAL_CODES` select | same test — selecting `971` changes the code and nothing else |
| `05…`, `9665…`, `+9665…` accepted | `PhoneNumber::normalise()` | `MobileVerificationGateTest::saudiForms` (8 forms); `PhoneSignInTest::saudiForms` (7) |
| Spaces, dashes and Arabic-Indic digits accepted | same | same data providers; `platforms`/`phone` unit tests |
| Normalised to E.164 before storing or comparing | `NormalisesPhoneNumbers` on every model with a phone column | `MobileVerificationGateTest` «…stores the number in e164» |
| Duplicates prevented after normalisation | `UniquePhoneRule` compares in E.164, across users AND live applications | `MobileVerificationGateTest` «the same number cannot open a second application in another spelling» |
| No manual country code needed for Saudi Arabia | the placeholder is `05x xxx xxxx`; the national form is what the field expects | live walk-through and `login-paths.spec.ts` |
| Not client-side only | every rule above lives in `PhoneNumberRule` / `UniquePhoneRule` / `PhoneNumber` on the server | the backend tests above call the API, never the form |

### One platform order, everywhere

`سناب شات، تيك توك، ميتا، جوجل أدز، إكس، لينكدإن` — `App\Support\AdPlatforms::ORDER` and
`src/lib/platforms.ts`, read by the integrations overview, the connection centre, the dashboard
filter, the request forms, the report engine and the demo seeders.

| Where | How it is proven |
| --- | --- |
| Integrations overview | `PlatformOrderTest` «the integrations overview is in the products order»; `integrations.spec.ts` «the six ad platforms come first, in order» |
| Report engine | `PlatformOrderTest` «the report engine reads the shared order» — it holds the constant itself, not a copy |
| Connection centre, request forms | `sortPlatforms` / `sortByPlatform` at each site; `platforms.test.ts` |
| Dashboard filter | `platforms.test.ts` «is what the dashboard filter offers» — asserted on the list itself, because the control folds by default (SIMPLIFY-001) and a page-text check finds no platform names at all and passes for the wrong reason |
| Demo data | `DemoAnalyticsSeeder`, `DemoIntegrationsSeeder`, `demoOverview.ts` reordered |
| Every spelling ranks alike | `PlatformOrderTest` and `platforms.test.ts` — `google_ads`, `meta_ads`, `twitter`, `snap` all canonicalise |
| Sorting never rewrites a key | both order tests — these keys are API filters and connector ids |

## Two guards that were failing open, found by the gate

Neither was in the brief. Both were reached by running the four units end to end on three browsers,
and both are the kind of defect a status check cannot see.

| Defect | Why it was wrong | Fix, and how it is proven |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `OnboardingGate` read `account: null` as "onboarded" | It is null for two different reasons: the platform owner holds no membership BY DESIGN, and a payload whose workspace could not be resolved. Reading both the same way put a brand-new customer on a portal home for a workspace the payload could not even name — and left them there, because nothing re-decides once the navigation has happened. | The owner passes on the flag; anyone else with no account goes to `/switch`, the destination `resolvePostAuthOutcome` already falls back to. `OnboardingGate.test.tsx` (6 tests) covers both null cases and the loop the exemption prevents. |
| `GET /alerts/rules` returned every row | Fine on the day a workspace writes its third rule and wrong forever after: the payload and the number of cards rendered both grow without limit, so the page a customer opens to ADD a rule gets slower every time anybody adds one. The acceptance suite reached 316 and took the third browser of a run past ten seconds to paint. | Bounded at 100, newest first — so the rule just created is the one at the top and a cap can never hide it — with `meta.total` travelling alongside so the page says «تُعرض أحدث 100 قاعدة من 316» rather than presenting a truncated list as the whole set. `AlertsIsolationTest` «the rules list is bounded and leads with the newest». |

## MKT-UGC-001 — announcing a service that is switched off

The brief asks for two things that pull against each other: **tell visitors the influencer/UGC service
is coming**, and **keep the sub-system disabled behind `influencers_ugc_enabled=false`** — out of the
plans, the permissions, the internal menus, registration and the portal switcher, with no request or
account able to be created against it.

So the card is deliberately the one thing in the services grid that cannot be pressed.

| Decision | Why |
| --- | --- |
| A plain `<div>`, not a `<Link>` | While the flag is off there is no catalogue entry to open, no intake to pre-select and no portal to reach. A card that looked like its neighbours would be a dead end dressed as a service. |
| Rendered in the services grid, not a new band | The grid is `auto-rows-fr` at `sm:2 / lg:3`, so the card inherits the exact card size, radius, padding and type scale of the real categories. Measured live: 11 cards, every one 357×132 at 1280 and 343×156 at 375. The page did not grow and nothing shifted. |
| Dashed border + muted `STATUS_TONE.soon` badge | The same token the platform cards on this page already use for a "coming" state. The card reads as different without inventing a second visual language. |
| Copy lives in `homeCopy.ts` under `serviceAreas.soon` | It has no `to` and no `key` — nothing an intake or a catalogue could resolve, by construction rather than by discipline. |
| Hidden when the flag turns ON | Then the real service is in the catalogue above it, and announcing it twice would read worse than not announcing it. |
| Hidden when the catalogue fails to load | The grid itself is not rendered in that state; an announcement floating where a failed list should be would misrepresent the failure. |

`PublicHomePage.test.tsx` covers all six: the Arabic wording and badge, the English wording and badge,
**that the card contains no link and no button and that no href anywhere on the page mentions
`influencer`**, that it sits inside the real grid without displacing a category, and that it vanishes
on a catalogue error.

### MKT-FIX-001 — two defects the live review turned up

Neither was in the brief, and neither is visible from a passing test suite.

| Defect | What it actually cost | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The public homepage scrolled sideways on a phone | At 375px the header asked for 423px — logo 163, the two toggles 76, «Create account» 128 — so `scrollWidth` exceeded `clientWidth` by 31px and **every section of the page**, not just the header, could be dragged off-centre. Nothing in the row was droppable: the language and theme toggles are how an Arabic-first product is read at all, and the CTA is what the page is for. | The wordmark yields below 480px (`max-[479px]:hidden`) and the row tightens to `gap-2` on mobile; the gradient mark keeps the brand in a third of the width. Measured after: overflow 0 at 375 in both languages and both themes, wordmark back at 1280. |
| React warned «two children with the same key: /login» on every render | The footer's product column keys its links by route, and since the portals merged (LOGIN-UNIFIED-001) «تسجيل الدخول» and «متابعة طلباتي» are two different invitations to ONE door. React was free to reuse the wrong node between them, and the console error buried anything real that might have been logged beside it. | Keyed by label at both sites — the footer column and the hero's account actions. Verified by hooking `console.error` and remounting the whole page through an SPA navigation: zero errors. |

## External integrations — classification

**No provider is `Connected`, `Synced` or `Live`.** The structure, interfaces and tests are complete;
what is missing is the credential. Every external provider is **`BLOCKED_EXTERNAL_CREDENTIALS`** until
real credentials are entered AND OAuth succeeds AND an API round trip succeeds AND a real sync runs.

| Provider | Kind | Setup state read from the system | Classification |
|---|---|---|---|
| Snapchat | advertising | `not_configured` | `BLOCKED_EXTERNAL_CREDENTIALS` |
| TikTok | advertising | `not_configured` | `BLOCKED_EXTERNAL_CREDENTIALS` |
| Meta | advertising | `not_configured` | `BLOCKED_EXTERNAL_CREDENTIALS` |
| Google Ads | advertising | `not_configured` | `BLOCKED_EXTERNAL_CREDENTIALS` |
| X | advertising | `not_configured` | `BLOCKED_EXTERNAL_CREDENTIALS` |
| LinkedIn | advertising | `awaiting_credentials` | `BLOCKED_EXTERNAL_CREDENTIALS` |
| Salla | commerce | `not_configured` | `BLOCKED_EXTERNAL_CREDENTIALS` |
| Zid | commerce | `not_configured` | `BLOCKED_EXTERNAL_CREDENTIALS` |

LinkedIn alone reads `awaiting_credentials` because its required `version` field ships an API-version
default (`2411`) that is not an operator credential, so it counts as "partially configured". Both
states are honest, neither is connectable, and the difference is cosmetic — deliberately not patched
after a green gate. Any `provider_connections` / `external_accounts` rows in the dev database are E2E
artefacts, not evidence of a sync.

Residue reached **485 tenants · 791 client spaces · 610 users · 2105 tasks** |
| E2E-ISO-002 | QA | A running dev stack cannot be silently adopted by the gate | — | The gate runs on **:8100 / :5273**, not :8000 / :5173; the Vite proxy target comes from `VITE_API_TARGET` | — | **VERIFIED** | `5f0750f` | `reuseExistingServer` is on outside CI. On shared ports a dev server left running would be adopted and every isolation measure bypassed — with a green run to show for it. Different ports make that impossible rather than unlikely |
| E2E-ISO-003 | QA | Sessions, cache and queues are isolated too, not just tables | — | `REDIS_PREFIX=campaignshub-e2e-` in `E2E_BACKEND_ENV` | — | **VERIFIED** | `5f0750f` | `SESSION_DRIVER` and `QUEUE_CONNECTION` are both `redis`. Without its own keyspace the gate's `queue:work` would drain jobs a developer's own stack had queued, and vice versa |
| E2E-ISO-004 | QA | Residue can be removed from a shared database without deleting real work | `db:purge-e2e-residue` — dry-run by default, `--force` to write, refuses outside local/testing/e2e | — | `PurgeE2eResidueTest` (7 cases: 1 positive, 6 refusals) | **VERIFIED** | `5f0750f` | It does **not** match names, and cannot: the specs use the product's own default tenant names, so the database held 139 tenants called «My Agency» and 136 called «BrandCo» indistinguishable from something a person typed. It keys on RFC 2606 / 6761 reserved email domains, which cannot be delivered to and so cannot belong to a customer. A tenant goes only when EVERY member is such an account — one real member keeps it — and a `demo-*` tenant is never touched |
| E2E-ISO-005 | QA | The development database is back to the seed | — | — | — | **VERIFIED** | `5f0750f` | `migrate:fresh --seed` rather than the purge, deliberately: 269 of the client spaces sat INSIDE `demo-agency`, which the purge protects by design, and a local dev database holds no customer data — its only legitimate content is the seed. **12 tenants · 13 users · 7 client spaces · 1 task**, down from 485 / 610 / 791 / 2105 |

Proof the isolation holds: `agency-portal` + `client-command-center` on chromium, 11 passed. The converted client landed in `mediabuying_e2e` (client spaces 7 → 8); `mediabuying` stayed at 12 tenants / 7 client spaces / 13 users, unchanged.

## Unit 4 — The live sweep of all four portals (`PORTALS-SWEEP`)

Every route of `/app` (13), `/portal` (9) and `/admin` was loaded in a real browser under its own
account and read: rendered content, console errors, failure panels. `/agency` was walked during
`AGENCY-PERMS`. **Zero console errors, zero failure panels, zero empty pages across all four.**

| ID | AREA | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PORTALS-SWEEP-001 | UIQA | Every page of `/app` renders its own content under the advertiser account | — | — | live walk of all 13 rail destinations | **VERIFIED** | `b84e725` | dashboard · projects · campaigns · content · analytics · reports · alerts · tasks · files · connections (→ integrations) · subscriptions · settings. No console error, no failure panel |
| PORTALS-SWEEP-002 | UIQA | Every page of `/portal` renders under the client account | — | — | live walk of all 9 destinations | **VERIFIED** | `b84e725` | requests · quotes · invoices · campaigns · reports · files · messages · profile · home |
| PORTALS-SWEEP-003 | UIQA | The advertiser portal's `team` section has an address that resolves | — | `/app/team` → `/app/settings/permissions` | live | **VERIFIED** | `b84e725` | `Portal::App::sections()` offers `team` and the server sends it in `account.nav`, but the advertiser's team lives inside Settings (deliberately — an agency scopes people to clients, an advertiser has one workspace), so the address answered 404 while the identical `/agency/team` worked. A moved section redirects: ADR 0002 decision 5 |
| AGENCY-PERMS-006 | PERM | The failure classification reaches every surface a signed-in user lands on | — | `ErrorState` takes an optional `error` and delegates to `QueryFailure`; the whole `/portal` tree wired directly | `States.test.tsx` (5 cases) + 668 existing | **VERIFIED** | `b84e725` | Was PARTIAL at `23d64c2`. Fixed at the primitive rather than at ~30 call sites: `ErrorState` is what those screens already render, so one prop per call site replaced a rewrite of each. It also read «Retry» in English on every Arabic page in the product; it now takes the language from the interface when the caller does not say |

Live proof of the classification: `client@campaignshub.io` opening another client's space
(`/portal/clients/demo-store-analytics/quotes`) now meets `portal-failure-not-found` — «العنصر
المطلوب غير موجود · ربما حُذف أو أنه خارج مساحة العمل الحالية» — with no Retry button, where it used
to read «تعذّر تحميل عروض الأسعار» and offer a retry that could only repeat the refusal.

### Investigated and NOT defects — do not re-open

- `/admin/plans` and `/admin/subscriptions` answer 404. They are section KEYS in `Portal::Admin::sections()`, not routes: both live as tabs on `/admin/billing`, and nothing in the product links to either address. `portal-audit.spec.ts` already crawls every rail link in all four portals, and it passes.
- A login that returned 401 once, against a browser session that predated a `migrate:fresh`. Not reproducible under controlled conditions: a session pointing at a deleted user logs in cleanly (verified), and a stale CSRF token yields 419, not 401. Caused by dropping the database under a live browser — a developer-only condition.

### Found, not fixed here — belongs to the demo-data unit

`client@campaignshub.io` has **nothing** in any of its eight sections: no requests, quotes, invoices,
campaigns, reports, files or messages. Every page is correct and every page is empty, so the portal
cannot be demonstrated by signing in to it.

A mail domain is case-insensitive so nothing was undeliverable, but this address is PRINTED on the privacy policy, the terms, the contact page and the security page, and compared as a string in code. Mixed case in a published address reads as a typo. Live: `/contact` and `/data-requests` both render `info@campaignshub.io` and nothing else |

Inviting a customer to write somewhere no one is listening is the same failure as claiming a delivery that did not happen |
| IDENTITY-PROD-003 | ADMIN | Outgoing mail comes FROM the platform | `MAIL_FROM_ADDRESS` → `info@campaignshub.io` in `.env.example` and `.env` | — | — | **VERIFIED** | `1c1996c` | It was `hello@example.com` — the Laravel skeleton default. Every invoice, receipt, report and verification mail the product ever sent would have carried it |
| IDENTITY-PROD-004 | ADMIN | Every brand URL falls back to the production domain | `config/brand.php` | — | `PlatformIdentityTest::every_brand_url_falls_back…` | **VERIFIED** | `1c1996c` | Asserted against the DEFAULTS written in the config file, not the resolved values: those are whatever the machine running the tests has in `.env` (here `localhost:5173`), which is right for a developer and proves nothing about a deployment |
| IDENTITY-PROD-005 | ADMIN | A deployment on campaignshub.io knows exactly what to set | `.env.example` gains a Production block: `APP_ENV/APP_DEBUG/APP_URL/FRONTEND_URL`, `SANCTUM_STATEFUL_DOMAINS`, `SESSION_DOMAIN=.campaignshub.io`, `SESSION_SECURE_COOKIE=true`, `SESSION_SAME_SITE=lax`, the two addresses | — | — | **VERIFIED** | `1c1996c` | Each is load-bearing and each fails quietly: a missing Sanctum origin returns 401 rather than erroring (reads as an authentication defect), and a `SESSION_DOMAIN` without the leading dot leaves a customer who signed in on one host arriving at the other as a guest |

**Decision recorded:** the COMPANY identity (legal name, registration number, jurisdiction, address) stays database-backed and edited at `/admin/settings` — it is a fact about the company, not about the deployment, and a plausible default would put an invented registration number on a published privacy policy. The DOMAIN stays in the environment: changing a string in a database cannot move a deployment, so a UI for it would be a control that does not control anything.

## Unit 6 — One sync, one figure, on every surface (`UNIFIED-002`)

| ID | AREA | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| UNIFIED-002 | ANALYTICS | The same sync produces the same figure on the dashboard, both breakdowns, the ad funnel and the client's own link | — | — | `UnifiedFigureConsistencyTest` (4 cases, 31 assertions) | **VERIFIED** | `a08a6b2` | Was a draft in `docs/pending`. It asserts EQUALITY BETWEEN SURFACES rather than an expected value, so it fails the day a page grows its own query — whatever that query computes. Each figure is also pinned against a neighbouring project seeded at 999, because two surfaces that both leaked would still agree with each other |
| UNIFIED-002-A | ANALYTICS | The ad funnel states the spend it is derived from | `MetricsAggregator::funnel()` returns `['stages','spend']`; the controller keeps `data` as the stage list and puts the spend in `meta` | — | as above | **VERIFIED** | `a08a6b2` | **Real gap.** The spend was computed here all along — every `cost_per` on every stage divides by it — and then discarded. The funnel published a dozen derived figures and never stated the number they came from: a reader could multiply their way back to it and could not read it, and nothing reconciled the funnel against the dashboard. `data` is unchanged, so no caller had to move |

**Not a defect, corrected in the test:** the draft asserted `data.sources` / `data.state` on `metrics/freshness`. The endpoint serves the per-source list as `data` and the single verdict as `meta.summary.state`. The draft's expectation was wrong and would have failed a working product.

Derived metrics (`frequency`, `roas`, `ctr`, `cpa`) stay OUT by construction: `readKeys()` returns only the additive keys, and a stored daily frequency summed over a month is a number with no referent. Fails on the previous code at `landing_page_views` |

Each mapping line is a semantic decision: `clicks`←`swipes` (a swipe-up IS the click), `purchases`←`conversion_purchases` never `conversion_start_checkout`, `add_to_cart`←`conversion_add_cart` never `conversion_view_content`, `landing_page_views`←the delivery metric never `conversion_page_views`, `video_views` alone (the `_5s`/`_15s` variants are the SAME viewers at longer thresholds). `frequency` is derived and `engagements` has no Snapchat total, so both stay null rather than being manufactured. Pagination was absent: every list read one page and stopped, which is a complete-looking answer with entities missing |
| GADS-001 | INTEGRATIONS | Google Ads read-only adapter — sales by conversion category, not every conversion | Second `segments.conversion_action_category = 'PURCHASE'` GAQL query merged by campaign+day; `video_views` and `engagements` added | Feeds every section through the unified pipeline; the integrations page still says «Awaiting credentials» | `AdPlatformConnectorTest` ×2 new (lead-gen account: 260 conversions / 8 sales; absent-not-zero) | **BLOCKED_EXTERNAL_CREDENTIALS** | `b59a8cb` | Read-only, and **no OAuth round trip has been made**. Google Ads has NO purchase metric: `metrics.conversions` counts whichever conversion ACTIONS the account was told to count — a call, a form, a signup, a store visit, a sale — and `metrics.conversions_value` is the value assigned to all of them. Both were reported as `conversions` and `revenue`, so a lead-generation account read its enquiries as orders and its internal lead value as money taken. The only honest way to get sales from this API is to ask by category, so there is now a second query; the category is filtered in GAQL AND re-checked in PHP, guarding against this method being pointed at an unfiltered query later. Within the PURCHASE category actions ARE summed — two checkout flows, web and app, are different sales. No pagination gap: every read goes through `searchStream`, which returns the whole result set in chunks |
| X-001 | INTEGRATIONS | X read-only adapter — the metric groups the mapping reads, and both response shapes | `metric_groups=BILLING,ENGAGEMENT,VIDEO,WEB_CONVERSION`; `at()` understands the nested conversion object; window length measured across every series | Feeds every section through the unified pipeline; the integrations page still says «Awaiting credentials» | `AdPlatformConnectorTest` ×2 new (groups + both shapes; window measured across every series) | **BLOCKED_EXTERNAL_CREDENTIALS** | `01e3fa3` | Read-only, and **no OAuth round trip has been made**. The request asked for `BILLING,ENGAGEMENT` while `unroll()` read `conversion_purchases` (WEB_CONVERSION) and `video_total_views` (VIDEO) — X never returned either, so both were mapped from a key that could not arrive, which downstream is indistinguishable from a platform that does not report them. The conversion group also answers a different SHAPE (an object with the count under `metric` and money under `sale_amount_local_micro`), so reading only the flat array returns null for every conversion. Second defect found while widening: the window length was measured across spend, impressions and clicks only, so a paused campaign still converting from earlier impressions measured as ZERO days long and every conversion in it was dropped — the failing assertion on the old code reads «actual size 0 matches expected size 2». Four canonical metrics became seven; `landing_page_views` stays absent because neither `clicks` nor `conversion_site_visits` is the delivery metric this key means elsewhere |

The two refusals matter more than the metrics added beside them. **`revenue` is gone**: it was `conversionValueInLocalCurrency`, the value the advertiser ASSIGNED to their conversions — on a B2B platform commonly an internal worth put on a lead — so reporting it as revenue put a ROAS on a client's report built from money nobody had taken. **`purchases` stays absent**: LinkedIn's adAnalytics has no purchase metric and no way to ask for one conversion category the way Google Ads can, so `externalWebsiteConversions` is carried as `conversions` (what it is) and a sales funnel on a LinkedIn-only account ends in «لم تُرسل» — the truth. `engagements` is read as LinkedIn's own total rather than assembled from likes/comments/shares, which would count one interaction more than once. `landing_page_views` absent: `landingPageClicks` is the click, the moment before the arrival. **Open question for the operator:** if a LinkedIn account genuinely measures sales, this connector currently cannot surface them — revisit against real data once credentials exist |
| META-001 | INTEGRATIONS | Meta read-only adapter — semantic mapping, no double counting, pagination, fixtures | `MetaConnector::ACTIONS` (canonical ← action types, FIRST PRESENT WINS) · `pick()` never sums across types · `readAll()` following `paging.next` · `video_play_actions` for views | Feeds every section through the unified pipeline; the integrations page still says «Awaiting credentials» | `AdPlatformConnectorTest` ×5 new (triple-count, full mapping with view_content/link_click traps, absent-not-zero, cursor) | **BLOCKED_EXTERNAL_CREDENTIALS** | `8d53e25` | Read-only, and **no OAuth round trip has been made**. The audit found a live ARITHMETIC defect, not just a short mapping: Meta reports the same purchase under `offsite_conversion.fb_pixel_purchase`, `purchase` AND `omni_purchase` — three views of one sale — and `sumActions()` added them, so purchases, revenue and therefore **ROAS were multiplied by three**. On the previous code the new test reads «Failed asserting that 36.0 is identical to 12.0». The old comment shows how: it was written to fix the opposite worry (reading one spelling «halves the conversions»), and the fix for that is a fallback ORDER, never addition. Values are still summed WITHIN a type, because Meta repeats a type across breakdown rows. `video_views` moved from `video_30_sec_watched_actions` to `video_play_actions` — the thirty-second metric exists only for videos that long, so on fifteen-second creatives the product showed «video views» as unreported for ads watched tens of thousands of times. Absent never zero: Meta omits an action type when its count is zero, so «no purchase action» cannot be told from «no pixel», and null is the honest answer. Pagination was absent from all four edges plus insights — `limit=500` is a page size, not a guarantee. `engagements` IS mapped here (Meta publishes a single `post_engagement` total), with `page_engagement` deliberately excluded because the two overlap |
| FUNNEL-PURCHASE-001 | METRICS | The funnel stage labelled «Purchase» counts purchases, not every conversion | `MetricsAggregator::FUNNEL_STAGES` ends in `purchases`; the label map follows | Every funnel: analytics, dashboard, campaign centre, interactive report, client link | `MetricsTest::the_funnel_stage_labelled_purchase_counts_purchases_not_every_conversion` — a lead-gen week of 400 conversions and 3 purchases | **VERIFIED** | `7ef8be5` | The terminal stage read `conversions` while the label said Purchase. `conversions` is the sum of EVERY event a campaign was optimised for — a lead, an install, a registration — so on a lead-generation account the funnel ended in a count of leads under the word «الشراء». It hid because Snapchat maps both canonical keys from `conversion_purchases`, making them literally the same number and the label accidentally true; `7922a63` made TikTok the first platform to map them apart, turning a dormant mislabelling into a figure a client would act on. Found while writing that mapping and filed as its own unit rather than folded into it. `conversions` is NOT demoted — still in the pivot, still summed, still «النتائج» on every dashboard, asserted in the same test — it is simply not the sale. An account reporting no `purchases` now ends its funnel in «لم تُرسل» rather than in somebody else's number. `readKeys()` unchanged, so no pipeline change and no migration; the demo funnel is untouched at Purchase 1,143 because the seeders write both keys |
| TIKTOK-001 | INTEGRATIONS | TikTok read-only adapter — semantic mapping, pagination, fixtures, idempotent sync | `TikTokConnector::METRICS` (11 canonical ← TikTok fields) · `readAll()` following `data.page_info.total_page` under a page ceiling · metrics requested FROM the map | Feeds every section through the unified pipeline; the integrations page still says «Awaiting credentials» | `AdPlatformConnectorTest` ×3 new (mapping with a 500-vs-12 trap, absent/explicit-null, pagination) | **BLOCKED_EXTERNAL_CREDENTIALS** | `7922a63` | Read-only throughout, and **no OAuth round trip has been made** — the adapter is complete and the connection is not. The dangerous line on this platform: `purchases`←`complete_payment`, NEVER `conversion`, which counts every event the campaign was optimised for and on a lead-gen buy is a count of LEADS. `conversion` is still carried as `conversions` because it IS the platform's own results figure; what it must never be is the sale. Also `checkout`←`initiate_checkout` (a started checkout is not a completed one), `add_to_cart` never a content view, and `video_views`←`video_play_actions` ALONE — `video_watched_2s`/`_6s` are the same viewers at longer thresholds. `frequency`, `landing_page_views` and `engagements` stay ABSENT rather than approximated, and the fixture supplies the raw material for all three so an invented one is caught. `array_key_exists` plus an explicit null check rather than `isset`: TikTok sends JSON null for some fields and `isset` cannot tell that from a key never sent. Pagination was absent from campaign/adgroup/ad AND the report itself. **One line to confirm on the first real sync:** `initiate_checkout`'s exact reporting spelling could not be verified verbatim (the developer portal renders no documentation to a fetcher) — mapped anyway because a wrong spelling FAILS SAFE, returning no key, storing nothing, and showing «لم تُرسل» rather than a zero |
| FUNNEL-NULL-001 | METRICS | A funnel stage no platform reported is `null`, not a measured 0 | `MetricsAggregator::funnel()` drops the COALESCE and answers `reported` + `from_stage` beside `count` | Five surfaces draw this funnel — analytics, dashboard, campaign command centre, interactive report and the client's live link — and each now says «لم تُرسل» rather than drawing a zero bar | `MetricsTest::a_funnel_stage_nobody_reported_is_null_and_a_measured_zero_is_still_zero` (one window carries BOTH a genuine 0 and two never-sent stages) · `funnelUnreported.test.tsx` ×5 | **VERIFIED** | `3874ea8` | Found live at `7c115d4`. `COALESCE(SUM(…), 0)` made «this platform does not count basket adds» and «nobody added to a basket» the same figure on the most-read chart in the product; «0 add to cart» beside 176 purchases reads to a client as a broken funnel. A null sum now means no row exists for that key, while a real measured zero still sums to 0 and still says zero. `from_stage` names the nearest REPORTED step above, so a funnel with an unreported middle never divides by a step that is not on the screen — the rule `CreativeFunnel` already set for one creative. The dashboard's own copy of the chart was found by the COMPILER when `count` became nullable, not by reading: `null / top` is 0 in JavaScript, so it drew the minimum-width bar with «0» inside it, above the fold. Fails on the previous code with «Failed asserting that 0 is null» |

Live before the fix: CTR **210.0%**, معدل التحويل **479.6%**, استهلاك الميزانية **3028%**, funnel step **210%** — true values 2.1%, 4.8%, 30%, 2.1%. Not rounding artefacts but impossible statements (more clicks than impressions, a budget spent thirty times over) on the page an agency reads before it talks to its client, beside correct spend/results/ROAS figures that lend them credibility. It survived because nothing asserted a RENDERED percentage anywhere, and because the analytics page — where anyone would have checked — is correct. All three assertions fail on the previous code |
| COMPACT-ZERO-001 | METRICS | An abbreviated figure may be shortened, never denied | `compact()` keeps significant digits for a non-zero value below 1 | Every money and count display; the funnel's cost-per row most visibly | `format.test.ts` ×5 | **VERIFIED** | `aee8109` | `Math.round` turned a cost of 0.028 SAR per impression into «تكلفة 0 SAR» on the funnel — «this step is free», beside a bar that cost thirty-six thousand riyals. `CreativeDetailPage` had already met this and worked around it locally by refusing to show money on its bars; the funnel had no such guard and stated it outright. Fixed at the rounding rather than a second time at a second call site. A genuine zero still prints «0» and a null still prints «—» |
| ROUTE-BOUNDARY-001 | PLATFORM | A crash inside the app lands on a page, not on a raw stack trace | Every top-level route carries `errorElement: <NotFoundPage />`; routes declaring their own keep it | Any render error in any portal | `routerErrorBoundary.test.tsx` ×2 — the structural invariant plus the rendered page | **VERIFIED** | `04868f3` | The `*` catch-all had a boundary and nothing else did, so a render error anywhere bubbled to React Router's DEFAULT boundary: «Unexpected Application Error!» over a raw English stack trace in a customer's portal. Exactly how the campaign Funnel tab's crash presented before `0490892` — the crash is fixed, the PRESENTATION belonged to the router. The test measures the gap it closed: **141 top-level branches** had no boundary, not just the campaign route it was filed under |
| PUBLIC-REPORT-NOAUTH | REPORTS | `/r/<token>` works with no account, no session and no cookies — «انتهت جلستك» only inside /app, /agency, /admin, /portal | `isSessionlessSurface()` skips the `GET /auth/me` probe on `/r/`, `/reports/share/` and `/reports/print/`, setting the answer to null rather than leaving the store loading | The client's whole report: KPIs, platforms, campaigns, creatives, funnel, filters, downloads | `public-report-noauth.spec.ts` ×4 (10/10 chromium) · `LiveReportShareTest` ×3 new · `sessionlessSurface.test.ts` ×2 | **VERIFIED** | `135d18c` | The report already rendered without a session and still does. What it ALSO did — found by watching the network, not the screen — was probe `GET /auth/me` on every load of `/r/<token>` and get **401, twice**. Harmless while nothing renders a 401, one release from «انتهت جلستك» on a report belonging to somebody who was never signed in. Proved at three levels: the browser opens both addresses from an EMPTY storage state with cookies cleared AGAIN after arrival (storageState only governs what the context STARTS with), never leaves `/r/`, offers no `/login` link, carries the session copy in neither language, keeps its filters working with the jar emptied, and receives no 401/419 on any request; the backend has no auth middleware on any `reports/shared` or `reports/print` route, asserted structurally so one added to the group later cannot pass unnoticed; and the same token re-read after a later sync writes 250 more spend answers **350**, not the 100 it was created with — a live link that does not move is a snapshot with a longer name |
| DEMO-COMMERCE | COMMERCE | The merchant's ledger the demo never had | `DemoCommerceSeeder` — one Salla store, 657 orders across 90 days, products, customers, abandoned carts | Makes «الفانل والمتجر» and the Store-Confirmed block reviewable for the first time | Exercised by the live review above | **VERIFIED** | — | `COMMERCE-001` shipped the connectors, the tables, the resolver and the store half of the funnel, and **no seeder ever wrote one order** — so every install said «لا يوجد متجر مربوط» on all four store stages and nobody had seen that code run. The orders span every case the resolver distinguishes (`utm_campaign_id` · `click_id_platform_only` · `conflict` · `none`) and the platforms deliberately over-report against them, because a demo where the two agreed would make the attribution page look pointless. Everything is `is_demo`; the credential payload is a labelled placeholder; Salla and Zid stay **Awaiting Credentials** |
| AGENCY-ANALYTICS | UX | `/agency/analytics` exists | — | Mounted, not copied — the same `AnalyticsPage` component, per ADR 0002 | `navGrouping.test.ts` pin extended deliberately | **VERIFIED** | — | Every metrics route is `portal:app,agency` on the server and always has been; only the link and the URL were missing, so `/agency/analytics` answered 404 for the reader who runs media across five clients and most needs it. The same gap `/agency/integrations` closed under CONNECT-001. Found because this unit's own panel could not be reviewed live — the demo store project lives in the agency tenant, and no `/app` user can reach it |

## Unit 8 — The objective comes from the platform (`REPORT-OBJECTIVE-002`, completing)

| ID | AREA | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| REPORT-OBJECTIVE-002 | REPORTS | The objective is derived from the platform automatically, correctable by a person, every change audited, the original never lost | `PlatformObjectiveMap` (6 providers, both sides of Meta's 2022 rename) · `CampaignObjectiveResolver` · `objective_platform_value` · adoption in `CampaignLinker::link()` and after every `ImportExternalCampaigns` sweep · `campaign.objective.derived` audit action | `objective_provenance` on `UnifiedCampaignResource` — source, path, the platform's raw string, who corrected it, and a sentence saying what it means | `PlatformObjectiveDerivationTest` (11 cases, 45 assertions) | **VERIFIED** | `bdbb228` | Was PARTIAL at `42b2dbe`. `external_campaigns.objective` held what each platform reported and **nothing ever copied it** onto the campaign the reports read, so every imported campaign sat at the column default |
| REPORT-OBJECTIVE-002-A | REPORTS | A person's correction outranks the platform, permanently | The resolver refuses to touch `objective_source = 'manual'` | — | `…::a_manual_correction_is_never_overwritten_by_a_later_sync` | **VERIFIED** | `bdbb228` | The correction exists *because* the platform's answer was wrong. Letting a later sweep overwrite it would make every fix last until the next sync and no longer |
| REPORT-OBJECTIVE-002-B | REPORTS | An unrecognised value is never guessed at | `resolve()` returns null; the campaign stays `unset` | — | `…::an_unrecognised_value_yields_nothing_rather_than_a_guess` | **VERIFIED** | `bdbb228` | Google's `advertising_channel_type` is the trap: SEARCH is where an ad runs, not what it is for, and it serves lead and shopping campaigns alike. The fail-safe is asymmetric on purpose — `Other` sits on the awareness path, so an unclassified campaign's spend never reaches the sales CPA, and the cost of being wrong is a cost-per-order that is too LOW rather than one inflated by brand spend |
| REPORT-OBJECTIVE-002-C | REPORTS | Two linked platform campaigns that disagree are left for a person | The objective is adopted only when every recognised link agrees | — | `…::disagreeing_platform_campaigns_leave_it_for_a_person` | **VERIFIED** | `bdbb228` | A roll-up gathering one reach buy and one sales buy has no honest single answer, and picking either would be inventing one |
| REPORT-OBJECTIVE-002-D | REPORTS | The platform's own string survives a correction | `objective_platform_value`, migration `2026_08_09_110000` | shown in `objective_provenance.platform_value` | `…::agreeing_platforms_are_adopted_and_every_raw_value_kept` | **VERIFIED** | `bdbb228` | It is the difference between «the platform reported this and it is wrong» — a mapping gap to fix centrally for every campaign — and «the platform never reported anything», which is one campaign needing a person. With only `source` recorded, both read as `unset` |
| REPORT-OBJECTIVE-002-E | REPORTS | The platform's own word is kept even when nothing recognises it, so «wrong about this» is distinguishable from «never said» | `CampaignObjectiveResolver::sync()` collects every stated raw value, mapped or not, and writes `objective_platform_value` before returning on the unclassified path; `provenance()` names the value in its note | The provenance note reaches the campaign page, the report and the client link unchanged | `PlatformObjectiveDerivationTest` (+4, fail-first) | **VERIFIED** | `1f9efed` | `provenance()` promised the platform value was «kept whatever happens afterwards», and `sync()` returned before writing it whenever nothing mapped — which is the one case the column was added for. **Every Google Ads campaign lands there by design**: the connector reports `advertisingChannelType`, deliberately unmapped because a channel is where an ad runs and not what it is for, so an operator asked to classify one saw a blank with nothing to correct FROM, and could not tell it from a campaign the platform never described or a sync that had not run. The classification does NOT move and `objective_source` stays `unset`, so nothing downstream can read a recorded raw value as a classification and let that spend reach a sales CPA — asserted |

## Unit 9 — A summary and a full report of the same project (`REPORT-LINKS-13`, part 1 of 2)

| ID | AREA | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| REPORT-FORM-001 | REPORTS | «Executive summary» and «detailed» are a report's own axis, independent of live/snapshot | `reports.form` (migration `2026_08_09_120000`, backfilled from `type='executive'` or `audience='executive'`, defaulting to `detailed`); validated in `ReportController` | The builder offers the two forms as a labelled pair with what each contains | `ReportFormTest` (8 cases, 37 assertions) | **VERIFIED** | `37d7e99` | Three questions were sharing two columns: `type` (what a report is ABOUT), `mode` (whether a link recomputes) and — smuggled into both — «summary or full detail». `type = 'executive'` sat in a list beside `campaign` and `weekly`, so a monthly report could not be issued as a summary without claiming to be a different kind of report |
| REPORT-FORM-002 | REPORTS | A shared link honours the form | `PublicReportController::show` applies `ClientReportView::executive()` for a summary and `filter()` otherwise; `live` reports the form too | The client page names which document it is, in both languages | `…::the_summary_link_is_trimmed_and_the_detailed_link_is_not` | **VERIFIED** | `37d7e99` | **Real defect.** Every shared link ran the plain client filter, so a report an operator had deliberately built as an executive summary arrived in full detail. The setting was honoured in the PDF export and dropped on the link — the copy most clients actually open. Live: `/r/pYFa…` renders 4 slides, `/r/pLTO…` renders 6, both with no session |
| REPORT-FORM-003 | REPORTS | Either form can be live or a snapshot — all four combinations | — | — | `…::either_form_can_be_live_or_a_snapshot` | **VERIFIED** | `37d7e99` | A link is live only when given a scope to stay inside (LIVEREP-001). That is a fact about delivery and says nothing about whether the document is five pages or thirty |
| REPORT-LINK-URL-001 | REPORTS | The shareable link is the canonical `https://campaignshub.io/r/<token>` | `ShareService::urlFor()` from `brand.frontend_url`; three controllers return `url` (absolute) and `path` (relative) | The copy button uses the server's URL | covered by the share tests | **VERIFIED** | `37d7e99` | The page built it from `window.location.origin` — right on production and quietly wrong everywhere else. An operator reviewing on staging, a preview deployment or localhost copied a host only they could reach and sent it to a client: silent on the sending side, total on the receiving one |

**Found by driving the product, not by the tests:** the first cut of the endpoint validated `form` and then dropped it — `store()` builds its attributes explicitly and never copied it across, so every report came out `detailed` whatever was chosen. All eight tests passed anyway, because they build fixtures with `Report::create()` and never go through the controller. `…::the_form_survives_the_endpoint_and_is_reported_back` now closes that.

**§14.6, §14.7 and §14.8 are now closed** — see the unit below. §14.5 scope customisation
(platforms, accounts, ad sets, ads, creatives, objectives, paths, visible metrics, saved templates)
remains as recorded. §14.9 attribution transparency (`REPORT-OBJECTIVE-005`) is **VERIFIED** — see the five rows above.

**Carried, and named rather than left implicit:** the attribution block is on the OPERATOR's analytics tab. The client's link carries `conversions_basis` in its payload (so its «conversions» figure states what it is), but the full Platform-Reported / Store-Confirmed panel is not yet a section a client can open — that needs its own visibility switch on the link builder, since a new client-visible block without an operator toggle would show figures nobody chose to share.

## Unit 10 — The report itself keeps the two figures apart (`REPORT-OBJECTIVE-003/004`)

| ID | AREA | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| REPORT-OBJECTIVE-003 | REPORTS | The generated report and its client link carry Direct and Blended separately | `objective_performance` in the snapshot, built from the same `daily_metrics` as the rest of it — not a second source | `ObjectiveSplitSlide` — both figures side by side, each with its formula printed, plus every path and every excluded campaign named with its spend | `ObjectivePerformanceTest::the_generated_report_and_its_client_link_keep_the_two_figures_apart` | **VERIFIED** | `fea982a` | Live on the demo store, real data: **Direct CPA 73.72 · Blended CPA 90.95**, with 16,839 SAR of non-sales spend named. Rendered through the client link with no session |
| REPORT-OBJECTIVE-004 | REPORTS | A report never prints a bare `CPA` when it means a blended one | — | The executive slide's cards and its exact-figures strip read «CPA (مبيعات مباشرة)» from `direct`; the blended pair is shown under its own name | live journey | **VERIFIED** | `fea982a` | **Critical defect.** The summary slide printed `CPA` and `ROAS` straight from `kpis`, which rolls every path together. On this demo project it read 81.82 — neither the direct 73.72 nor the blended 90.95, since `kpis.cpa` divides all spend by ALL conversions including the lead campaign's. Three numbers, one label |
| REPORT-OBJECTIVE-004-A | REPORTS | The split survives into the summary form | `ClientReportView::EXECUTIVE_SLIDE_TYPES` keeps `objective_performance` | — | `ReportFormTest` + the live journey | **VERIFIED** | `fea982a` | The summary is where a blended CPA does the most damage: it is the version that gets forwarded and quoted, with no per-platform pages behind it to argue with. Trimming the section that says which figure is which would leave the most-read document the least qualified |
| UNIFIED-002-B | ANALYTICS | One definition of «an order» across every surface | `ObjectivePerformance` counts `conversions` alone | — | as above | **VERIFIED** | `fea982a` | It counted `purchases + conversions`, which made it more complete and made it DISAGREE with the dashboard, the breakdowns and the report's own `kpis` — all of which divide by `conversions`. Two definitions means the report's CPA and the dashboard's differ for the same scope and nobody can say which is the product's answer. One definition applied everywhere beats a better one applied in a single place |
| QA-FUNNEL-SHAPE-001 | QA | The snapshot's `funnel` stays a list of stages | Unpacked at both call sites (`ReportGenerator`, `LiveReportService`) | — | `ReportsTest::the_snapshot_funnel_stays_a_list_of_stages` | **VERIFIED** | `fea982a` | **Regression I introduced at `a08a6b2`.** Making the aggregator return `['stages','spend']` silently emptied the funnel slide of every report — `InteractiveReport` maps over `data.funnel`, and an object is not an array. The entire backend suite passed with it broken, because nothing asserted the shape of a key whose only consumer is a React component |

Every one of the seven list endpoints answers with rows; the attachment downloads its real bytes (200, `text/plain`) |

| PORTAL-BRAND-001 | PORTAL | The portal names the client on every page, not only where a slug happens to be in the URL | `ClientPortalController::branding()` resolves the single reachable space when none is selected | — | `AgencyWhiteLabelTest::a_contact_with_one_space_gets_that_spaces_brand_without_selecting_it` | **VERIFIED** | `2a03880` | **Real defect.** A contact who reaches one space is deliberately never asked to choose, so they live on the spaceless `/client/*` routes — where branding read «no slug» as «no client» and answered with the agency's brand. The portal greeted the client by name on its home page and called itself «CampaignsHub» on every other one. `…::the_merged_view_falls_back_to_the_agencys_own_brand` had a single-space fixture and so was passing for a reason unrelated to its name; it now has two spaces, which is what «merged» means |

Invisible until the demo client had campaigns on more than one objective to look at |

**Observed while filling it, not fixed here:** `message_threads.request_id` is a `uuid` column while external requests are ULIDs, so a conversation cannot be tied back to the request it is about (`MessagingController` validates that field as `uuid` and would refuse one). The column is dormant rather than broken — nothing writes it today — and the seeder leaves it null rather than forcing a value.

## Unit 12 — What a report covers (`REPORT-SCOPE-001…006`, §14.5)

| ID | AREA | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| REPORT-SCOPE-001 | REPORTS | One scope object across twelve axes — client, project, platforms, accounts, campaigns, ad sets, ads, creatives, objectives, marketing paths, date range, metrics | `ReportScope` (`app/Domains/Reports/Support`), `reports.scope` jsonb, `MetricsAggregator::forAccounts()` | — | `ReportScopeTest` (15 cases) | **VERIFIED** | `73ec6dc` | The scope was three things in three places: the project on the report row, a campaign list on the share, a date range re-derived from the period columns. Seven more axes in that arrangement would have meant seven fields written once and read — or forgotten — in four places |
| REPORT-SCOPE-002 | REPORTS | The scope narrows EVERY section, not the headline | Applied once in `ReportGenerator` to the engine the KPIs, platforms, campaigns, timeseries, funnel and budget all read; `ObjectivePerformance` takes the platform and account bounds too | — | `ReportScopeEndpointTest::excluding_a_campaign_removes_its_spend_and_results_from_every_section` | **VERIFIED** | `73ec6dc` | The mandatory acceptance case. A scope honoured by four of seven sections leaves a report whose parts do not add up to its own total — discovered by a reader in front of their client. The split slide is bounded for the same reason: it is what somebody consults when the headline looks wrong |
| REPORT-SCOPE-003 | REPORTS | A scope can only ever narrow — fail-closed | `ReportScope::intersect()`; an emptied intersection becomes an impossible id, never an empty list; the controller filters foreign ids the same way | The picker DELETES an emptied axis rather than sending `[]` | `…::asking_outside_the_ceiling_matches_nothing_rather_than_widening_it`, `…::a_foreign_id_is_dropped_without_reopening_the_axis`, `ReportScopePicker.test.tsx::omits_an_axis_it_has_emptied` | **VERIFIED** | `73ec6dc` + `3afdd44` | An empty list reads as «no bound», which is exactly how a fail-closed filter turns into an open one. Both ends of the wire had to agree on that, so both are asserted |
| REPORT-SCOPE-004 | REPORTS | Honest about grain | Ad sets and ads resolve UP to their campaigns; `explain()` states `figures` / `campaign` / `creatives` per axis | The picker prints the note beside those axes, not in a footnote | `…::an_ad_set_narrows_to_its_campaign_and_says_so`, `ReportScopePicker.test.tsx::warns_that_ad_sets_and_creatives_do_not_narrow_the_figures` | **VERIFIED** | `73ec6dc` + `3afdd44` | Platform insights are ingested at campaign grain, so a spend figure presented as one ad set's would be wrong by an unknown multiple and uncheckable by the reader. A stale selection whose rows are gone resolves to nothing rather than to «the whole project» |
| REPORT-SCOPE-005 | REPORTS | Editable without creating a new report | `PUT reports/{report}/scope` — same id, regenerated, audited as `report.scope.updated` | Row action → editor that loads the stored scope first | `…::a_reports_scope_is_edited_in_place_and_the_report_regenerates` | **VERIFIED** | `73ec6dc` + `3afdd44` | A second report would leave the first live, unchanged and wrong, with the client holding it. The editor starts from the stored scope because showing an already-narrowed report as «nothing selected» invites a save that silently widens it |
| REPORT-SCOPE-006 | REPORTS | Saved as a reusable template | `report_scope_templates` (project-scoped or tenant-wide), CRUD endpoints | Save/apply/remove inside the picker | `…::a_scope_can_be_saved_as_a_template_and_read_back`, `…::a_shared_template_cannot_name_project_specific_selections` | **VERIFIED** | `73ec6dc` + `3afdd44` | A tenant-wide template may not name one project's campaigns: applied elsewhere the axis resolves to nothing and the report comes back empty, which reads as a data problem rather than as a template that never applied |
| LIVEREP-003 | REPORTS | A live client link's ceiling covers the new axes | `LiveReportService` applies accounts, objectives, paths, ad sets and ads from the SHARE only | — | `LiveReport*` (18 cases, unchanged and still green) | **VERIFIED** | `3afdd44` | Read from the share and never from the query string: a filter no reader can set is a filter no tampered URL can loosen. The ceiling's campaign list is passed into the resolution so an ad set cannot pull in a campaign the ceiling never granted |

**Live evidence (demo store, one report narrowed in place):** whole project → sales campaigns on Meta and Google. Spend `88,866.92 → 60,006.88` SAR · campaigns `15 → 6` · platforms `4 → 2` · `funnel_spend` tracking `kpis.spend` exactly · Direct CPA `73.72 → 84.85` · Blended CPA `90.95 → 84.85` · non-sales spend `16,839.34 → 0`. Direct and Blended converging is the arithmetic proof that only sales-path campaigns remained.

**Found while verifying, not a defect in this unit:** the first regeneration returned the OLD figures because a `queue:work` daemon from an earlier session was still holding pre-change code. Restarting the worker made the scope take effect. `docs/RESUME_STATE.md` already carries this warning for `artisan serve`; it applies to the queue worker just as hard, and this is the second time it has cost a live-verification cycle.

**Also observed:** `reports/scope/options` returns an empty `accounts` list on the demo store, because `DemoAnalyticsSeeder` writes `external_account_id` values that exist in no `external_accounts` row. The endpoint resolves real account names and honestly returns nothing rather than inventing them, so the account axis cannot be exercised on demo data.

## Unit 13 — §15 creative analysis (in progress: slices 1–3 of the programme)

| ID | AREA | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CREATIVE-001 | §15.1 | Canonical creative model — asset, dimensions, duration, copy, CTA, destination, first seen, last active, raw provider payload | `external_creatives` +18 columns; `ExternalCreative` casts and `assetExpired()` | — | covered by the API tests | **VERIFIED** | `1687b27` | A creative was a name, a format and a thumbnail: enough to list, not to analyse. `raw` sits beside the printed columns so a mis-mapping is recoverable without a re-sync |
| CREATIVE-002 | §15.4, §15.15 | A metric the platform never reported is NOT zero | Video columns nullable (DEFAULT dropped); `CreativeMetrics` does not coalesce; response carries a `reported` map | — | `CreativeAnalysisTest` (3 cases incl. a real 0 kept apart from a missing metric) | **VERIFIED** | `1687b27` | `NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` made «this platform sends no quartiles» and «nobody watched it» the same row — «completion rate 0%» beside 40,000 impressions. The `reported` map exists because a genuine zero and a missing metric are both falsy in JavaScript |
| CREATIVE-003 | §15.5, §15.7 | A creative is judged by the job it was bought for; no overall winner across paths | `CreativeMetrics::headline()` / `comparable()`, both reading `MarketingPath::headlineMetrics()` | — | `CreativeAnalysisTest`, `CreativeLibraryApiTest::comparing_across_objectives_refuses_an_overall_winner` | **VERIFIED** | `1687b27` + `dd9068d` | An awareness video has no CPA. Printing one is a terrible number attached to content never asked to sell — it is what makes somebody switch off the top of their funnel. Per-metric winners still stand across paths; «better creative» does not |
| CREATIVE-004 | §15.9 | Fatigue is a pattern, and «insufficient data» is a verdict | `CreativeFatigue` — weighted signals, each needing data on both sides, plus spend-up-with-flat-results | — | 4 cases (fatigued, stable, improving, insufficient) | **VERIFIED** | `1687b27` | A single threshold condemns a quiet week and clears real decay. `stable` where we mean «we cannot tell yet» reads as «we looked and it is fine» when nobody has looked |

| QA-VITEST-001 | X | The frontend suite must not fail correct tests | — | `testTimeout: 15_000` against vitest's 5s default | 101 files / 704 tests / 0 failures | **VERIFIED** | `f31f6dc` | 7 failures, then 2, then 0 in isolation and 0 with changes stashed — and the slowest was logged at 5180ms against a 5000ms budget. A timeout is indistinguishable from a real failure in a gate, which teaches re-running until green: the habit that hides a genuine intermittent failure |

**Defects found by the fixtures:** `thumbnail_url` / `preview_url` / `destination_url` were `varchar(255)`, shorter than the signed platform URLs they hold — the sync would reject exactly the rows it most needs, or truncate to a link that 404s. Widened to 2048. And the credential guard withheld inline images, because `data:` is not an http scheme; allowed now, restricted to `image/`.

**Still to come in §15:** the E2E half of the acceptance tests (Light/Dark, Tablet, three browsers, PDF export ceilings). §15.6, §15.8, §15.10, §15.11, §15.12 and §15.13 are done, and carousels are supported end to end — see CREATIVE-014 … CREATIVE-049.

**Environment note for anyone re-verifying zoom.** In this headless browser `document.timeline.currentTime` is frozen at 0, so every CSS transition sits at 0% forever and an element carrying `transition-transform` computes to the identity matrix no matter what its inline transform says. Removing the class makes the same element scale exactly as specified (496px → 992px at 200%). Zoom is not broken; the clock is stopped. The same applies to `CreativeViewer`, which has always used this pattern.

**Stale demo rows in the development database — CLEARED.** 2,040 `creative_daily_metrics` rows carried fractional clicks and conversions, all written before the seeder fix at `93897cc`. `DemoCreativeAnalysisSeeder` was re-run while seeding the carousel cards, which rewrote them; the guard test was always the proof the seeders themselves were correct.

**Not offered rather than offered dead:** Product and Tag filters (priority 4). There is no `commerce_products`↔creative link and no creative tags table, so both would be controls that return nothing — which reads as «no data» rather than «this cannot be asked yet».

**Closed at the source (`93897cc`).** The fractional `conversions` behind «Orders 1,288.75» were two seeders keeping decimals through their arithmetic — see DEMO-INT-001.

**Stated rather than implied, in `CreativeVisibility`:** turning zoom or download off removes the controls and the URL behind them, and cannot stop a reader saving an image their browser has drawn. The bound that actually withholds a creative is EXCLUDING it, which is why exclusion is part of the ceiling and not part of the switch list — and why the operator's panel says so on screen.

**CLOSED at the cause (`17e7bae`).** The intermittent `DemoClientPortalTest` failures were one line:
`DemoPortalLoginsSeeder` picked the demo account's client space with `orderBy('created_at')->first()`,
and the demo agency's six spaces share a `created_at` to the second — so the ordering had ties, and
Postgres answered from physical row order. The account was scoped to an arbitrary one of six.

Why it never reproduced: in isolation the table is fresh, insertion order IS physical order, and the
tie always broke the same way. Only a full suite, where hundreds of rolled-back transactions leave
dead tuples for new rows to land among, reordered it — and which cases failed depended on which space
it landed in, so it never looked like the same failure twice.

Demonstrated, not assumed: the same `ORDER BY created_at LIMIT 1` over three tied rows returns a
different row after an unrelated row is rewritten; and pinning the seeder to each of two wrong spaces
made a different subset of the class fail (5 cases, then 4). Fixed by naming the space from the same
constant that fills it, with a totally-ordered fallback; two siblings carrying the same latent bug
were fixed with it, one of them production code. Verified by a full clean run: 1431 passed.
