A press and its release must reach the SAME element for a click to exist; the target moved in between, so no click event was dispatched at all — no error, no timeout, no effect. Measured before touching code, sampling each box every 100ms: the analytics tab fell 68px, the campaign tab 130px, and `More filters` moved 655px sideways — all within 200ms, all because a control mounts when its query lands. `min-h` on the tile was the first attempt and still left 10px, because a reserve a variant can exceed is arithmetic, not a constraint. Firefox only because it is the slowest here, so its settling most often overlaps the press. A person meets the same thing: reach for More filters and it moves out from under the cursor |
| PAY-AUDIT-001 | SUBSCRIPTIONS | The caps a plan sells are the caps the API enforces | `SubscriptionService::usage()` counts the thing itself — live projects, campaigns, seats (memberships **plus pending invitations**), unrevoked connections, this month's reports — instead of reading `usage_counters`, which nothing wrote; `EnsureWithinPlanLimit` wired to nine mounts across five metrics, including project `clone` and `restore` | The refusal already carried used/limit/upgrade_path; it can now actually be reached | `PlanLimitEnforcementTest` (8, all through HTTP), `SubscriptionTest` (5 rewritten to reach the cap by creating), `SubscriptionLifecycleTest` (1 rewritten) | **VERIFIED** | — | Not one of the five caps was enforced. `increment()` was the only writer of `usage_counters` and had NO CALLERS in `app/` — the table was written by tests and nothing else, so `usage()` returned 0 for every tenant and `withinLimit()` was permanently `0 < cap`. The suite missed it because `SubscriptionTest` reached the cap by calling `increment()` itself, three times, then asserted the comparison: it proved the arithmetic and never that creating a project moved the number. Feeding the meter would have been the wrong fix — projects, campaigns, seats and connections are STOCK, and a monotonic counter never returns a slot, so a customer who archived last quarter's work would watch capacity they had paid for ratchet away. Proven by reverting `usage()` alone: 7 of the 8 new tests fail against the meter. `clone` and `restore` are capped because archive-create-restore would otherwise walk around the cap in three keystrokes, and the seat is taken at the INVITE because since TEAM-INVITE-001 an invitation creates no `User` to count |
| CLICK-STABLE-002 | PLATFORM | A modal's actions do not move when its body fills in | `Modal` is a flex column with `max-h-[88vh]`: the title row and the `footer` row are `shrink-0`, the body is `min-h-0 flex-1 overflow-y-auto`. The report builder and the scope editor pass their actions as `footer` instead of rendering them under the picker | The buttons that finish the job are always at the bottom of the modal, reachable without scrolling a long body | The gate's `report-pdf-download.spec.ts`, plus the 928-test frontend suite over every modal in the product | **VERIFIED** | `79feafa` | The PDF export died on firefox with `waitForResponse` on `POST /reports` timing out after three minutes. A 403 would have SATISFIED that wait, so no POST was ever sent — the click on «إنشاء وتوليد» never became a click. Measured on firefox from the moment the modal opened: the button went `y=807 → y=1437`, 630px down within 200ms, because `ReportScopePicker` stands in at `h-40` for a picker that renders six to nine axis blocks and then grows a SECOND time when its templates query lands. Third distinct surface with the same defect after CLICK-STABLE-001, so the fix is at the shared primitive rather than at the caller. Proven by reverting the two files and re-measuring |
| PAY-AUDIT-004 | SUBSCRIPTIONS | A commercial refusal names what was refused and offers the way through | `EnsureEntitlement` returns the same envelope shape as `EnsureWithinPlanLimit` — `meta.entitlement`, `capability`, `plan`, `upgrade_path` — instead of `abort(403, …)`; `upgradeRefusalFrom` reads either shape out of `ApiError.meta`, and a `MutationCache.onError` in `providers.tsx` raises it for EVERY mutation in the product | `UpgradeRequiredDialog`, mounted once above the router: the server's sentence, the usage against the cap as a figure, the plan in force, an explicit «nothing was deleted», and a link to the plans | `upgrade.test.ts` (5), `UpgradeRequiredDialog.test.tsx` (5), `EntitlementMatrixTest` (+1) | **VERIFIED** | — | The backend already named an `upgrade_path` on the plan-limit refusal and NOTHING in `frontend/src` read it — `grep plan_limit\|upgrade_path` returned nothing, so the well-formed refusal reached the customer as a generic red toast. Wired at the MutationCache because there is one answer and hundreds of mutations, and `upgradeRefusalFrom` is deliberately strict about the two flags: most 403s are permission refusals, and telling somebody to buy a bigger plan when a colleague has not granted them a role would be worse than saying nothing. TWO defects were found by the live review and neither by the suite: `<Link>` throws above the router, where this is mounted, so the first live run logged «An error occurred in the &lt;Link&gt; component» and rendered nothing — the unit test had wrapped it in a `MemoryRouter`, a mounting condition the application never uses, and now renders it bare. And the first backend test asserted the new shape on `app/clients`, which proved only that `portal:agency` refuses first: `EnsureEntitlement` guards two route groups that both also carry `portal:agency`, so NO production route can currently trigger it — recorded, with `collaborations`/`roster`/`deliverables` named as the sections that will need it when the influencer module ships |
| PAY-AUDIT-002 | SUBSCRIPTIONS | Subscriptions are sold in USD; the advertising side still reports in SAR | `SubscriptionPlanSeeder` denominates starter/growth/scale in **USD**; `PlatformBillingController::updatePlan` accepts `currency` (3 letters, upper-cased so `usd` and `USD` cannot become two currencies) and audits it alongside the prices | The plan chooser and every quote state the currency they are in | `PlanCommercialTermsTest` (3 currency cases), `PlanCatalogueTest` (rewritten to read the catalogue rather than hard-code amounts) | **VERIFIED** | — | Owner decision of 2026-08-09, and it is a SEPARATION rather than a switch: plans, subscriptions and CampaignsHub's own billing are USD, while dashboards, analytics, reports and cross-platform comparison keep SAR as the reporting currency, always preserving each platform's original amount and original currency and converting only for the unified view. Agency→client invoices keep their own currency and do not follow the subscription currency. `currency` was the one commercial term `/admin` could not edit, so the catalogue was denominated by a seeder and only a deploy could change it. **Existing subscribers are untouched by design**: `assignPlan` captures `unit_amount` AND `currency` on the subscription row, so anyone already paying keeps what they agreed to — migrating them moves real money and is a decision, not a side effect |
| PAY-AUDIT-003 | SUBSCRIPTIONS | Every plan opens with a paid 30-day introductory month; the annual term is bought outright | `SubscriptionPlan::offersIntroFor($interval)` — the TERM decides, not the plan; `PlanCatalogue::quote` and `SubscriptionCheckout::forRegistration` both read it, so the quote and the charge cannot disagree; all three plans seeded at 30 days with an introductory price, starter included | `PlanChooser` says «أول 30 يومًا بـ …» instead of «تجربة», and shows it only on the monthly term | `PlanCommercialTermsTest` (7), `PlanCatalogueTest` (+1 annual-skips case), `PlanChooser.test.tsx` (+2), plus `SubscriptionLifecycleTest`/`PaidRegistrationTest`/`CommercialJourneyTest` rewritten to the new terms | **VERIFIED** | — | Owner decision of 2026-08-09: no free tier, no free trial, no 7 free days — a paid first month at an introductory price on EVERY plan including «البداية», which had been excluded on the argument that the entry plan is itself the affordable way in. Implementing it surfaced a real defect the old terms had hidden: `quote()` and the registration checkout both asked `offersTrial()`, which reads the plan and cannot see what is being bought, so an ANNUAL applicant was quoted and charged a symbolic first month and set renewing in thirty days — the public quote and the charge disagreeing about the same purchase. Two more found on the way: the signup badge said «تجربة» for a charge, and would have read «30 أيام» — the Arabic agreement error MAIL-007 and MAIL-014 each had to correct, since 11+ takes the singular accusative |
| SUB-USD-001 | SUBSCRIPTIONS | Every CampaignsHub subscription figure is USD, end to end | `config/subscriptions.currency` defaults to **USD** — it is the fallback every renewal, reactivation, plan change, proration and notification lands on when a subscription row carries no currency of its own, and it was `SAR`, so anything that fell back was quietly re-denominated. `config/billing.currency` deliberately stays SAR (an agency invoicing its client is a different party and a different transaction) | Verified live on a genuinely new account: signup, plan selection, checkout and billing | The live walk below, plus `PlanCommercialTermsTest` | **VERIFIED** | — | Walked with a real registration rather than asserted: the plan step showed `26.99 / 79.99 / 129.99 USD`, the sandbox gateway said «Authorise a payment of 8.99 USD», and `/app/subscriptions` showed `79.99 USD / شهرياً` with zero occurrences of SAR. The advertising side is untouched — `live-shared-report.spec.ts` still requires SAR in a report, which is the reporting currency and stays that way |
| SUB-COMMIT-001 | SUBSCRIPTIONS | The introductory price is not exploitable — 3 months minimum, on Growth | `subscription_plans.minimum_commitment_months` (editable from `/admin`, audited like a price) and `subscriptions.commitment_ends_at`, fixed from the plan AS IT STOOD when the customer paid; `SubscriptionPlan::commitmentMonthsFor()` ties the commitment to the offer, so it applies to the monthly term only; `chargeDueRenewals` keeps a cancellation pending and keeps charging until the commitment is served, then honours it without anybody asking twice | The disclosure states the commitment and what it totals | `MinimumCommitmentTest` (10) | **VERIFIED** | — | Month 1 at 8.99, months 2–3 at 79.99 — total 168.97. Without it the offer is an arbitrage: subscribe at 8.99, cancel on day 29, repeat, and the product is 89% off forever. Written so it is a TERM and not a trap: the customer can always ask, the request is recorded, nothing paid for is taken away, and `test_the_cancellation_takes_effect_once_the_commitment_is_served` exists precisely so the first test does not describe a trap. Editing the plan later cannot move a commitment already agreed — the same protection `unit_amount` has |
| SUB-CONSENT-001 | SUBSCRIPTIONS | The whole commitment is disclosed and explicitly agreed before any payment | `PlanCatalogue::quote` returns `regular_monthly`, `commitment_months`, `total_committed` and `next_payment_on` beside `due_now`; `commitment_consent_at` on the registration and the subscription; `SubscriptionCheckout::requiresCommitmentConsent()` lets the controller refuse **422**, with the service exception kept as the backstop for a caller that skips the controller | `CommitmentDisclosure` — six facts, each on its own line, and a checkbox that gates the Pay button | `MinimumCommitmentTest` (consent cases), `AccountStatusPage.test.tsx` (+1), `upgrade`/`PlanChooser` suites | **VERIFIED** | — | Every figure comes from the server's quote and none is recomputed in the browser: the same sum implemented twice eventually disagrees, and the half a customer is shown must be the half they are charged. Found by driving it live: the gate worked but answered **500**, because the service threw a `RuntimeException` and the handler rendered it as a crash with the exception text in `errors.exception` — the same mistake `EnsureWithinPlanLimit` had already recorded. A refusal that looks like a broken server tells somebody to try again later instead of ticking the box in front of them |
| LAUNCH-PRICING-001 | SUBSCRIPTIONS | Launch Pricing: Starter $19/$190, Growth $49/$490 (recommended, $9 intro for 30 days, 3-month commitment), Agency $99/$990, Enterprise by conversation | `SubscriptionPlanSeeder` reseeded to the four plans; migration `a_plan_can_be_sold_by_conversation` adds `subscription_plans.contact_sales` and renames `scale` → `agency` across plans, `tenants.subscription_plan`, `registration_requests.plan_code` and `subscription_payments.plan_code`; Enterprise is `is_public: false` so `PlanCatalogue::isOffered()` refuses it at checkout as well as hiding it; `SubscriptionController::plans()` excludes `contact_sales` and orders by `sort_order` | `PlanChooser` rebuilt: the regular price is the headline and the offer sits beneath it, at most four real differences per card, «موصى بها» on Growth alone, and the full table behind «مقارنة جميع المزايا» in a modal | `PlanCommercialTermsTest` (+3 incl. «enterprise is in the catalogue and not on sale» and «only a contact_sales plan may publish no price»), `PlanChooser.test.tsx` (12), `RegisterPage.test.tsx` (21), `registration-onboarding.spec.ts` «the signup path decides which plans exist, and every price is USD» | **VERIFIED** | — | `scale` → `agency` was checked before it was done: `tenants.subscription_plan` is only displayed or grouped, never compared to a literal, and plan codes share no namespace with `account_type` or the portals, both of which already use the word «agency» for something else. Enterprise uses a real `contact_sales` column rather than a 0.00 price because **0.00 already means free here** and «there is no free tier» is a rule with a test behind it — pricing it at zero would have forced that test to be weakened, which is how a real rule becomes a decorative one. The owner's later decision keeps Enterprise internal: active, editable in `/admin`, and absent from signup. The introductory figure is deliberately NOT the headline: leading with $9 sells a number nobody pays for more than a month, and the surprise arrives on the second charge |
| PLAN-FIT-001 | SUBSCRIPTIONS | The signup path is the kind of use, and it decides which plans exist | `planFit.ts`: `OFFERED` binds self-service → Starter + Growth and multi-client → Agency alone; `COMPARISON` reads only axes the catalogue actually publishes; `whyUpgrade()` computes the deltas from the catalogue so a repricing changes the sentence too | «كيف تريد البدء؟» is asked of EVERYONE (it used to appear only for visitors arriving from a homepage card), and the plan step renders `register-journey-required` instead of a catalogue until it is answered; the marketing panel follows the path | `PlanChooser.test.tsx` «offers the agency path one plan and nothing to compare it against», `RegisterPage.test.tsx` «will not show a catalogue before the visitor says how they will use it», `registration-onboarding.spec.ts` (live, 3 browsers) | **VERIFIED** | — | Both paths were being shown the same three plans, with Growth and Agency describing themselves in terms of agencies to somebody who had just said they run their own campaigns — a price list that does not change when the question changes is not a choice. Growth was briefly on the agency list too and does not belong there: client workspaces, per-client team scopes and white-label are Agency's, so offering Growth would sell a plan the customer must leave. `automation` and `advanced analytics` were named in the brief and **do not exist in the product**; they were deliberately not added, because a published axis nothing enforces is a promise nobody is keeping |
| LAUNCH-LIMITS-001 | SUBSCRIPTIONS | Every cap a plan publishes is measurable, enforced, editable from `/admin`, and returns used/limit/upgrade_path | `SubscriptionService::count()` counts client workspaces (live rows, archived and soft-deleted excluded); `EnsureWithinPlanLimit::class.':clients'` mounted on `client-workspaces.store` AND `.restore`; the campaigns mount REMOVED; `upgrade_path` now names the portal the person is standing in; `PlatformBillingController` accepts `contact_sales`, `trial_fee`, `trial_days`, `minimum_commitment_months` and `limits` | `/admin` billing gains the offer row (intro price/days/commitment), the five caps (blank = unlimited) and three separate availability switches — Active, Offered at signup, Sold by conversation | `PlanLimitEnforcementTest` (13: +4 clients, +1 «campaigns are not capped by any plan» which walks the route table), `PlanCommercialTermsTest` «every published limit is one the service can measure», `BillingPage.test.tsx` (9) | **VERIFIED** | — | The `clients` cap shipped in the catalogue and in nothing else — `usage()` had no branch for it, so it fell through to a meter nothing writes, read 0, and «1 client» admitted a hundred. Exactly the PAY-AUDIT-001 failure reproduced by adding a fifth cap without the count behind it; proven by stashing the route file, where both new cap tests fail. Campaigns are deliberately NOT metered: the plan is sold on what it costs us to hold, and charging per campaign penalises using the product properly — the mount was removed rather than left inert, because a gate that only passes for want of a published cap is one `/admin` edit from becoming a paywall nobody decided on. The upgrade path was hard-coded to `/app/subscriptions`, which was harmless until this cap existed: client workspaces are `portal:agency` only, so every clients refusal would have pointed the customer at a portal they were not in |
| AUTH-FIT-001 | AUTH | Signup fits a laptop screen without a horizontal scrollbar at any width | `AuthShell` grid tracks become `minmax(0,…)` and the form column `min-w-0`, so no wide child can force the page sideways; fluid `clamp()` sizing replaces fixed type and spacing in `AuthShell`, `AuthPanel`, `RegisterPage` and `PlanChooser`; the form measure WIDENED to 33rem, which made the page shorter by removing wrapped lines | Same layout, colours, components and copy — scale and spacing only | Live browser review at 1440×900, 1366×768, 1280×720, 1024×768, 768×1024, 430×932, 390×844, 375×667, in RTL and LTR and in both themes | **VERIFIED** | — | Horizontal scroll is zero at every one of those widths. Step 1 fits with no scroll at all on 1440×900, 1366×768 and 1024×768; on the plan step the primary CTA is inside the viewport at 1440×900, 1366×768, 1280×720 and 1024×768 in Arabic, with only the trailing «already have an account» line below the fold. **In English at 1366×768 the plan step still needs ~28px of scroll to reach the CTA** — English wraps more lines than Arabic, and the remaining options were shrinking text below a readable size, which the brief explicitly ranks below usability. Found and fixed during the review: the journey heading and its description sat at either end of one row and overlapped once both became sentences |

| GATE-WK-001 | E2E | Four webkit tests hang on `page.goto … waiting until "load"` in a full-project run | — | — | Reproduced twice with the tree, once with the whole tree STASHED | **BLOCKED_PRE_EXISTING_DEFECT** | — | `/login`, `/app/integrations` and `/admin` — the three routes that read provider/credential status on mount. Each passes in isolation and as a group of four; only the full 279-test webkit project reproduces it, and the fourth failure MOVES between runs between two sibling tests that both `goto('/admin')`, so it is positional rather than a defect in any one assertion. Attribution is settled: stashing all 58 changed files and re-running the full webkit project fails the same four, so this predates Launch Pricing / PLAN-FIT-001 / LAUNCH-LIMITS-001 / AUTH-FIT-001. Lead: a hanging outbound call in a provider-status endpoint would block a worker of the single `php artisan serve` (`PHP_CLI_SERVER_WORKERS=4`); with all four blocked, later requests queue and `load` never fires inside 30s — which fits «only deep into a long run, never in isolation». NOT closed by a retry, a raised timeout or a weakened assertion |

## §19 — Closing the non-external open items (2026-08-10)

> These four rows CLOSE earlier rows above. Where an earlier row says `PARTIAL` or
> `BLOCKED_PRE_EXISTING_DEFECT` for the same id, the row here supersedes it and names what closed it.
> Nothing here was rebuilt: two were already built and carried a stale status, and two were real.

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GATE-WK-001 | E2E | Four webkit tests hang on `page.goto … waiting until "load"` in a full-project run | — | — | The gate itself: chromium 287 · firefox 279 · webkit 279, `REAL_GATE_EXIT=0` | **VERIFIED** | `47c9ef9` | Supersedes the `BLOCKED_PRE_EXISTING_DEFECT` row in §18. Root cause was ours and had nothing to do with a provider endpoint: `.env` sets `REPORTS_CHROMIUM_ENABLED=true` and the gate never overrode it, so `GenerateReportJob` opened a SECOND headless browser against `{E2E_ORIGIN}/reports/print/…` and pulled the SPA module graph out of the very Vite server the tests were driving. Proven by disabling Chromium printing (4 failures → 2) and then fixed durably by giving the print browser its own frontend on **:5373** — switching printing off would have removed the proof that the exported Arabic PDF is a real Chromium file, which is coverage this product had already had to earn once |
| AGENCY-PERMS-006 | PERM | The failure classification reaches every surface a signed-in user lands on — including the dropdowns | `PanelState` accepts either the caller's own sentence (verbatim, unchanged) or the raw error, and classifies it: permission / session / not_found / retryable, with Retry rendered ONLY on the arm retrying can fix | The ~19 taxonomy-backed selects across clients, requests, alerts, campaigns and reports each LOST a ternary instead of gaining one | `optionsFailure.test.tsx` (5, both languages) | **VERIFIED** | `7f20c61` | Closes the «inline `optionsError` on taxonomy selects» remainder of the `PARTIAL` row at `23d64c2`. Every one of those selects printed «تعذّر تحميل الخيارات» for a refusal, an expired session, a deleted list and a dead server alike, beside a Retry whose only possible outcome on three of those four was the same answer again — the exact defect `QueryFailure` closed for full-page surfaces, still alive inside the controls because the classification lives in a component the dropdowns do not render. The influencer surfaces named in the old row are behind a disabled feature flag and are not a gap. A latent race in `PlatformLegalPage.test.tsx` surfaced with the 130th test file and was fixed at the assertion (wait for the VALUE, not the element); attribution proven by running the suite with the source change and without the new file — 954 passed — and again with the whole change stashed |
| REPORT-LINKS-13 | REPORTS | Two shareable interactive link types per client/project, openable with no sign-in: an executive summary and a full detailed dashboard | `report_shares.form` (migration `2026_08_13_090000_separate_a_shared_reports_form_from_how_it_updates`) + `ReportShare::formOr()` — the FORM is the operator's choice per link and is independent of `mode` (live/snapshot), so one generation serves both the sponsor and the performance manager; `PublicReportController` resolves it per request | `ReportsPage` link builder offers the form; `PublicReport` renders the summary and the detailed dashboard differently, and `SharedCreativeSection` follows the form | `ReportFormTest` (8, incl. «either form can be live or a snapshot» and «an unknown form is refused»), `SharedReportCreativesTest` «summary and detailed links differ and stay independent of live and snapshot», `LiveReportShareTest` (14) — 85 passed together | **VERIFIED** | `6d8d503` + the form migration | Supersedes the `PARTIAL` row in §13, which was written before this landed and describes a gap that no longer exists. Each of the four items that row listed as outstanding is built and covered: the summary-vs-detailed distinction (above), the creator-side scope picker (§14.5, `2026_08_10_090000_give_a_report_a_scope_and_a_reusable_one`), saveable scope templates (the «reusable one» in that same migration) and **Excel export** (`ReportExporter::xlsx()` through PhpSpreadsheet, a real workbook per section, not a renamed CSV). Documentation debt, reconciled — not a unit reopened |
| JOURNEY-PORTAL-001 | SUBSCRIPTIONS | Both full live journeys end in the portal their path sold: Self → `/app`, Agency → `/agency` | — | — | `registration-onboarding.spec.ts` — both walks, live on chromium, 11 passed | **VERIFIED** | `581866a` | The two journeys — signup → path → plan → sandbox checkout → **webhook** provision → sign in → onboarding → dashboard — already existed and already ran in the gate. What they did not prove is the binding the signup question exists to carry: both ended on `toHaveURL(/\/dashboard/)`, which matches `/app/dashboard` and `/agency/dashboard` alike, so the assertion passed for either portal and therefore proved neither. Now named, including across the reload. The payment is deliberately still the real sandbox round trip: a test that wrote a paid row instead would prove the product can be activated by something other than money, which is the one thing this path exists to prevent |

## §20 — Production readiness: the first two units (2026-08-10)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PAY-VERIFY-001 | SUBSCRIPTIONS | A confirmed payment is verified server-side on status **and amount and currency** and reference before anything activates | `ApplySubscriptionPaymentEvent::chargeMatches()` replaces `amountMatches()` — the figure to the fils AND the currency, upper-cased on both sides so `usd` and `USD` are one currency; the mismatch audit records both sides | — | `PaymentActivationSecurityTest` (15, +2: wrong currency, and case is not a currency) | **VERIFIED** | `1847122` | A real hole, not a hardening pass. Subscriptions are sold in USD (PAY-AUDIT-002) while BOTH live adapters default a stated currency to SAR, so a verified webhook reading `49.00 SAR` compared equal to a `49.00 USD` invoice on the only field anybody checked, and the account activated for roughly a quarter of the price. An amount without its currency is a number, not money. Proven fail-first by stashing the service file alone: the new test fails at the `assertSame('failed', …)`. A provider that states no currency keeps exactly the latitude one that states no amount already had, and no more |
| PROD-CONFIG-001 | PLATFORM | A production install fails LOUDLY on configuration that would mislead a customer or lose their money | `ProductionReadiness` — typed `fail`/`warn` findings over app debug/key, `APP_URL`/`FRONTEND_URL` (https, never localhost), the session cookie (secure, and a domain that actually covers the app's host), Postgres, a non-`sync` queue, both payment gateways (chosen-but-unconfigured, secret without webhook secret, a TEST key in production, a test key mixed with a live one) and mail; `php artisan production:check [--json] [--warnings-as-failures]`, non-zero on any failure | — | `ProductionReadinessTest` (16 — each case breaks ONE key and asserts that key is named, so a deleted check cannot pass silently) | **VERIFIED** | `1475d6e` | Nothing checked any of this. An install could boot in production with `APP_DEBUG=true`, a `localhost` callback, a Moyasar test secret and no webhook token and never crash — customers would pay into a gateway whose confirmation could not arrive. A command rather than a `boot()` throw on purpose: aborting the application on a misconfiguration turns one wrong variable into an outage for the customers already inside. `fail` means «this will mislead somebody or lose their money»; `warn` means «honestly unfinished» — an unconfigured mail provider warns, because the product already refuses to record a message as sent without one. **The report carries the SHAPE of a key (test/live/absent) and never its value**, which is asserted. Run against this install: 0 failing, 1 warning (mail) |

## §21 — the sign-in door (2026-08-10)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LOGIN-CARD-001 | AUTH | One card holds both credentials; the marketing shell is untouched | — | `LoginPage` — address, password, «تذكرني», «نسيت كلمة المرور؟», one primary button; then a labelled rule and «أو الدخول بدون كلمة مرور»; `IconField` is a flex ROW, so an icon and a placeholder cannot overlap | `LoginPage.test.tsx` (23), `login-paths`, `auth-forms`, `auth-redesign`, `auth-visual` baselines | **VERIFIED** | `bc1ad27`, `5e4c1ef` | The absolutely-positioned icon over a padded input is what put the lock underneath «أدخل كلمة المرور»: it holds only while the padding and the icon's offset agree about the icon's width, and they stop agreeing when the icon, the font or the direction changes. Two defects the review turned up in the same component: the input's own touch target had shrunk to ~46px once it became a flex child (`self-stretch`), and its 15px text sat under the 16px threshold at which iOS zooms the page on focus. `/register` and `/forgot-password` re-rendered pixel-identical, which is the evidence that the shell was not touched |
| LOGIN-OTP-001 | AUTH | Email OTP with every property a credential needs | `EmailSignInController` + `ContactVerificationService` — single-use (consumed before it can buy a second session), sha256 at rest, 10-minute TTL, `throttle:otp-request`/`otp-check` per IP, a 60s cooldown per DESTINATION, five attempts, previous codes retired on issue, `session()->regenerate()`, audited without the code or its hash, fail-closed | `OtpField` — six boxes, paste, backspace-into-previous, Arabic-Indic digits folded to Latin, `dir="ltr"` | `EmailSignInTest` (19), `OtpField.test.tsx` (10) | **VERIFIED** | `bc1ad27`, `5e4c1ef` | `start` answers identically for an address nobody holds and `verify` fails a code never tied to an account with the same message a wrong code gets, so neither response says whether an address is registered. The cooldown originally keyed on the destination alone, which made the rule «one sign-in per minute per address» — sign out, sign back in, and a control built to stop a stranger mailing you told YOU to wait. A CONSUMED challenge now imposes no wait, which cannot weaken it: consuming one requires holding the code |
| LOGIN-MAIL-001 | AUTH | The code is actually sent, through the product's one mailer | `ContactVerificationService::start(deliver:)` — the plaintext lives for one method, so the caller composes the message through a closure; `EmailSignInController` sends `CredentialMail::SIGN_IN_CODE` via `TransactionalMailer`, and `delivery_status` becomes the ledger's outcome | `DELIVERED` allow-list — `sent`/`queued`/`delivered` are silent, everything else is said out loud | `EmailSignInJourneyTest` (mail: 4), `login-otp-journey` delivery biconditional | **VERIFIED** | `5e4c1ef` | The hollow spot. `SIGN_IN_CODE` existed, rendered in the admin gallery, and was sent by NOTHING — wiring real SMTP would have changed nothing and nobody would have received anything. `sandbox` counts as not-arrived on purpose: the transport accepted it and it reached a log file. A throwing transport is recorded, not propagated — a sign-in form that 500s when a mail host blinks tells the visitor nothing and loses the code already minted for them |
| LOGIN-E2E-001 | AUTH | The destination comes from the SERVER, and the session it opens is real and bounded | `GET /auth/memberships` → `PortalResolver::landingPathFor` — admin flag, memberships, portals | `resolvePostAuthOutcome` reads that answer; nothing routes by email or account type | `EmailSignInJourneyTest` (15), `e2e/login-otp-journey.spec.ts` (10 × 3 browsers) | **VERIFIED** | `5e4c1ef` | admin → `/admin`, advertiser → `/app/dashboard`, agency → `/agency`, two workspaces → `/switch`, none → `/onboarding`. Then the questions a URL cannot answer: `/auth/me` returns the person, a portal endpoint answers, every other portal is refused, and the platform owner does not become a tenant member. Sign-out is proven in the BROWSER, not the backend: Laravel's test client shares one auth container across the requests in a test, so an HTTP logout followed by `GET /auth/me` cannot tell «the server invalidated it» from «the harness kept its own» — the backend test says so instead of reaching for `flushSession()` to manufacture a green tick |
| LOGIN-HELP-001 | AUTH | A way to ask for help that is not a way to sign up | `POST /contact` extended with a closed `topic` (5 needs) and a `source`; with a topic, `subject`/`message` become optional and the server fills them | `HelpRequestModal` — a panel over the page, so asking a question does not cost the `?redirect=` somebody arrived on | `LegalIntakeTest` (+8), `HelpRequestModal.test.tsx` (7) | **VERIFIED** | `bc1ad27`, `5e4c1ef` | Grants no session, creates no account, no workspace, no subscription — asserted. A closed list because the point is to GROUP: «choosing a plan» and «connecting accounts» go to different people, and a free-text subject cannot be routed. The long-form contact page is unchanged: with no topic both fields stay required |

## §22 — money that is actually confirmed (2026-08-10)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PAY-CONFIRM-001 | PAYMENTS | A charge is verified backend-to-backend before it settles, where the webhook cannot prove its own body | `PaymentProvider::confirmsPayloadIntegrity()` + `fetchPayment()`; Moyasar re-reads `GET /v1/payments/{id}` and `ApplySubscriptionPaymentEvent` uses THAT for status, amount and currency. Refusals audited as `event_contradicted`, `reference_mismatch`, `unconfirmed` | — | `PaymentActivationSecurityTest` (20, +5) | **VERIFIED** | `4e9f217` | Moyasar authenticates with a shared secret carried INSIDE the body, so «verified» proved the sender knew a token and nothing about the figures beside it — anyone who came by that token could post a paid event for any amount and the account activated. Stripe and the sandbox HMAC the raw body, so they are not re-asked; a round trip there would add a failure mode to an attested path and would strand every signup on a credential-less machine. An unreachable gateway leaves the payment EXACTLY as it was — not failed, because nothing is known to be wrong with the money and marking it failed starts dunning a customer who has paid. There is no fallback-to-the-body path: a guarantee anyone can opt out of by making one request fail is not a guarantee |
| PAY-TOKEN-001 | PAYMENTS | A saved payment method, and an honest reason when a renewal cannot use one | `subscription_payment_methods` (token `encrypted` + `hidden`, no PAN/CVC column exists), `SubscriptionPaymentMethod`, `RecurringBilling::{methodFor,modeFor,readiness,remember,forget}`, `PaymentProvider::{supportsUnattendedCharge,chargeStoredMethod}`; Moyasar builds the real `POST /v1/payments` token charge | — | `RecurringBillingTest` (23) | **READY_FOR_CREDENTIALS** | `0e0397b` | The renewal opens an invoice nobody visits, so the period lapses and the account goes past due — which reads like dunning working and is actually the absence of unattended billing. Three reasons are now distinguished because they have different owners: `no_gateway` and `provider_unsupported` belong to whoever deploys, `no_saved_method` to the customer. `readiness()` is about the GATEWAY and must not be read as «every subscriber renews». Tokens are scoped to the gateway that minted them — charging a Stripe token through Moyasar is a wrong-vault lookup whose «no such token» reads as a declined card. Expired cards are skipped before a charge is spent proving what the expiry already said. The sweep now takes the money where it can — see `PAY-TOKEN-002` |

## §23 — authentication, and the gate that could not prove it (2026-08-10)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PAY-TOKEN-002 | PAYMENTS | A renewal with a card on file is taken, not asked for — and never both | `SubscriptionCheckout::open()` forks on `RecurringBilling::methodFor()`; `takeFromSavedCard()` debits the token and returns no `checkout_url`; the fork is narrowed to `purpose === 'subscription'` (the sweep's renewals and trial conversions) | — | `RecurringBillingTest` (7 of 23) | **READY_FOR_CREDENTIALS** | `c0d44cd` | The double-charge this exists to prevent is a charge that BOTH debits the saved card and opens a hosted invoice — it looks entirely normal in the interface until the customer's statement arrives with two lines. So it is one route or the other. `submitted` is not `paid`: the payment stays pending and settles only through the verified webhook and the gateway re-read (`PAY-CONFIRM-001`). A refusal is recorded and stops — no hosted page is offered as a second attempt, because from here a decline and a timeout are indistinguishable and offering a page to pay for money that may already be gone is the risk being avoided; the sweep's past-due grace is the customer's path. Registration, plan change and reactivation keep the hosted page: somebody is in front of the screen, and for a reactivation the card on file is usually the one that just failed. Idempotency is unchanged, so two sweeps in one period charge once. **READY_FOR_CREDENTIALS, not VERIFIED** — no Moyasar credentials and no real token exist to prove a live round trip |
| PAY-TOKEN-003 | PAYMENTS | The card a settled payment leaves behind is filed, so the renewal PAY-TOKEN-002 can take actually has something to take | `PaymentProvider::savedPaymentMethodFrom()` on all five adapters (Moyasar reads the token; Stripe, sandbox and null return null, matching the `supportsUnattendedCharge() === false` they already answer); `ApplySubscriptionPaymentEvent::rememberCard()` files it from the same verified, gateway-re-read event that settles the money — after provisioning on a registration, because the tenant did not exist a moment earlier; `GET /subscriptions/current` reports the renewal mode + card label; `DELETE /subscriptions/payment-method` (`subscriptions.manage`, audited); `/admin/settings/integrations/payments` and `production:check` report `RecurringBilling::readiness()` | `RenewalCard` on `/‹portal›/subscriptions`; the recurring block on `/admin/settings` payments | `SavedPaymentMethodTest` (18); `PlatformPaymentSettingsTest` (+2); `SubscriptionsPage.test.tsx` (+5); `PaymentSettingsPage.test.tsx` (+2) | **READY_FOR_CREDENTIALS** | `ba0089d` | `RecurringBilling::remember()` existed, was tested, encrypted its token and picked a default — and had NO CALLER anywhere in the application. So `subscription_payment_methods` was empty in every real deployment, `methodFor()` always answered null, and PAY-TOKEN-002's fork could not fire for anybody: every renewal, for every customer, was a hosted invoice somebody had to remember to visit. The failure is quiet in the worst way — the customer agreed to automatic renewal before they paid (SUB-CONSENT-001), nothing charged them, the period lapsed, `markPastDue` fired on schedule and the account was suspended after grace, which from the outside looks exactly like dunning working. A provider that cannot charge a token must not store one: a card on file the gateway will never debit tells the customer they are set up for automatic payment, which is the one thing they most need to know is untrue. There is deliberately no endpoint that ADDS a card — a token arrives one way only, from a payment the gateway settled — and the audit trail records «visa ···· 4242», never the credential. Storing is bookkeeping and must never roll back a settlement, so a failure there is recorded and swallowed. **READY_FOR_CREDENTIALS, not VERIFIED**: what is proven is the refusal (no token, no card, no charge) and the whole path from a settled event to a debited renewal; what is NOT proven is that Moyasar's live payload puts the token where the adapter reads it |
| IDENTITY-ACCOUNTS-001 | PLATFORM | One canonical sign-in per portal, on the official `campaignshub.io` domain, and never rendered in the product | `DatabaseSeeder` provisions `platform@campaignshub.io` ADDITIVELY (a legacy owner row is left alone — renaming somebody's sign-in address underneath them locks them out of the console that would let them fix it); `DemoPortalLoginsSeeder`, `DemoAccountsSeeder`, `DemoClientPortalSeeder` provide `admin@` / `advertiser@` / `agency@` / `client@` | No change — `/login` was already the single entry and the backend already decided the destination from account state, membership, role, permissions and portal | 92 backend (seeder + portal + platform suites) · the e2e auth setup and 11 specs · `LoginPage.test.tsx` | **VERIFIED** | `383137f` | Proven on a clean install rather than asserted: `migrate:fresh --seed` on the test database provisions all five and leaves **zero** rows on a legacy domain. The four supporting personas (analyst, member, manager, viewer, talent) deliberately keep their original addresses — none is a portal entry point, and renaming them would churn a hundred call sites to no end. The addresses and their password live in seeders, tests and `DEMO_ACCOUNTS.md` and are rendered nowhere in the application: a demo login that advertises itself is a production credential leak waiting for one environment variable to be wrong |
| COMMERCE-TZ-001 | COMMERCE | A merchant's day is not a UTC day — the instant is true, the report window is the client's, and the merchant keeps their own calendar date | `StoreTime` resolves every store timestamp through an explicit chain (payload zone → store → client workspace → UTC, recorded); `StoreInstant`; `SallaConnector::date()` passes the `{date, timezone}` wrapper through intact; `commerce_orders.placed_at_timezone`/`placed_on`/`time_source` and the cart equivalents, with the migration re-anchoring every existing wall clock; `ReportingTimezone::window()` returns UTC instants plus the client's own dates; `StoreFunnelService` and `AttributionTransparency` both use it | The funnel footer, the dashboard store strip and the client link each name the timezone and count orders whose zone was assumed | `CommerceMerchantDayTest` (16, fail-first); `StoreFunnelTab.test.tsx` (+2); `DashboardStore.test.tsx` (+2) | **VERIFIED** | `19b0527` | The zone was thrown away at the connector and `Carbon::parse()` read the merchant's wall clock in the app's timezone — 01:30 in Riyadh stored as 01:30 UTC. It survived review because it was wrong CONSISTENTLY: windows were built in the same frame, so «5 August» matched exactly the rows a merchant would call 5 August. **Fixing the parse alone is worse than fixing nothing** — the corrected instant falls on 4 August in UTC and leaves the merchant's day, so their dashboard and the client's report disagree with nothing to explain it. Two hard-won details: a string that already carries an offset is trusted outright, because applying a zone on top of an absolute instant is how a timezone fix becomes a bigger bug in the other direction; and Eloquent binds a `DateTimeInterface` through `Y-m-d H:i:s` and **drops the offset**, so a Carbon reading 00:00 in Riyadh silently asks Postgres for 00:00 UTC and the first three hours of the client's day leave every result — which is why `window()` returns UTC instants and separate client dates. An order whose zone nobody states is KEPT and marked `assumed_utc`: losing a real sale is the worse error, and the assumption is counted on every surface rather than hidden |
| PAY-ENV-001 | PLATFORM | A LIVE gateway key outside production is refused, not only a test key inside it | `ProductionReadiness::checkPayments()` fails on a non-test secret whenever `APP_ENV` is not `production`; `checkRecurringBilling()` warns when the chosen gateway cannot charge a saved card | — | `ProductionReadinessTest` (+4) | **VERIFIED** | `ba0089d` | The existing check caught a TEST key in production, where the symptom is nobody being charged. This is the other direction and the more expensive one: a laptop, a staging box or a CI run holding `sk_live_…` charges real cards against a database that is thrown away nightly — and it is the easier mistake to make, because copying a working production `.env` is how most staging environments start. The recurring warning is a `warn` and not a `fail` on the same reasoning as mail: an attended renewal is a real way to be paid, and what makes it worth saying is the SHAPE of the failure when a customer misses the invoice |
| AUTH-PHONE-001 | AUTH | A mobile number is a sign-in credential only once somebody proved they hold it | `users.phone_verified_at`; `ProvisionWorkspace` sets it (the mobile gate proved it); `MeController` keeps the number and clears the proof on change, ignoring a re-spelling; `PhoneSignInController` requires it and resolves duplicates by earliest proof; `PhoneConfirmationController` at `/me/phone` — single-use, hashed, expiring, cooldown, `otp-request`/`otp-check`, purpose-scoped, audited, fail-closed | — | `PhoneConfirmationTest` (14) | **VERIFIED** | `9b7c87f` | `PATCH /me/profile` wrote `users.phone` from a payload with a format rule and nothing else, and the phone sign-in signs in whoever answers a code sent to that column — so an unproved number was a way into an account. Not only adversarial: somebody correcting a typo could put a stranger's number on their account, and that stranger, holding their own phone and doing nothing wrong, could sign in as them. Existing numbers are backfilled as verified from `created_at` — every one arrived through a gated registration, and marking them unverified would lock real customers out to close a hole that only opens afterwards |
| AUTH-PHONE-002 | AUTH | The screen that puts the proof back, and refuses to offer a channel that cannot work | `GET/POST/DELETE /me/phone` (unchanged — AUTH-PHONE-001) | `PhoneCredentialCard` on the PERSONAL Account security page (`/‹portal›/account/security`), not workspace settings; `PhoneField` for the number, `OtpField` for the code (now takes a `testId` prefix so sign-in and this are separately addressable) | `PhoneCredential.test.tsx` (14), `account-settings` E2E | **VERIFIED** | `d3a54b9` | AUTH-PHONE-001 closed the hole and left nowhere to put the proof back: an owner could lose their code sign-in and not restore it. The panel is personal because `/me/phone` is self-only — the number decides how THIS person signs in and how their account is recovered. WhatsApp is drawn from `channels.whatsapp`, never from intent: unconfigured it is labelled «awaiting credentials» and is not selectable, because a «send by WhatsApp» control over an unwired channel is a button that cannot work. With NEITHER channel configured the fact is stated before the button is pressed, and both options stay selectable — disabling every one would leave a radio group with no answer and would put the development and E2E path behind credentials that by definition are absent. `awaiting_provider_credentials` is reported as «nothing was sent», never as «check your phone». The E2E restores the shared demo owner through the profile field, which is the only way to CLEAR a number: the panel deliberately has no «forget it entirely», because a number is a contact detail as well as a credential |
| AUTH-SOCIAL-001 | AUTH | No third party stands between somebody and their own account | OAuth endpoints, `oauth_identities` and migrations all UNTOUCHED | `SocialSignIn.tsx` removed — dead since LOGIN-CARD-001, imported by nothing | `portal-distinctness` asserts the card offers no third-party button | **VERIFIED** | `9b7c87f` | Non-destructive by design: an account created through Google keeps working and its holder signs in with the email code, which needs no password. Invitations, recovery, signup and portal routing never touched OAuth |
| GATE-VITE-001 | TOOLING | The gate's two dev servers each get their own Vite dependency cache | — | `vite.config.ts` reads `VITE_CACHE_DIR`; `playwright.config.ts` gives the print server `node_modules/.vite-print` | The gate itself: chromium 8.1m where it had been 43.6m, `REAL_GATE_EXIT=0` | **VERIFIED** | `79158a2` | Two Vite processes cannot share `node_modules/.vite`: each runs its own optimizer, and either one re-optimizing rewrites the directory and bumps the hash every client URL carries, so the other server's in-flight requests ask for a `?v=` that no longer exists — «Load failed» in the browser, 502 through the proxy, and a `page.goto` that never fires `load`. Laravel was ruled out by a failing test that never calls it: `login-paths.spec.ts:61` fills an invalid number, expects client-side validation, and took 17.3 minutes. The login specs navigate on nearly every test, which is the burst that trips the race |

## §24 — sign-out that holds, and a demo world that builds in one command (2026-08-11)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ACCESS-EXIT-003 | AUTH | A session that was signed out cannot be brought back by a request that started before the sign-out | `SessionRevocations` — a SHA-256 of the session id in the cache, one session lifetime + 5 min, never the id itself in the key or the value; `RejectRevokedSessions` appended to the API group so it runs inside Sanctum's stateful pipeline after `StartSession`, refusing 401 and destroying the resurrected payload; recorded at all three places an authenticated id is retired: `AuthController::logout()`, `EnsureAccountActive` (suspended account) and `MeController` (`logoutOtherSessions`, `updatePassword` with sign-out-other-devices), each BEFORE `invalidate()`/`regenerate()` mints a new id | `ACCESS-EXIT-002` barrier unchanged and kept; the two comments that named the overturned cookie-re-issue hypothesis corrected | `StaleSessionResurrectionTest` (8, fail-first); `login-otp-journey` sign-out × 3 browsers | **VERIFIED** | `b637203` | `logout` was never defective — it destroys the session, measured against Redis (`EXISTS` 1 → 0, `TTL` -2). The session was being PUT BACK: Laravel writes the session when a request finishes, under the id it was loaded with, so a dashboard request that left the browser before the click restores the payload after the delete. Proven byte for byte with no sleeps, retries or scans — restore the captured 287 bytes under the deleted key and the same cookie jar goes from 401 to 200. Six conclusions were overturned by measurement before this one survived (the gate leg · the browser · `page.request` · the client barrier alone · two harness attempts · a stale dev server), which is why the marker had to be measured rather than reasoned. It lives OUTSIDE the session because resurrection replaces the payload wholesale and would replace any flag inside it. Scoped to one id: no global sign-out, no request serialisation, no lock on dashboard traffic — nothing here knows which user a session belonged to, so a second device is untouched by construction, and that is asserted rather than assumed. The client barrier stays: it keeps most of these requests from being sent, and it cannot recall one already in flight |
| DEMO-RESEED-002 | DEMO | One `migrate:fresh --seed` builds the same demo world as running the seeders twice | `DatabaseSeeder` calls `DemoCreativesSeeder` after `DemoClientPortalSeeder` and `DemoCreativeAnalysisSeeder`, whose daily metrics it selects its campaigns on; `widen_the_creative_preview_columns::down()` no longer attempts to narrow a column that holds longer values | — | `DemoCreativesReseedTest` (+1, order); the sequence itself: `migrate:fresh --seed` → `rollback` (all) → `migrate` → `db:seed` → `db:seed`, every step exit 0 | **VERIFIED** | `584bbc3` | The creatives seeder ran before the seeder that writes the client campaigns' metrics, so a FIRST seed skipped three campaigns: 78 creatives and 2,583 metric rows where two consecutive seeds give 90 and 2,943. Nothing duplicated, nothing orphaned — which is exactly why it lasted, since every demo database anyone had looked at had been seeded twice and the second run quietly filled the gap. Found by rolling every migration back and up on a seeded database, which also found a `down()` that could not run at all: narrowing `thumbnail_url` back to 255 raises `SQLSTATE[22001]` against demo thumbnails that are data URIs. A widening is not reversible — truncating or nulling the values discards a customer's data to satisfy a schema change nobody asked for — so it now reverses what it can, nothing, and says so, rather than aborting a rollback half way through a batch |

## §25 — the deletion URL every ad platform asks for (2026-08-12)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LEGAL-DELETE-001 | LEGAL | `/data-deletion` public, bilingual, sessionless — and a real flow with identity verification, a reference, a status lookup, an audit trail and a signature-verified platform callback | `DeletionVerification` (SHA-256 of the code, 60-minute TTL, 5 attempts, `hash_equals`, retired on use); `DataDeletionController` — `submit`/`verify`/`status` + `POST /webhooks/data-deletion/{provider}`; migration `a_deletion_request_must_prove_who_sent_it`; `ConnectionRevocations::revokeForTenant()`; the fail-closed gate in `LegalInboxController`; compliance URLs on `/admin` integration readiness | `/data-deletion` page — request, verify, look up; added to `SESSIONLESS_PREFIXES` so it never probes `/auth/me` | `DataDeletionFlowTest` (12), `DataDeletionPage.test.tsx` (7), `sessionlessSurface` (+1), `LegalIntakeTest` (22, fixtures now carry the proof) | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | `/privacy` and `/terms` already existed (`LEGAL-001`) and were not rebuilt. What was missing is the third URL and the thing behind it: `POST /data-requests` accepted a deletion naming ANY address from anyone and opened it `pending`, ready for an operator to work through — the `verifying` status was in the enum and nothing set it. So «somebody typed this address into a form» and «somebody who can read that inbox asked for this» were one event, and only the second justifies destroying anything. The code is stored hashed, because a table of live codes beside the requests they unlock is a table that deletes accounts for whoever reads it. Every refusal — unknown reference, wrong code, expired — is the SAME sentence, deliberately: an endpoint that distinguishes them is a way to ask which addresses have requested deletion. Completing a verified deletion revokes the tenant's provider connections through the same path a person's own Disconnect uses, because deleting our copy while a live token still points at their ad account is half a deletion. The Meta callback verifies the `signed_request` HMAC and **refuses 503 with no app secret** rather than answering with a confirmation it cannot stand behind; a signed one opens exactly one request, idempotently. **Nothing here is `LIVE_VERIFIED`** — no platform has been configured to call it |

## §26 — the production install had no data (2026-08-13)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PROD-PROVISION-001 | PLATFORM | A production deploy provisions the reference data the product is built out of, without ever creating demo data | `php artisan platform:provision` — the five reference seeders (permissions, request catalogue, subscription plans, paid-media taxonomy, metric definitions), literal list so a new demo seeder cannot join by being well named, `--pretend`, idempotent, run unguarded exactly as `db:seed` runs them; `scripts/deploy-production.sh` calls it after `migrate --force` | — | `ProvisionPlatformTest` (5, fail-first) | **VERIFIED** | `2b65beb` | Proven on the live site rather than reasoned about: `/api/v1/plans` answered `{"plans": []}` and the paid-media catalogue `{"categories": [], "services": []}`, so the homepage services section was empty, `/services` said «لا توجد خدمة مطابقة» and sign-up died at plan selection with «تعذّر». Not one of those screens was defective — each rendered an empty catalogue faithfully, which is exactly why the frontend was the wrong place to look. The deploy ran `migrate --force` alone because the checklist says never to seed production; correct about demo data, quietly wrong about reference data, which `DatabaseSeeder` runs in every environment. After the merge the auto-deploy provisioned the live site: **3 plans, 10 categories, 94 services**, and no tenant or user created — asserted by test, not assumed |

## §27 — the mobile pass, and two throttles keyed by the wrong thing (2026-08-14)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| MOBILE-001 | UIQA | A control is a thumb target on a phone and keeps its desktop density above `sm` | — | `Button` sizes are mobile-first (`min-h-11 sm:min-h-0 sm:h-9/10/11`); the duplicated 36×36 icon-button pattern corrected in 13 files — every portal shell, the auth shell, both marketing headers, the notification centre | `responsive-audit.spec.ts`; measured targets on `/`, `/privacy`, `/terms`, `/data-deletion` | **VERIFIED** | `c803d41` | Every size in the system was a desktop size. Desktop is pixel-identical after the change — only the phone moves, which is what "mobile-first" has to mean to mean anything |
| MOBILE-002 | UIQA | One public header, owned once, so a primitive change cannot break a page that never heard about it | — | `PublicHeader` — brand, wordmark responsive rule, language + theme toggles, page actions, and a menu that collapses the section nav and the secondary actions below `lg`; `PublicHomePage`, `PublicServicesPage` and `PublicPageShell` all pass content into it | `responsive-audit` 42/42 on 7 public routes + 4 portals at 375/390/430/768; `PublicHomePage.test.tsx` 24 | **VERIFIED** | `f5e1fa0` | Three pages hand-built the same row, so one row had three width budgets. MKT-UGC-001 had already fixed this defect in ONE of them (wordmark down below 480px) where the other two could not inherit it — so the moment MOBILE-001 widened the shared controls by 8px each, `/privacy`, `/terms` and `/data-deletion` overflowed by 8px at 375. **The defect was the duplication, not the eight pixels**: widening a control is a normal thing to do to a design system, and a row that breaks when one does is a row nobody owns. Caught by the audit spec added in the same series, not by looking. The phone also gains seven destinations it did not have — the section nav and «Log in» / «Track my requests» / «Request a service» were `hidden lg/md/sm:block` with nothing behind them, which is information removed to make a row fit |
| MOBILE-003 | UIQA | A 44px hit area, without a 44px control | — | `TOUCH_TARGET` expands the hit area with a transparent pseudo-element (`-inset-3`, dropped at `sm`); `TOUCH_CONTROL` gives real controls the mobile height. Applied at `FilterBar`, `MetricStrip`, `forms/internals`, `PaidServicesPanel` | `responsive-audit` re-run after the change: 42/42, no overflow reintroduced; the emitted CSS verified in the browser (`-12px` each side) | **VERIFIED** | `f5e1fa0` | The remaining undersized targets were nearly all a small «×» inside a chip or a clear inside a select. Growing them would wrap the filter row and bring the overflow back, so the hit area grows and the drawn pixels do not. Inline cross-reference links inside content are deliberately NOT forced to 44px — WCAG 2.5.8 exempts a link in a sentence, and a data table where every reference is a 44px block is a worse page |
| SIGNUP-THROTTLE-001 | AUTH | «Send me the code again» is limited per APPLICANT, not per address | `registration-resend` limiter — per application (production 3/min) plus an address ceiling (12/min), environment-aware with `_local` keys so the limiter stays reachable by a test; the literal `throttle:6,1` removed from `/auth/registration/{registration}/resend` | — | `RegistrationResendThrottleTest` (3, fail-first) | **VERIFIED** | `f5e1fa0` | A literal throttle is keyed by the authenticated user or, for a guest, the IP — and every caller of this endpoint is a guest by definition, because an applicant has no account yet. Six resends a minute SHARED by everyone behind one address: an office, a campus, a hotel, any carrier doing CGNAT. Presented as a broken sign-up page — the E2E walk failed at «no dev code was issued», and the page snapshot showed the form intact with «Too many requests» where the code should have been. Neither raising the number nor retrying the test would have been a fix. Same defect and same shape as APP-100 on `/register`, whose comment calls that «the last public endpoint still throttled inline»; it was not, and this one is |
| LEGAL-THROTTLE-001 | LEGAL | Asking to be deleted is not rationed by the address you ask from | `data-subject-request` limiter — per subject (production 3/min) plus an address ceiling (12/min); the literal `throttle:5,1` removed from `/data-deletion` and `/data-requests` | — | `DataSubjectRequestThrottleTest` (4, fail-first) | **VERIFIED** | `f5e1fa0` | The same defect as SIGNUP-THROTTLE-001 on the flow it matters most on. `/data-deletion` is the URL handed to Meta, TikTok, Snapchat and Google as this platform's deletion contact — it is opened by a REVIEWER, and it is the one right that has to work when the customer has already lost access to everything else. An agency filing for several clients, a household, or two reviewers at one desk met «عدد كبير من الطلبات» having asked once. Found the same way: `legal-public-urls` failed in a full run and passed alone, and the snapshot showed the form intact with the throttle notice above it, which is what ruled out the page. The fail-first states the KEYING without naming a number — ask as one person until refused, then assert the next person still gets through — so it stays true whatever the allowance is set to |

## §28 — the portal frame, and an app shell on the phone (2026-08-16)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SHELL-001 | UIQA | A portal fills its window, and the footer sinks to the bottom of a short page instead of stranding dead space beneath itself | — | `PortalFrame` — the frame owned ONCE: `min-h-[100dvh]`, the sticky rail, the drawer, the header slot, and a `<main>` that is a flex COLUMN whose content box takes the slack so the footer is genuinely last. `AppShell`, `AgencyShell`, `AdminShell`, `InfluencerShell` all render through it; `CreatorShell` and the client `PortalShell` take the same fix inline (no rail) | `responsive-audit.spec.ts` — 24 new: 4 portals × 1280/1366/1440/1536/1920/2560, asserting the gap below the footer and zero horizontal overflow | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | `<main>` was `flex-1` with block-flow children, so on a page shorter than the viewport it stretched, the footer sat under the short content, and the rest was empty — measured at **hundreds of pixels**, growing with the window, which is why it looked worst on a large monitor. Four shells carried a byte-identical copy of that markup: one defect in four places, and the same duplication that produced MOBILE-002 a day earlier. What is asserted is the property, not a screenshot: the distance from the footer's bottom edge to the bottom of the document, which is the footer's own padding on a correct shell at any content length. `100vh` → `100dvh` in the same pass, because `100vh` is the viewport with the URL bar hidden and every portal was therefore ~60px too tall on a phone before anybody scrolled. **The fix's own first attempt reintroduced the defect**: the tab-bar clearance was an inline style, which cannot carry a media query, so 64px of empty page appeared under the footer on every DESKTOP window — caught by the guard written for the original, which is what the guard is for |
| MOBILE-APP-001 | UIQA | A phone gets an app shell — bottom navigation, not the desktop rail made small — with nothing removed | — | `MobileTabBar` (4 tabs + a More bottom sheet, `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)`, 44px targets, Escape/backdrop close, active pill); `mobileTabs.ts` DERIVES the sheet from the portal's own rail; wired into all four portals, and the client portal's horizontal strip becomes `hidden md:block` | `mobileTabs.test.ts` (7) — bar ∪ sheet == rail exactly, no duplicates, entitlement filter reaches the sheet; `responsive-audit.spec.ts` — 8 new: rail absent, bar present, bar covers nothing after scrolling to the bottom, More opens and holds real links | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Every portal shipped ONE navigation — a desktop rail, hidden below `md`, reachable on a phone only through a hamburger that opened the same rail. Every destination cost tap-open-scan-tap and nothing said where you were. The tab bar is four destinations always visible plus everything else one tap away. **The sheet is derived, not restated**: a hand-written "everything else" list is correct the day it is written and silently wrong the first time somebody adds a rail section — it exists on desktop, not on a phone, and no test fails because nothing claimed the two were the same set. `mobileTabs.test.ts` makes that claim checkable. Bars: `/app` الرئيسية·الحملات·التحليلات·المشاريع · `/agency` الرئيسية·العملاء·الحملات·الطلبات · `/admin` نظرة عامة·طلبات التسجيل·المستأجرون·الاشتراكات · `/portal` الرئيسية·الطلبات·الحملات·التقارير. `/influencers` has exactly four sections, so the bar IS its navigation and it has no sheet. **The first cut removed a feature on mobile and the suite caught it**: dropping the hamburger below `sm` is right for an app shell, but the PROJECT SWITCHER lived only in the rail and the drawer, so an advertiser on a phone could no longer change project — `campaigns-responsive` asserts «the project switcher stays reachable at every width» and failed at 320 and 375. The switcher (and the agency's client → project scope) now sits at the top of the More sheet, above the sections, because choosing the project comes before choosing the section. The test's CLAIM is unchanged; only the door moved |

## §29 — the phone's first screen (2026-08-16)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| MOBILE-HERO-001 | A HOME | On a phone, the headline and all three doors — إنشاء حساب · تسجيل الدخول · متابعة طلباتي — are in the first screen without scrolling | — | `HeroSection` re-ordered for one column: promise text → account actions → benefits → «كيف تريد البدء؟» → the dashboard preview. The actions move out of the chooser card into their own block (`hero-actions`), rejoined to the card seam-to-seam from `lg` up so the desktop layout is unchanged. Headline 21px on a phone / 32px from `sm`; hero padding and inter-element spacing tightened on mobile only | `mobile-first-screen.spec.ts` (30, fail-first) — 375×667 · 390×844 · 430×932 × ar/en × light/dark, asserting real geometry at `scrollY = 0`; plus a companion test that the benefits, all three chooser paths and the journey strip are still present | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The single column stacked eyebrow → headline → description → support → four benefits → chooser title → subtitle → three path cards → includes list, and ONLY THEN the three buttons: about 900px of page before the first decision, so on a 667px phone every door was below the fold and two of them were otherwise only in the hamburger. A marketing page that does not ask for the thing it exists to ask for. Fixed by `order` alone — **nothing removed, nothing duplicated**, and `contents`/`lg:block` on the promise wrapper lets the benefits separate from the headline on a phone and rejoin it on desktop. The guard measures the BOX at `scrollY = 0` rather than DOM presence, because presence was never the problem; it also checks paint (`display`/`visibility`/`opacity`) so an element that is technically in the viewport but invisible cannot pass. Fail-first confirmed against `4bd4116`: fails in all four locale/theme combinations at 375. `homepage.spec.ts` scoped the returning-user links to «the last div containing the chooser heading» — a positional heuristic, repinned to `hero-actions`; the route assertions are unchanged. Both homepage visual baselines regenerated deliberately |
| CATALOGUE-THROTTLE-001 | PLATFORM | The public price list and service catalogue are limited per minute in production, and do not measure the acceptance suite off it | `public-catalogue` limiter — **production unchanged at 60/min/IP** (`subscriptions.public_catalogue_throttle`), 1200 off it; the literal `throttle:60,1` removed from `GET /plans`, `GET /plans/{code}/quote` and `/public/catalog/paid-media-services` | — | 89 backend (`PublicPlan`/`Subscription`/`ProductionReadiness`); the gate itself | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Surfaced by MOBILE-HERO-001, and in a different file: thirty new homepage loads pushed the shared per-IP window over 60, and the spec that runs next alphabetically — `platform-control` — read the 429 as `body.data.plans` on **null** and failed «Cannot read properties of null». Nothing was wrong with the plan catalogue; the request had been refused. This is the third instance of the same shape in this repository (SIGNUP-THROTTLE-001, LEGAL-THROTTLE-001): a literal per-IP throttle on a public, guest-only endpoint, driven by a suite that runs from one address. The fix is the environment split `auth-login`, `registration`, `requests-intake` and `otp-check` already carry — **no production control is weakened**, and the two endpoints are read-only reads that send nothing and charge nothing |

## §31 — Phase 2 integrations hardening: TikTok (2026-08-16)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OAUTH-WS-001 | U PERM | The client workspace an OAuth flow is started for must belong to the caller's tenant, and still exist | `AdPlatformOAuthController::start` and `StoreOAuthController::start` validate `client_workspace_id` with `Rule::exists('client_workspaces','id')->where('tenant_id', …)->whereNull('deleted_at')` — the rule `TaskController`, `ProjectController` and `AICredentialController` already use for this field | — | `OAuthClientWorkspaceOwnershipTest` (8, fail-first): cross-tenant, non-existent and soft-deleted all refused for ads AND commerce; the legitimate workspace carried through to the connection and every discovered account; refused before a state is minted; omitting it is still a tenant-level connection | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Both entry points validated `['sometimes','nullable','uuid']` — the SHAPE of a string and nothing else. The value went into the single-use state and, on the callback, onto the `ProviderConnection` and every `ExternalAccount`. An operator of tenant A holding `integrations.connect` in their OWN tenant could post tenant B's client-workspace id and file a real, live platform credential — and everything syncing through it — under another company's client. `ClientWorkspace` is `BelongsToTenant`, but a global scope defends QUERIES and this code ran none: it moved a bare string from a request body to a column. `deleted_at` is in the rule because the table is soft-deleted and a live credential filed against a removed client shows on no surface. Deliberately NOT a per-user membership rule — no controller here has one, an agency owner connects for any of their own clients, and inventing that inside a security fix would break a legitimate flow wearing the same commit. Fail-first: the four refusal cases returned **200** with an authorisation URL |
| TIKTOK-AUTH-001 | J INTEGRATION-TIKTOK | The TikTok callback exchanges the code TikTok says is exchangeable | `PlatformOAuth::callbackCodeParameter()` — `auth_code` for TikTok, `code` for everyone else; the callback asks it instead of hardcoding `code`. The documented body is exactly `{app_id, secret, auth_code}`, so the undocumented `grant_type` is removed from the exchange and from `ProviderProbe` | — | `TikTokAuthorizationCodeTest` (7, fail-first) — driven with the pair of parameters TikTok's own documented redirect carries | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Read from TikTok's current *Authorization* and *Authentication* pages, opened in a browser because the portal renders nothing to a fetcher. TikTok's redirect carries `code` AND `auth_code` with **different values**, and the authorization page states of its own example that the one to extract is the `auth_code` value. We read `code` and posted it as `auth_code`: **every TikTok connection failed at the first exchange**, always, for every advertiser. It survived because no fixture ever sent both parameters — one that sends a single `code` cannot tell them apart, and that is what the flow test sent. **No fallback to `code`**: it would post the value now known to be wrong and report TikTok's refusal to the customer as a platform outage. CONFIRMED unchanged by the same audit: the long-term token does not expire and has no refresh grant; scopes belong to the app and come back on the token response; `/oauth2/advertiser/get/` is the documented per-customer advertiser list, which the connector already calls with the customer's own token; no PKCE; polling not webhooks; the sandbox is a whitelisted advertiser account, not a second host — so ENV-FAKE-001's verdict stands |
| TIKTOK-SCOPE-001 | J INTEGRATION-TIKTOK | A granted scope that arrives as a list is kept, not cast to a string | `PlatformOAuth::scopeFrom()` joins an array scope; absent or empty still yields null so a refresh keeps the previous scope | — | covered by `TikTokAuthorizationCodeTest` — the exchange returns `"scope": [4]`, exactly as TikTok's example does | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | `(string) $body['scope']` is right for the five OAuth providers and wrong for TikTok, which documents `scope` as `number[]`. Casting an array raises «Array to string conversion» in PHP 8 **inside the token exchange**, so the callback ended in `outcome=failed` with that message shown to the customer. Found by the fail-first above: the first run redirected to `reason=Array+to+string+conversion`, which is a second defect the first one was hiding — TikTok never got far enough to reach it. Joined rather than dropped because TikTok grants what the ADVERTISER approved, which need not be what the app asked for |

## §32 — Phase 2 integrations hardening: Meta (2026-08-16)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| META-ATTRIB-001 | J INTEGRATION-META | Every stored metric records the attribution window it was actually measured over | `MetaConnector` sends `action_attribution_windows` as JSON `["7d_click","1d_view"]` and stamps `attribution_window` on every row it maps; `InsightRowNormaliser::window()` reads that key off the row — as it already reads `currency` — and passes it to `NormalizedMetric` instead of letting the constructor default stand. A row that names none still gets `default` | — (none needed: `AnalyticsPage` already warns on more than one window; the backend had never given it a second value) | `MetaAttributionWindowTest` (7, fail-first) — the request names the window; it is JSON, not `[0]=` brackets; every row carries it; it reaches storage; an undeclared row is still `default`; two providers on different windows are **two groups, not one**; and no Meta URL is on an expired version | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | `grep -rn "attributionWindow:" app/` returned **zero** results — nothing had ever passed one, so every row of every provider stored the literal `default`. `MetricsController::normalization` groups on that column, the panel warns when it gets more than one value, and `AttributionTransparency::platformRows()` groups per provider on it: a grouping over a column with one value returns one bucket, always. The warning could not fire, and the single row displayed **read as a clean bill of health** — a panel built to catch a comparability defect reporting a uniformity it had never verified. Meta's insights reference makes it concrete: `action_attribution_windows` defaults to `default`, and «يشير الخيار default إلى `["7d_click","1d_view"]`» — a SPECIFIC window, not a neutral one, and different from the five other providers' own unstated defaults. The value asked for is deliberately Meta's own default: a shorter or longer one would be a reporting policy nobody chose, applied retroactively to every client's history. JSON rather than a PHP array because Graph parses `list<enum>` as JSON and would silently ignore `action_attribution_windows[0]=…` — ignored being indistinguishable from never sent |
| META-VERSION-001 | J INTEGRATION-META | Meta is called on a Marketing API version that has not expired | `authorize_url`, `token_url` and `api_base` moved from **v21.0** to **v25.0** | — | `MetaAttributionWindowTest::test_no_meta_url_is_pinned_to_an_expired_marketing_api_version` — asserts the major as a NUMBER with a floor of 25, and that the three URLs agree | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The Marketing API keeps its own version table, separate from the Graph API's, and on it **v21.0 expired on 9 September 2025** — eleven months before this fix. Neighbours, for runway: v22.0 → 19 Feb 2026, v23.0 → 9 Jun 2026, v24.0 → 6 Oct 2026, v25.0 → no expiration set. It never surfaced as a failure and could not have: Meta's platform versioning guide states that calls to a version that is no longer usable **default to the next-oldest usable version** — no error, no header, no log line, figures still arriving from a version nobody chose. Asserted as a number rather than the literal `v25.0` so a later upgrade passes and only a downgrade, or standing still until v25.0 itself retires, fails |

## §33 — Phase 2 integrations hardening: Google Ads (2026-08-16)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GADS-VERSION-001 | J INTEGRATION-GOOGLE | Google Ads is called on an API version that has not been sunset | `api_base` **v18 → v25** | — | `GoogleAdsAccountHierarchyTest::test_google_is_not_pinned_to_a_sunset_api_version` — the major as a NUMBER with a floor of 25 | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Google's released-versions table runs v21 (6 Aug 2025 → Aug 2026), v22 (15 Oct 2025 → Oct 2026), v23 (28 Jan 2026 → Feb 2027), v24 (22 Apr 2026 → May 2027), v25 (22 Jul 2026 → Aug 2027). **v18 is on none of them.** Unlike Meta, which silently falls back to the next-oldest usable version (META-VERSION-001), Google's own definition is that a sunset version can no longer be used and requests to it FAIL on or after the sunset date — so **every Google Ads call this platform made was refused, for every customer, always**. Floor asserted as a number so an upgrade passes and only standing still fails |
