| GADS-HIERARCHY-001 | J INTEGRATION-GOOGLE | Discovery returns the customer's real ad accounts, not the manager accounts they are reached through | `fetchAdAccounts` treats accessible customers as ENTRY POINTS and queries `customer_client` under each — every direct and indirect client, plus the entry point at `level = 0` — skipping any row where `customer_client.manager` is true; `hidden = FALSE` filtered in the query; accounts keyed by id so one reachable through two entry points is discovered once | — | `GoogleAdsAccountHierarchyTest` (9 new, fail-first — 8 of 9 failed) driven with Google's own worked example: M1 + C3 accessible, C1 and C2 under M1 | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | `listAccessibleCustomers` returns **the accounts the authenticated user can act on directly** — which includes MANAGERS and excludes the customers beneath them. Google's own example: a user with rights on manager M1 and account C3 can reach M1, C1, C2, C3, but the call returns only M1 and C3. The connector treated every resource name as an ad account, so for any agency working through an MCC we recorded the MANAGER as an ad account and **never discovered a single account holding campaigns or spend**. A manager holds no campaigns, so that one "account" synced cleanly and reported nothing forever — which reads as a quiet month, not a broken integration. Using `customer_client` also fixes three lesser things in passing: name, currency and timezone come from the platform instead of `null`; a client Google reports as `CANCELED` is no longer badged `active`; and the extra per-customer `SELECT customer.descriptive_name` round trip is gone |
| GADS-MCC-001 | J INTEGRATION-GOOGLE | The manager account a call is made through is the customer's, discovered from their own hierarchy | `login-customer-id` now comes from `ApiAdvertisingConnector::loginCustomerId()` (null for every other provider) rather than from credentials: the entry point while walking a hierarchy, otherwise the `parent_external_id` recorded for that account at discovery, and **no header at all** for a directly-held account. Removed from `config/ad_platforms.php`, from the `/admin` field list in `ProviderCatalogue`, and from both credential checklists | — (the field disappears from the admin form with the catalogue entry) | `GoogleAdsAccountHierarchyTest` — a managed customer is queried through its own manager; a directly-held one sends no header; a system value set in config is ignored; the developer token is still sent; and the admin form's field list is exactly `client_id, client_secret, developer_token` | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Google documents `login-customer-id` as the customer id of the manager account through which the caller reaches THAT client account — it belongs to each customer's hierarchy, not to this platform. One value in one system row was sent on every call for every tenant, and was wrong for all but at most one of them. It was also written into `parent_external_id` on every discovered account, making one operator's MCC id the recorded parent of every client's accounts. **SNAP-ORG-001 again: same shape, same layer, same cause.** The `/admin` field is removed as well as the header, because a field on that form is an instruction — leaving it after the value stopped being read would invite an operator to paste one customer's manager id into a platform-wide setting and believe they had configured something. `GOOGLE_ADS_LOGIN_CUSTOMER_ID` is deliberately read nowhere: an env var that is silently ignored is worse than one that is absent. CONFIRMED unchanged: the developer token IS ours and stays required; `listAccessibleCustomers` ignores any `login-customer-id` supplied, so the old value never affected discovery either way |

## §34 — Phase 2 integrations hardening: X (2026-08-16)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| X-PKCE-001 | J INTEGRATION-X | A provider that requires PKCE actually gets a code challenge and a matching verifier | `PlatformOAuth::codeVerifier()` (96 chars, gated on the catalogue's `usesPkce`) and `codeChallenge()` (S256, base64url); `authorizationUrl()` adds `code_challenge` + `code_challenge_method` only when a verifier exists; `exchangeCode()` presents `code_verifier` and **refuses** when a PKCE provider has none. Both `AdPlatformOAuthController` and `StoreOAuthController` mint it at `start`, carry it in `AuthorizationState`'s `extra`, and read it back from the RECORD — never the query string | — | `XPkceFlowTest` (7 new, fail-first) — the challenge exists and names S256; it is the recomputed SHA-256 of the stored verifier; the verifier is 43–128 unreserved chars; two flows do not share one; the exchange presents the matching verifier; a flow whose verifier was lost is refused rather than attempted; and the set of providers sending a challenge equals the set declaring `usesPkce`, walked from the catalogue | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The catalogue declared PKCE mandatory in **three** places — the class header, `usesPkce: true`, and a `tokenNote` describing the round trip — and `grep -rn "code_challenge\|code_verifier" app/Domains/Integrations/` returned **only those three comments**. Not one line of code. The only PKCE in the repository was `Identity/Services/OAuthFlow`, the unrelated staff sign-in flow, which is why a reader could find PKCE and assume it applied. X's own documentation: «We only provide authorization code with PKCE and refresh token as the supported grant types» — it is the ONLY authorization-code flow X offers, so **every X connection would have been refused at the authorise step**, always, while the console called the integration ready. Documentation describing a mechanism nobody wrote is worse than none: it is what a reviewer checks instead of the code. The verifier rides in the authorisation state rather than the session because the callback is a PUBLIC route with no session left — inheriting single-use, short-lived and tenant-bound for free. Fails CLOSED: exchanging without a verifier would send X a request it must reject and show the customer X's refusal as though the platform were broken. `usesPkce` now DRIVES behaviour instead of merely describing it, so declaration and implementation cannot drift again. CONFIRMED unchanged by the same audit: `ads-api.x.com/12` is current (X's Versions table — 12.0 introduced 27 Oct 2022, deprecation and end-of-life both **TBD**), `offline.access` is already requested and is what makes a refresh token exist, the token call already uses Basic auth as X requires for confidential clients, and `GET accounts` is the documented per-customer discovery |

## §35 — Phase 2 integrations hardening: LinkedIn (2026-08-16)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LINKEDIN-PAGE-001 | J INTEGRATION-LINKEDIN | Every LinkedIn collection is read to its end, not to LinkedIn's default page | `LinkedInConnector::readAll()` walks `start`/`count` with a page of 100 and a `MAX_PAGES` ceiling, terminating on LinkedIn's documented end-of-dataset rule (fewer elements returned than the count requested). All four calls now use it: `adAccounts`, `adAccounts/{id}/adCampaigns`, `adAccounts/{id}/creatives`, `adAnalytics` | — | `LinkedInPagingAndVersionTest` (8 new, fail-first — 5 failed) — accounts, campaigns and analytics each read past the first page; an explicit `count` above 10 and at most 1000 with `start=0`; a short page ends the walk in ONE request; the walk is bounded | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | **Not one of the four calls passed `count` or `start`, or looked at `paging`** — each read `elements` once and returned. LinkedIn's pagination page gives the default `count` as **10**. So an agency saw at most ten ad accounts, and within each at most ten campaigns, ten creatives and ten rows of analytics. Nothing errored and nothing was logged: every total on every surface was short by whatever the eleventh campaign onward did, and a truncated account does not look broken, it looks smaller. Worst on analytics, where one row is one campaign on one day — a handful of campaigns over a month exceeds ten rows immediately, so the first page alone was reported as the whole of the spend. Same defect Meta had and fixed via `readAll`, except Meta's page size was 500 (needs a large account to bite) and LinkedIn's is 10 (bites at a small one). Terminates on the documented short-page rule rather than on an empty page, which would spend an extra round trip on every sync of every account on an API that throttles per application |
| LINKEDIN-VERSION-001 | J INTEGRATION-LINKEDIN | LinkedIn is called on a version that has not been sunset | `LINKEDIN_ADS_VERSION` default **202411 → 202607** | — | `LinkedInPagingAndVersionTest` — the version as a NUMBER with a floor of 202607, plus a test that the configured value is what actually goes out as the `LinkedIn-Version` header | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | LinkedIn's versioning page names **202607** as the latest and states versions are supported a **minimum of one year**; every page of the marketing documentation currently carries the banner «The Marketing Version **202507** (Marketing July 2025) **has been sunset**». The pin was **202411** — eight months OLDER than a version LinkedIn has already retired. The header is mandatory rather than advisory (the connector's own comment says an unpinned call is rejected outright), so every LinkedIn call was made against a version that no longer exists. Third provider in a row on a dead version: `v18` Google Ads, `v21.0` Meta, `202411` here — of the four checked, only X was current, and that was verified rather than assumed. Floor asserted as a number so a later monthly bump passes and only standing still fails. One line of `phpstan-baseline.neon` was updated, not added: an existing ignore pattern stopped matching because `readAll` makes the inferred type MORE precise (`raw: array<mixed>` → `array<string, mixed>`) |

## §36 — Phase 2 integrations hardening: Salla and Zid (2026-08-16)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ZID-WEBHOOK-001 | K INTEGRATION-ZID | A Zid delivery is authenticated the way Zid actually authenticates it | `WebhookSignature::basic()` verifies `Authorization: Basic base64(username:password)` with a single `hash_equals` over the whole header; `commerce_platforms.php` and `ProviderCatalogue::zid()` replace `webhook_secret` with `webhook_username` + `webhook_password`; `webhookSignatureHeader` becomes `Authorization`; both credential checklists updated | — (the admin form follows the catalogue's field list) | `ZidWebhookAuthTest` (10 new, fail-first — 3 failed) — the documented pair verifies; wrong password AND wrong username both refused; no header refused; unconfigured refuses; an HMAC is now refused; the catalogue names the real header and asks for the real fields; **and Salla still verifies a real HMAC over the raw body, and a Zid Basic header is not accepted by Salla** | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The catalogue declared `x-zid-signature` and the code computed an HMAC against it. **Zid publishes no signature scheme at all**: its Create Webhook reference gives the only authentication as «If username and password are provided when creating a webhook, Zid will include a Basic Authentication header … This allows partners to verify that the webhook request is coming from Zid.» So `x-zid-signature` was a header Zid never sends — every genuine delivery was refused with «The delivery carried no signature», and the operator was asked to configure a signing secret they could never obtain because Zid has none to issue. It fails CLOSED, so never a security hole: this is X-PKCE-001's shape — a mechanism described in detail, in the place a reviewer looks, implemented against a provider that does not have it, which is worse than an omission because the description is what gets checked instead of the behaviour. ONE `hash_equals` over the whole header rather than two comparisons, because separate checks leak which half was wrong through timing and the username is the easier half to guess. `RequiresConfirmation` STAYS but now means something different: it used to encode «we do not know what Zid sends», and now encodes a documented fact — Zid's deliveries carry a shared credential and no per-message integrity, so the poll is the stronger statement |
| SALLA-WEBHOOK-AUDIT | K INTEGRATION-SALLA | Salla's webhook verification matches Salla's published scheme | **No change.** Header `x-salla-signature`, bare 64-char SHA-256 HMAC of the request body, `hash_equals`, `api.salla.dev/admin/v2` — every one already correct | — | `ZidWebhookAuthTest` adds two regression cases pinning Salla's HMAC path and proving Zid's Basic header is refused by Salla | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Audited and deliberately left alone; the finding is that nothing needed changing. One difference runs the other way and is recorded so nobody "fixes" it backwards: **Salla's own Node sample hashes `JSON.stringify(req.body)`**, the re-encoding that `WebhookSignature`'s first rule forbids because it reorders keys and re-escapes unicode and then fails for valid deliveries — we hash the RAW body, which is more correct than the vendor's published example. Salla also offers a second strategy, Token, announced in `X-Salla-Security-Strategy`; Signature is what Salla assigns by default to every Partner App and to any webhook registered without one, and it is what is implemented. A subscription deliberately created with Token is REFUSED rather than accepted — the fail-closed direction — and is left that way rather than widened speculatively |

## §37 — INTEGRATIONS-END-TO-END-PRODUCT-ACTIVATION-100 (2026-08-17)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ASSIGN-PROJECT-001 | J ORCHESTRATION | Discovered data is filed only into the project somebody ASSIGNED it to | `AccountAssignment` is the single answer to which project owns an account; `AccountStructureSyncer` files there or reports `awaiting_assignment` | — | `ProjectIntegrationAssignmentTest` (12, fail-first) | **VERIFIED** | `fdff1fc` | `projectIdFor()` fell back to `Project::where(tenant_id)->orderBy('created_at')->value('id')` — the tenant's OLDEST project, chosen because it was created first. The first sync of a discovered account filed its campaigns there, and every later sync re-filed them there "correctly" because a campaign now existed to point at: one arbitrary choice, made once, silently permanent. With the live connection's 309 accounts and one project, that is 309 accounts' campaigns in a project nobody chose. `ProjectIntegrationBinding` and its bind endpoint existed the whole time and nothing in the sync path read them |
| SWEEP-UNASSIGNED-001 | J ORCHESTRATION | Scheduled sweeps queue assigned accounts only | both sweeps `->tap(scopeToAssigned)` | — | covered by the same suite | **VERIFIED** | `fdff1fc` | Every discovered `active` row was queued. Discovery alone is what puts a row there, so the nightly sweep would have pulled all 309 against Snapchat's rate limit, into a project nobody chose |
| DISCOVERY-NOT-SYNC-001 | J ORCHESTRATION | Discovery records discovery; only real data records a sync | `discovered_at` + `access_lost_at` columns; `last_synced_at` left null at discovery | — | `ConnectionOrchestrationEntitlementTest` | **VERIFIED** | `fdff1fc` | The ads path wrote `last_synced_at = now()` during discovery, so 309 accounts each claimed a sync that never ran — and that claim is what made the missing assignment step invisible. `StoreOAuthController` always got this right, with a comment saying so. **Backfill cleared it on production for accounts with no campaign and no successful run — all 309, correctly, since none was ever assigned** |
| PLAN-ADACCOUNTS-001 | I BILLING | «Connected Ad Accounts» counts distinct ACTIVELY-ASSIGNED accounts across all providers | `SubscriptionService::count('ad_accounts')`; caps added to every plan | wizard shows «after confirming: n / limit» | 309-discovered-costs-nothing; cap refuses past the limit | **VERIFIED** | `fdff1fc`, `8cb917c` | No metric existed at all, so the cap the plans are sold on was unenforceable. Counting `external_accounts` would have been wrong in the most expensive way: consent to SEE 309 is not a decision to connect them. Distinct, so one account serving two projects is one connected advertiser |
| QUOTA-RACE-001 | I BILLING | Two concurrent confirmations cannot both take the last slot | count taken under `lockForUpdate` inside the bind transaction; partial unique index on `(external_account_id, project_id) WHERE is_active` | — | last-slot race; duplicate active binding refused by the DB | **VERIFIED** | `fdff1fc` | `withinLimit()` then `create()` is two statements; both readers saw «one left» and both wrote |
| SHARE-SILENT-001 | F ISOLATION | One account feeds one project; cross-client-workspace assignment is impossible | `mayAssign()` fences tenant AND client workspace; `confirm=true` sharing removed | — | agency A/B refused; 409 names where it already lives | **VERIFIED** | `fdff1fc` | Sharing was in the code rather than in any requirement, and the failure mode is a client seeing another client's spend. A tenant check cannot catch the agency case because both clients are one tenant |
| WIZARD-HIERARCHY-001 | J ORCHESTRATION | Selection is offered by real provider hierarchy, paged and searchable | `ProviderHierarchy` capability model; hierarchy/accounts/plan-usage endpoints | `ConnectionWizard` — organisation → accounts → project → review → confirm | `ConnectionWizardHierarchyTest` (11) | **VERIFIED** | `8cb917c` | Parent level is taken from what each ADAPTER returns: Snapchat organisations and Google managers get the step; Meta, TikTok, X and LinkedIn publish no parent our discovery captures, so their wizard collapses it rather than asking a customer to choose something invented. 309 accounts are never rendered flat |
| WIZARD-RESUME-001 | J ORCHESTRATION | An unfinished authorisation resumes without a second consent | `ConnectionWizardState` DERIVES the step from the record | resume banner + five honest states | `ConnectionWizardResumeTest` (8) | **VERIFIED** | `8cb917c` | Progress kept in the browser is lost when the tab closes, and the product's answer was a fresh OAuth for an authorisation that never lapsed. Derived state cannot drift and never expires |
| SYNC-SCOPE-001 | J ORCHESTRATION | A worker re-proves its scope from the database at execution | `isActivelyAssigned()` asked by both jobs | — | `SyncScopeReverificationTest` (6, fail-first) | **VERIFIED** | `c07f60b` | The sweep filter decides what to ENQUEUE; it cannot decide what is authorised when the job RUNS. Detaching stopped the next sweep and did nothing about jobs already queued, which then fetched data after the customer asked us to stop |
| CLIENT-ANALYTICS-FLAKE-001 | F ISOLATION | The client-analytics isolation test asserts isolation, not a substring | — | — | assertion rewritten onto the figures and the project list | **VERIFIED** | `c07f60b` | Pre-existing and latent: `assertStringNotContainsString('9999', $body)` over raw JSON, where client B's spend was 9999 and client A's project drew uuid `a6f953c9-…-e3ffdc099991`. Uuids are hex and hex contains decimal digits, so it failed at random on entirely correct data. Not re-run and not weakened — a test that cries wolf on tenancy is worse than none, because the next real failure gets re-run away |
| SNAPCHAT-LIVE-SELECTION | J INTEGRATION-SNAPCHAT | The live connection completes selection → assignment → first scoped sync | routes deployed and auth-guarded on production (401, vs 404 for an absent route) | wizard deployed | — | **BLOCKED_OPERATIONAL_EVIDENCE** | `c07f60b` | Every step up to the authenticated session is proven. The remaining evidence — organisations rendered, one account selected, project assigned, connected count 1 not 309, first scoped sync producing campaigns and metrics — requires signing in to production as the operator. Entering credentials is outside what may be done here, so this is recorded as blocked rather than claimed. **Snapchat is NOT `LIVE_VERIFIED`** |

## §38 — INTEGRATIONS-END-TO-END-RUNTIME-CLOSURE-100, PR A (2026-08-17)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PROJECT-CREATE-WORKSPACE-001 | J ORCHESTRATION | An advertiser can create a project without inventing a «client» to hold it | `CanonicalWorkspace` resolves — and creates once, under the tenant lock — the single-client tenant's own container; `ProjectController::store` makes `client_workspace_id` optional and resolves it in the domain; additive `client_workspaces.is_canonical` + partial unique index per tenant | wizard's project step is a real form: a name the customer types, and a client picker revealed only when the server says the choice is theirs | `AdvertiserOwnershipTest` (7, fail-first — 3 failed), `ConnectionWizard.test.tsx` (3) | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | **The live production failure.** Pressing «إنشاء مشروع ومتابعة الربط» answered «حدث خطأ غير متوقع.» and created nothing. No request was ever sent: the wizard read `workspaces.data?.[0]?.id`, and an advertiser has no CLIENT workspaces because a `client_workspaces` row is an AGENCY's client. `projects.client_workspace_id` is NOT NULL, so the product had nowhere to put an advertiser's own project and the wizard papered over it with `[0]` — which is nothing for an advertiser and the WRONG client for an agency. Resolution is now by account type, and it never picks among several: a `personal` account type is always asked; a `company` one gets its canonical container; an unanswered one is decided by shape and only where the shape leaves exactly one possible answer |
| ERROR-HONESTY-001 | A UX | A refusal the interface decides keeps the words it was written in | — | `Refusal` error class; `toApiError` returns its message with `kind: 'refusal'` | `ConnectionWizard.test.tsx` — an empty project name reads «اكتب اسم المشروع.», not the generic string | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The second half of the production failure, and the reason the first half was invisible. `toApiError` reads `message` off an axios ENVELOPE; a locally thrown `Error` has no response, no status and no envelope, so it landed in the `unexpected` branch and its own precise message was replaced. A distinct class rather than «trust every `Error.message`» because most thrown errors are ours — «Cannot read properties of undefined» is not a sentence to show a customer |
| BATCH-ATOMIC-001 | J ORCHESTRATION | A selection is applied whole or not at all | `ConfirmAccountSelection` + `POST projects/{p}/integrations/bindings/batch`: tenant lock, every account re-proved inside the transaction, quota counted for the BATCH, idempotent on re-confirm | wizard sends one call carrying every ticked account | `AtomicSelectionConfirmTest` (8, fail-first) — over-plan writes nothing and queues nothing; foreign connection, other tenant, other client workspace and assigned-elsewhere each void the whole batch | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The wizard looped `bindAccountToProject` once per account. Each request was individually correct and the SEQUENCE was not a decision anybody made: ten accounts against a plan with room for eight left eight connected, two refused, and nothing to undo — from the server's side nothing had failed. Partially applying somebody's choice is worse than refusing it |
| FIRSTSYNC-AUTO-001 | J ORCHESTRATION | The first sync starts because the selection was confirmed | `FirstSync` dispatched from `DB::afterCommit()`: structure, then a metrics backfill of `integrations.first_sync.backfill_days` (30, clamped to a year) | the done step says the sync has started because it has | `AtomicSelectionConfirmTest` — the chosen account is queued, the unchosen one is not | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Confirming used to leave a sync button on the page and wait, so the first thing a new integration showed was an empty project. Queued after the commit rather than inside it: a job that reaches a worker before its binding is visible finds no assignment, correctly refuses, and reads to everybody as «confirmed, and nothing happened». The backfill window is configuration, not a constant — it is exactly the number that gets revised once a real account's history has been measured |
| DISCOVERY-REFRESH-001 | J INTEGRATION-SNAPCHAT | A catalogue can be brought up to date with the token already held | `AccountDiscovery` (lifted out of the OAuth callback, unchanged in behaviour) + `POST connections/{c}/refresh`, throttled 10/min; upsert on the table's own unique key; an account that stops coming back is MARKED `access_lost_at`, never deleted | «تحديث الأسماء من المنصة» on the organisation step, with a count of how many arrived unnamed | `DiscoveryRefreshTest` (8) — names filled in, ids unmoved, no duplicates, bindings untouched, lost-and-returned both directions, viewer refused, other tenant 404, revoked 409 | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Production shows a column of raw organisation UUIDs where names belong: the 309 accounts were catalogued before this product recorded `parent_name` at all. The authorisation never lapsed and the token still works — but discovery was PRIVATE to the OAuth callback, so the only offered route to a name was a second consent screen. Re-consenting to repair our own omission is not a fix, it is a bill |
| PARENT-NAME-HONESTY-001 | A UX | An identifier is never rendered as a name | hierarchy returns `name: null` instead of falling back to the external id | «الاسم غير متاح» in muted type, with the id beneath it and a refresh beside it | `ConnectionWizard.test.tsx` — a UUID parent renders the unavailable label, and the count of unnamed parents is stated | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The fallback `'name' => $id` is precisely why production looks like a debug console. An id shown as a name claims the provider called it that; saying the name is unavailable is true, and it points at the thing that fixes it |

## §39 — INTEGRATIONS-END-TO-END-RUNTIME-CLOSURE-100, PR B (2026-08-17)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| METRICS-RUN-PROJECT-001 | F ISOLATION | A metrics run belongs to the project the account was ASSIGNED to | `AccountMetricsSyncer::projectIdFor()` reads `AccountAssignment` first, falling back only to an existing campaign's project; an unassigned account is refused with status `awaiting_assignment` before any provider call; `metric_sync_runs.project_id` becomes nullable | — | `SyncCheckpointAndHealthTest` (13, fail-first — all 13 failed) | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | **The half of ASSIGN-PROJECT-001 that survived its own fix.** `AccountStructureSyncer` had this exact method and this exact bug and was corrected in `fdff1fc`; `AccountMetricsSyncer` has its own copy and was not. `MetricSyncRun` carries `BelongsToProject` and `SyncRunController` reads runs project-scoped, so a run for an account assigned to client B was filed under client A's OLDEST project for as long as it had no campaigns yet — which is precisely the window a FIRST sync runs in. Client A then read sync history naming client B's provider, account and row counts, and `DataFreshnessService` computed A's freshness from B's runs. The `NOT NULL` on `project_id` was the REASON for the fallback: the column's own comment said «a run with no home would be unreadable», so a schema rule forced a claim the data could not support. Existing rows are left exactly as they are — they record what the product did, and silently re-attributing history replaces one unfounded claim with another |
| SYNC-CHECKPOINT-001 | J ORCHESTRATION | An account records what a sync attempt actually did | additive `last_sync_attempt_at`, `last_sync_error_category`, `next_sync_at`; written from `finish()` — the one place every outcome passes through | account rows carry health, attempt, category and next-sync | same suite | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | `last_synced_at` alone had to stand in for «never tried», «tried and failed» and «succeeded, due again at 03:30», and all three render as an absent or old date — so a broken integration and a brand-new one were the same pixel on every screen. Worse, the stamp was written inside `retainRaw()`: a connector that is not an `ApiAdvertisingConnector` synced successfully and the account still read «never synced», and the stamp landed BEFORE `finish()` decided the status, so a run ending «the provider returned no insight rows» had already claimed a sync. A checkpoint belongs to the outcome, so it is written where the outcome is decided. Nothing is backfilled: null means «never recorded», which is the truth for every pre-existing row |
| ACCOUNT-HEALTH-001 | A UX | Health is per ACCOUNT, and a connection summarises rather than claims | `AccountHealth` — seven states in «most actionable first» order, each naming a different person's next move; `summarise()` returns counts; `ConnectionWizardState` carries the summary | per-account state chip where accounts are chosen, in words and colour | same suite — precedence proven in both directions (revoked over history, access-lost over stale, delayed ≠ failed) | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Ten accounts behind one Snapchat authorisation, nine syncing and one whose access was withdrawn, rendered as a single green «متصل» — and that one account is the only fact on the card anybody needed. `DELAYED` is deliberately distinct from `FAILED`: nothing errored, the data is merely older than the schedule allows, and reporting a late sweep as an error sends somebody to reconnect an authorisation that is working. Derived, never stored — a health column is a second opinion that drifts from the timestamps it was computed from, and the drift shows up as a green tick over an account that has not synced since Tuesday |
| COMMERCE-PROJECT-001 | F ISOLATION | A store's data goes where somebody assigned it, and nowhere else | `StoreSyncer::projectIdFor()` reads `AccountAssignment`; an unassigned store is refused with `awaiting_assignment` before any provider call; `SyncStoresCommand` sweeps assigned stores only; `SyncStoreJob` and the commerce webhook re-prove scope at execution; `ConfirmAccountSelection` accepts `store` accounts through the same transaction, lock and workspace fence | — | `CommerceAssignmentAndQuotaTest` (6), `OneOwnershipRuleTest` | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The THIRD copy of the same rule, and the worst of the three because commerce had no assignment concept at all to fall back FROM. `ProjectStores` said so in its own comment: «which project such a store will land in is not knowable until the first sweep files it». So a Salla or Zid store's orders and revenue were filed into the tenant's OLDEST project and then made permanent, because the next sweep found orders already there and «correctly» kept filing to the same place |
| COMMERCE-QUOTA-001 | I BILLING | A store costs nothing against a cap that is about advertising | `SubscriptionService::count('ad_accounts')` and `AccountAssignment::assignedCountFor()` both narrow to `account_type = 'ad_account'` | — | `CommerceAssignmentAndQuotaTest` — 2 Snapchat + 1 Meta + 1 Google = 4, and adding Salla + Zid leaves it 4 | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Introduced by the fix above and caught before it shipped: «Connected Ad Accounts» counted every active binding, which was correct while only an ad account could hold one. The moment a store could, connecting a SHOP would silently spend a slot on a cap the customer is sold on the six ADVERTISING platforms. No store quota is invented in its place — stores answer to the caps the product actually sells: projects, client workspaces, subscription state |
| WEBHOOK-ASSIGN-001 | J ORCHESTRATION | A delivery for an unassigned account queues nothing | `IntegrationWebhookController::scheduleSync()` asks `isActivelyAssigned` before dispatching, and records the delivery as `ignored` with the reason | — | `IntegrationWebhookTest` (12, fixtures now assigned) | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The jobs re-prove their own scope, so nothing would have been written either way. What this adds is that nothing is QUEUED: a provider pushing events for an authorisation whose accounts were never attached would fill the queue with work that exists only to refuse itself — and every one of those refusals reads as activity on a connection that is doing nothing. Signature verification, the 401 for an unsigned delivery and the idempotency ledger are untouched: this is about what happens after a delivery is accepted as genuine |
| NO-FOURTH-COPY-001 | F ISOLATION | No fourth copy of the ownership rule exists | repository-wide sweep of `orderBy('created_at')`, `first()`, `value('id')` and every `'project_id' =>` write across structure, metrics, commerce, webhooks, polling, imports, reports and reconciliation | — | `OneOwnershipRuleTest` holds the INVARIANT rather than any one caller | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Two hits remain and both are correct: `ProjectOrders::forWindow()` sorts orders WITHIN a project it was given, and `InsightRowNormaliser` takes the project from the `ExternalCampaign` the row maps to — which is itself filed from the binding. Every other integration write takes its project from a value passed down from the assignment |
| SYNC-CONFIG-001 | J ORCHESTRATION | The backfill, restate and staleness windows are configuration, not literals | `config/integrations.php` — `first_sync.backfill_days` (30), `incremental.restate_days` (7), `health.stale_after_hours` (48), `locking.account_sync_seconds` | — | exercised by the suites above | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Each of these is a number that gets revised once a real account's history and a real provider's rate limit have been measured, and a number that has to be revised inside a service is a number nobody revises. The first-sync and settling windows are now visibly different DECISIONS rather than two literals that happen to differ |
## §40 — INTEGRATIONS-END-TO-END-RUNTIME-CLOSURE-100, PR C (2026-08-17)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CAMPAIGNS-VISIBLE-001 | B CAMPAIGNS | A synced platform campaign appears on the Campaigns page | `ImportExternalCampaigns` adopts each imported `ExternalCampaign` into a `UnifiedCampaign`, keyed `firstOrCreate` on `(project_id, name)` — the table's own unique key — and only when the link is absent | — (the page already lists them) | `DownstreamActivationTest` (9, fail-first), `RuntimeClosureEdgeCasesTest` (9) | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The Campaigns page lists `unified_campaigns`. **Nothing in the sync path had ever created one** — `unified_campaign_id` was only ever set by hand, by a request conversion, or by the demo seeder. So a real first sync filled `external_campaigns` correctly, attached every metric correctly, and `/app/campaigns` was empty with no error and nothing to press. Keyed on `(project_id, name)` because creating one per platform campaign unconditionally dies on a 23505 the moment two ad accounts in one project run a campaign of the same name — an agency with two Snapchat accounts both running «Ramadan Sale» is not exotic. Adoption happens ONCE: a campaign somebody has renamed, merged or re-classified is never re-adopted or overwritten by a later sweep |
| DOWNSTREAM-SCOPE-001 | F ISOLATION | The chain from assignment to every surface is scoped, and provably so | — | — | `DownstreamActivationTest` — campaigns, metrics, the campaigns page, `metrics/summary`, `metrics/platforms`, `metrics/freshness` and BOTH clients' analytics, each asserted for the assigned project AND against the other client's | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Each surface already had its own tests; none held the CHAIN. Every assertion comes in a pair, because «the data arrived» is trivially satisfied by filing everything into the first project to hand — which is exactly what `ASSIGN-PROJECT-001`, `METRICS-RUN-PROJECT-001` and `COMMERCE-PROJECT-001` each did |
| RUNTIME-EDGE-001 | J ORCHESTRATION | The outcomes that are not the happy path each have their own right answer | — | — | `RuntimeClosureEdgeCasesTest` — multi-account aggregation into one project; a credential-less first sync is `awaiting_credentials` and not `failed`; a refusal still records the attempt; an empty window carries NO error category; metrics and structure both idempotent on re-sync; a person's edits survive the next sweep; the sweep queues assigned accounts only and asks for a settling window | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | «Awaiting credentials» and «failed» need different people to act, and collapsing them buries the one failure that means something. An empty window deliberately sets no category: an account with no spend last Tuesday is not an account that needs attention, and filling the attention count with that noise is how people learn to ignore it |
| UNIFIED-IDENTITY-001 | B CAMPAIGNS | One visible campaign per PLATFORM campaign, and never a merge nobody asked for | identity is the external campaign — tenant, project, provider, account and the provider's own id, recorded in `meta` and expressed as the link on the external row; the visible name is disambiguated by the provider's campaign id when the project already holds that name | — | `RuntimeClosureEdgeCasesTest` — a second sync duplicates nothing; two accounts in one project with the same campaign name both land | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Matching on name would merge two budgets under one heading on the strength of a matching string. That is worse than leaving them apart: the separation is visible and fixable, a wrong merge is neither. `unified_campaigns` is unique on `(project_id, name)`, so the disambiguation is what stops an agency with two Snapchat accounts both running «Ramadan Sale» from killing the second account's sync on a `23505` with nothing on screen to explain it |
| UNLINK-WINS-001 | B CAMPAIGNS | A deliberate unlink survives every later sweep | adoption fires on FIRST import only, never on `unified_campaign_id === null` | — | `RuntimeClosureEdgeCasesTest` — adopt, unlink, sync again, still unlinked; `CampaignTest` — suggestions still list unlinked externals | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | `unified_campaign_id === null` is the obvious condition and it is wrong: `CampaignLinker::unlink()` sets exactly that, so adopting on null means the next sweep silently undoes a person's decision — and the suggestions list, built from unlinked externals, would never have anything in it again. Writing this test also caught a real defect in the adoption code: the objective-refresh list was pushed unconditionally, so an unlinked campaign put `''` where a uuid was expected and killed the whole import with a `22P02` |
| PARTIAL-FAILURE-001 | J ORCHESTRATION | A provider failure after confirmation costs the customer their data, not their decision | bindings are never rolled back by a runtime failure; the account's health carries the outcome | per-account state chip | `RuntimeClosureEdgeCasesTest` — a failed first sync leaves the binding active and marks the account `failed`; one account failing leaves the others `healthy`; structure can succeed while metrics refuses, and what structure filed stays filed | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | «The batch synced» over a batch where one account refused is the claim this prevents. The binding is the customer's decision; a provider having a bad minute is not a reason to discard it |
| FIGURE-ISOLATION-001 | F ISOLATION | One figure, followed to every surface, asserted as a NUMBER | — | — | `ProjectFigureIsolationTest` (7) — client A spends 500, client B spends 9999; project summary, platform breakdown, campaign breakdown, both clients' analytics, the per-project rows and the campaign lists each asserted on the figure and on ids | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Every assertion is a number read from a named path. The weaker forms — «not 9999», or a substring search of the body — are how the previous isolation test failed: it searched raw JSON for `9999`, and a project uuid containing those digits made it fail at random on data that was entirely correct (CLIENT-ANALYTICS-FLAKE-001) |
| CACHE-NOT-NEEDED-001 | H PERFORMANCE | Data is fresh after a sync without a cache to invalidate | **No change.** There is no read-path cache: dashboard, analytics, campaigns, reports and the client portal all query live | TanStack Query invalidated at confirmation | `DownstreamActivationTest` reads every surface immediately after the sync and finds the new figures | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | §38 asks for cache invalidation after a successful sync. Audited: the only caches in the application are the OAuth state store, the PDF render handoff, the request-label lookup and the scheduler heartbeats — none carries a figure. Adding a server cache in order to have something to invalidate would be **inventing** the staleness that requirement exists to prevent. Recorded rather than worked around |

## §41 — INTEGRATIONS-VS-PROJECTS-IA-001 (2026-08-17)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PROJECT-INVENTORY-001 | A UX / F ISOLATION | A project shows what was LINKED to it, never what the tenant discovered | `PlatformOverviewController` starts from `ProjectIntegrationBinding` where `is_active`, and returns a flat `accounts` list so a store appears beside the ad accounts; each row carries name, parent name, id, health, last success, last attempt and next scheduled sync | the panel counts «حسابات مرتبطة», shows the organisation name and the account's own health, and no longer offers anything that belongs to source management | `ProjectShowsOnlyAssignedTest` (5, fail-first — at the live scale: 309 discovered, 1 assigned) | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | One line: `ExternalAccount::query()->get()`. That model carries `BelongsToTenant`, not `BelongsToProject` — correct for the model, because a discovered account genuinely belongs to the tenant and to no project until somebody says otherwise — and reading it unqualified on a PROJECT screen turned the tenant's whole inventory into that project's contents. On the live connection that is **309 accounts on a page about one**, each with its own sync button. Integrations is where sources are chosen; a project is the RESULT of those choices, so an unassigned account is not filtered out of the list — it is not part of the question the screen answers |

## §42 — The frozen gate's two real failures, and where the gate runs (2026-08-18)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GATE-OVERFLOW-001 | A UX | The integrations page does not scroll sideways on a 320px phone | — | the Connection Center's filter row wraps; the search box and the status select may shrink to it instead of dictating its width | `connection-wizard.spec.ts` @ mobile-320, now reporting the offending ELEMENTS and not only the pixel count | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | A real defect, found by a test written in the same programme. A 320px screen cannot hold a 180px-minimum search box beside a fixed `w-44` (176px) select, and the row was not allowed to wrap — so the select hung **24px off the start edge** and the whole page scrolled. The spec said «24px» and nothing else; it now names the element, so the next such failure is a fix rather than a hunt |
| GATE-LINKMODAL-001 | E TESTING | The link-external spec waits for «the list resolved», not for «the pool is non-empty» | — | the modal's empty state is nameable (`link-external-empty`) — «resolved and empty» is a real answer and had no signal | `campaigns-linking.spec.ts` — the wait now accepts a row OR the empty state | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Not a product defect. The spec waited for `link-external-row` **with «unlinked only» still on**, three lines below its own comment saying the target is «correctly absent from the list» in that state — so it was really waiting for some OTHER external to be unlinked, and passed only because imports used to arrive unlinked. `CAMPAIGNS-VISIBLE-001` made every synced campaign visible by adopting it, the pool emptied, and this waited 15s for a row that was never coming. Proven rather than assumed: the failed run's database showed `Sandbox Awareness` linked to `E2E Link A …`, the test's OWN campaign — so the first link had succeeded and the failure was on the second modal. Runtime fell from 19.6s to 5.8s once it stopped waiting for the impossible |
| GATE-RUNNER-001 | E TESTING | The gate's verdict does not depend on what else the machine is doing | — | — | a `gate` job runs `npm run gate` **verbatim** on a clean runner — same script, same config, same three projects, same `workers: 1` | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The laptop run produced single layout assertions taking **57 minutes** and wholesale firefox failures across specs that had passed on chromium minutes earlier — an environment collapsing, presenting as a hundred product defects. Chromium finished in 11.9m with exactly the two real failures above. Nothing about what the gate MEANS changes: no `continue-on-error`, no retry wrapper, no timeout inflation — the script still exits with the first non-zero code it saw, and that code is the verdict |
| GATE-CHROMIUM-ENV-001 | E TESTING | The gate turns the Chromium report renderer on itself | — | `REPORTS_CHROMIUM_ENABLED: 'true'` in `e2e/env.ts`; pikepdf installed in the gate job | `report-pdf-download.spec.ts` — unchanged, and now reaching the renderer it asserts | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | `config/reports.php` defaults the switch to FALSE so machines without Node/Chromium keep working. `e2e/env.ts` set where the print browser should go and nothing turned it on — on the developer's machine `backend/.env:88` did, and on a clean runner nothing did. The export fell to the Dompdf fallback, `ReportDownloadController` refused it on `renderer_version`, no download event fired, and the spec timed out at 90s **on all three browsers** — which is what identical failures across three engines usually mean. Fixed in the gate's own env, not in the CI job: a gate whose verdict depends on a file not in the repository is not a gate, it is a local habit |
| GATE-SNAPSHOT-PLATFORM-001 | E TESTING | Visual baselines exist for the platform the gate runs on | — | 8 `*-chromium-linux.png` baselines committed beside the existing darwin ones | `auth-visual.spec.ts`, `homepage.spec.ts` | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Playwright names snapshots per platform, and every committed baseline was `-darwin`. On Linux the specs reported «A snapshot doesn't exist … writing actual» — not a regression, the honest consequence of moving the gate to Linux. The baselines are the runner's own renders, taken from the failed run's artefact and **reviewed before being accepted**: the Arabic RTL login page is complete and correctly laid out, so it is a baseline rather than a rubber stamp. Cross-platform comparison was rejected as the alternative — font rendering genuinely differs, so it would fail on non-defects |

## §43 — The Connection Command Center: discovered ≠ enabled ≠ assigned (2026-08-18)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LIFECYCLE-001 | A DATA | Discovered, enabled, excluded and assigned are four states, not one word | `AccountLifecycle`; `external_accounts.management_state` (nullable = discovered) | four labelled chips, each with its own words and tint | `AccountInventoryTest` ×4, `AccountInventoryPanel.test.tsx` | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | One real Snapchat authorisation returned **309** accounts and the product had one word — «متصل» — for all of them. `assigned` is DERIVED from `ProjectIntegrationBinding` and is deliberately **not storable**: a second copy of «who owns this account» is the exact defect RUNTIME-100 spent three PRs removing. Assignment outranks curation, and excluding an assigned account is refused (409) rather than silently losing to that rule |
| INVENTORY-001 | A UX | One inventory across every connection and all 8 providers, ad accounts and stores together | `AccountInventoryController::index` | `AccountInventoryPanel`, mounted under the platform + store panels | `AccountInventoryTest::test_stores_and_ad_accounts_are_listed_together` | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The wizard answers «which of these 309 do I want» once, during a flow. This answers the question the customer has every day after: what does the platform reach, what is it doing with it, and where. Tenant-scoped because a source belongs to the tenant and is LENT to a project |
| INVENTORY-COUNT-001 | A UX | The counts describe the whole; the list describes the filtered part | summary computed before the filters | «٤ من ٣٠٩» on every chip | `test_the_summary_counts_the_whole_inventory_not_the_filtered_page` | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | A summary computed after the filter says «4 of 4» — true of the page, useless to the person deciding whether they have finished choosing |
| INVENTORY-PAGE-001 | A CORRECTNESS | A filtered page is full | state filters expressed in SQL, incl. the two that have no column | — | `test_a_filtered_page_is_full` | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | `assigned` lives in the bindings and `discovered` is the ABSENCE of a decision; filtering either in PHP after paginating returns a page short by however many rows the filter removed, and the customer pages through an inventory that appears to have holes in it |
| NAME-FIRST-001 | A UX | A raw identifier is never presented as a name | `AccountLabel` — `name` / `reference` / `named_by_provider`, three fields, never merged | name leads; the id is a labelled reference; an unnamed row says the blank is the provider's | `test_an_identifier_is_never_presented_as_a_name`, `test_a_long_numeric_identifier_is_not_a_name`, `test_a_real_name_is_untouched` | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Choosing between two UUIDs is not a hard question, it is an unanswerable one — and every such row is a chance to attach the wrong client's spend to the wrong project. Connectors normalise a missing name by writing the external id into `name`, so `name === external_id` is the ABSENCE of a name, already stored. A short numeric string is deliberately not matched: «2024» is a plausible name and refusing it would invent a blank the provider did not have |
| CURATION-BULK-001 | A UX | One decision may cover many accounts, atomically | `setStateBulk`, ≤500 ids, whole-or-nothing in a transaction | one bulk bar; `exclude` disabled while an assigned account is selected | `test_a_bulk_exclusion_containing_an_assigned_account_moves_nothing`, `test_a_clean_bulk_exclusion_moves_every_account_named`, panel test asserts ONE call | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The real number is 309. A half-applied bulk action leaves the customer unable to tell which half, and their only recourse is to check all 309 by hand |
| BACKFILL-001 | A PIPELINE | History the scheduled sweep will never cover can be pulled deliberately | `backfill`, ≤90 days inclusive, dispatches `SyncAccountMetricsJob` | a per-row dialog with locale-independent `YYYY-MM-DD` text inputs | `test_a_backfill_queues_the_requested_window`, `..._is_refused`, `test_the_backfill_window_boundary_is_exact`, `test_a_reversed_window_is_refused` | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Refused (409) for an account no project owns — the same fault RUNTIME-100 closed everywhere else: data with nowhere honest to land. Native `type="date"` is deliberately NOT used; it renders in the browser's locale, so the value read back would not be the value stored |
| ACCOUNT-LOGS-001 | A OPS | Sync history is readable per ACCOUNT, not only per provider | `logs`, project-scoped `MetricSyncRun` filtered by `external_account_id` | a per-row dialog showing each run's window, count and raw error | covered by the inventory suite's fixtures | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | With ten accounts behind one authorisation, a provider-level log is nine other accounts' noise. Errors are shown exactly as recorded: a log that tidies its own errors is a log nobody can debug from |
| HEALTH-HONESTY-002 | A UX | An account nothing has ever tried to sync has no health, not a green one | `health` returned only for `assigned` rows | no badge rather than a neutral one | `test_an_unassigned_account_has_no_health_rather_than_a_green_tick` | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Extends ACCOUNT-HEALTH-001 to the inventory: inventing a state for 305 discovered rows is how a page stops meaning anything |
| COMMERCE-QUOTA-002 | B BILLING | Every row says whether it spends an ad-account slot | `counts_toward_ad_account_quota` | a chip on store rows | `test_a_store_row_says_it_costs_no_ad_account_slot` | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | COMMERCE-QUOTA-001 said on the row rather than left to be inferred. No store quota is invented that the product does not sell |
| I18N-PROVIDERS-001 | E I18N | Provider and account-type names are translated, not keys | `lang/{ar,en}/providers.php`, `lang/{ar,en}/integrations.php` | server-supplied labels rendered as-is | asserted through the label tests | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | «snapchat» in the middle of an Arabic sentence is how a product tells its customer it was assembled rather than written. `google` and `google_ads` are BOTH real keys — stored rows say one, the connector registers the other |
| DISCONNECT-UI-001 | A UX | Disconnecting is reachable, and says what it costs before it happens | `ProviderConnectionController::revoke` (already existed) | `revokeConnection` client + a two-press `DisconnectButton` labelled with the account count | `IntegrationsPage.test.tsx` — «asks once, naming the number…», and «offers nothing when there is no connection to revoke» | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The endpoint existed on the server and **nothing in the interface reached it**, so «قطع الاتصال» was a documented capability the customer could not perform. Revoking disables every project binding this connection's accounts feed, in every project; a customer who has just been told «متصل · 9 تعمل» has no way to know that, so the confirmation states the count rather than asking «هل أنت متأكد؟» — a confirmation that does not say what is about to happen is a speed bump, not a safeguard. It also invalidates the inventory query, or the account list would keep showing «مرتبط بمشروع» over a source nothing can read |

## §44 — The four numbers, and the six words (2026-08-19)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SYNC-COUNTS-001 | A PIPELINE | A run records WHERE its rows stopped, not only how many survived | `metric_sync_runs.provider_raw_rows / parsed_rows / mapped_campaign_rows` beside `metrics_upserted`; each of the six connectors counts what the platform handed it, at the point it iterates the platform's own records | the sync log shows «صفوف من المنصة» beside «قياسات محفوظة» | `SyncRunTruthTest` — a Snapchat body with rows and no discovered campaigns; an empty Snapchat body; both asserted on integers | **IMPLEMENTED_NOT_VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | `metrics_upserted = 0` is the end of a sentence nobody can finish: it is equally true of «the provider sent nothing», «we parsed nothing», «we placed nothing» and «we stored nothing», which have four different owners. Nullable on purpose — a run recorded before the counters measured nothing, and 0 would be a claim it never made |
| SYNC-STATUS-001 | A PIPELINE | Six words, each meaning one thing | `SyncRunStatus` — running / success / no_data / partial_mapping / failed / awaiting_assignment, used by the metrics syncer, the structure syncer AND the store syncer; historic rows re-labelled from the sentence they already carry | `lib/syncStatus.ts` — one module owns the word AND the colour | `SyncRunTruthTest` ×5 | **IMPLEMENTED_NOT_VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | `partial` meant both «the provider had no rows» and «we could not place the rows it sent» — an ordinary weekend and a mapping defect, under one word in one colour. `success` is narrowed to require metrics to have landed: a green tick over an empty dashboard is the most expensive lie this pipeline can tell. `awaiting_credentials` folds into `failed` and survives as the error CATEGORY, which is what actually decides who acts |
| NO-DATA-NOT-RED-001 | A UX | «No data for the period» is neutral, never red and never amber | — | `syncStatusMeaning('no_data').tone === 'neutral'`; `StoresPanel` states it in words instead of falling through to an error style | `syncStatus.test.ts` ×4 | **IMPLEMENTED_NOT_VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | A paused campaign is not a fault. Colouring it as one is how a customer learns to ignore the row that IS a fault |
| SYNC-STATUS-UNKNOWN-001 | A UX | A status this build does not know is neutral and verbatim, never green | — | `syncStatusMeaning` falls back to neutral | `syncStatus.test.ts` — «shows an unrecognised status verbatim and neutral» | **IMPLEMENTED_NOT_VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | **A real defect this found.** `AnalyticsPage` chained `failed → red`, `partial → amber`, `else → GREEN`. After the split, `no_data`, `partial_mapping` and `awaiting_assignment` all fell to the else — so an account nobody had ever assigned to a project was reported to the customer on a positive green pill with an untranslated English key on it |
| SYNC-LOG-001 | A OPS | A sync log reads as a record of events, not a stack trace | `MetricSyncRun::logRow()` — ONE serializer for both logs, carrying trigger, window, the four counts, duration and error | the campaign sync log shows automatic / manual / backfill, duration and provider rows; the provider's own words move behind a `<details>` | `SyncRunTruthTest::test_a_run_states_its_trigger_duration_and_counts`, `..._the_trigger_is_read_from_what_asked_for_the_run` | **IMPLEMENTED_NOT_VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Two controllers each built their own dictionary from the same row, so a field added for one log was simply absent from the other. The error was a red English paragraph on the face of every failed row — unreadable to the Arabic reader it is aimed at, alarming to the one it is not, and saying nothing the badge had not already said |
| SYNC-OWNERSHIP-001 | F ISOLATION | A manual sync is gated on the BINDING, not on the presence of campaigns | `SyncRunController::store` asks `AccountAssignment::projectIdFor()` | — | covered by `SyncRunTruthTest` fixtures and `OneOwnershipRuleTest` | **IMPLEMENTED_NOT_VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | It asked whether any `external_campaigns` row existed. An account linked minutes ago, whose structure sync had not landed — the exact state a first sync exists to resolve — was refused with «that ad account does not feed this project». The one button that could have fixed an empty project was the one button it would not let you press. A campaign row is a CONSEQUENCE of ownership, not a record of it |
| SNAP-DIAGNOSE-001 | A OPS | Production can be asked where its rows stopped, without calling any provider | `integrations:diagnose` — read-only; `InsightPayloadRows` recovers row counts and campaign ids from bodies already retained | — | `SyncRunTruthTest` ×2 on the payload reader, incl. «an unreadable body says so rather than reporting zero» | **IMPLEMENTED_NOT_VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The counters measure a sync as it happens, which does nothing for the run that already happened — and that is the run being asked about. What survives is the provider's own body, kept since INTEG-RAW-001. An unknown shape returns NULL rather than a plausible zero, because a generic «count the arrays» walk returns a number for any body at all, including the wrong one |

## §45 — One runtime, eight providers, and no step that does nothing (2026-08-19)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ONE-RUNTIME-001 | J ORCHESTRATION | There is one integrations runtime | `Domains/Integrations/Connectors/*`, `ConnectionCenterController`, `AwaitingCredentialsConnector`, `config/connectors.php`, `routes/api/connections.php` and `ConnectionCenterTest` **deleted** — 3,200 lines | `features/connections/*` deleted; `/integrations` renders `AdPlatformsPanel` + `StoresPanel` + `AccountsPanel` | `integrations.spec.ts` rewritten against the real cards | **IMPLEMENTED_NOT_VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Two runtimes, and the customer met both on one page: a grid of sixteen «connectors» from `config/connectors.php` in which every real platform was a `NullConnector` — could not authorise, could not sync, existed to be listed — with the REAL panels mounted underneath. Meta appeared twice, once as a card that could do nothing. §1 forbids the parallel registry and the null adapter for a real platform; both are gone rather than reconciled |
| EIGHT-PROVIDERS-001 | J ORCHESTRATION | Production offers exactly the eight | `IntegrationController::index` lists only what `ProviderCatalogue` holds; `ProjectIntegrationController::connect` (the sandbox) 404s in production | Drive moves from `/integrations/drive` to `/files/drive` | `integrations.spec.ts` — «the local fake is not offered as a provider», and the six asserted by registry key | **IMPLEMENTED_NOT_VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | GA4, GTM, Microsoft Ads, Pinterest, Shopify, WooCommerce and CRM existed ONLY in the deleted config, so they left with it. The sandbox stays in the registry outside production — the end-to-end suite and the demo seeder need a connection to drive without a real credential — and is filtered at the surface, so «what this product integrates with» and «what a test can drive» stay separate facts. **Drive is a deliberate reading**: §2 removes it from the integrations catalogue, and its folder links feed the files library and the client portal's attachments, so the capability moved rather than being deleted |
| NO-CURATION-001 | A UX | The journey has no step whose only effect is to change a word | `AccountLifecycle` deleted; `external_accounts.management_state` dropped; the state and bulk-state endpoints removed | `AccountsPanel` — linked / not linked, and nothing else | `AccountInventoryTest` rewritten (15); `AccountsPanel.test.tsx` — «offers no curation step, because none of them ever did anything»; `account-inventory.spec.ts` asserts the ABSENCE | **IMPLEMENTED_NOT_VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Discovered / enabled / excluded was internal bookkeeping promoted to customer-facing vocabulary. **Enabling an account did nothing**: it did not sync it, did not attach it, did not spend a quota slot — only a binding ever did — so the customer was asked to learn a state machine in order to press a button with no effect. The tests assert the controls are ABSENT, because a curation step that quietly returns is the customer learning it twice |
| SANDBOX-PROD-001 | F ISOLATION | No production install can create a fake connection | `connect` aborts 404 on `environment('production')` | — | covered by the route's own guard | **IMPLEMENTED_NOT_VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | `AdvertisingConnectorRegistry` already excluded the sandbox CONNECTOR in production; the endpoint that creates the sandbox CONNECTION was not guarded, so demo accounts could be discovered on a live install |
| DIAGNOSE-CONTAINER-001 | A OPS | The production diagnostic runs where the application is | `Production Diagnostics` goes through `docker compose exec -T backend` | — | proven by the run itself | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The first attempt answered `bash: line 8: php: command not found`. Production runs in containers; the workflow now does what `deploy-production.sh` does |

## §46 — Downstream, and a log that can be read (2026-08-19)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DOWNSTREAM-CREATIVES-001 | F ISOLATION | The creatives SCREEN carries what the sync discovered | — | — | `DownstreamActivationTest::test_the_creatives_surface_carries_the_synced_creatives` | **IMPLEMENTED_NOT_VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The suite already asserted the creative ROWS land in the right project. A creative can be filed perfectly and still be invisible on the screen that reads them, which is a different question and one of §10's nine surfaces |
| DOWNSTREAM-SHARE-001 | F ISOLATION | A client link carries the synced figures, with no session | — | — | `DownstreamActivationTest::test_a_shared_client_link_carries_the_synced_figures_without_a_session` | **IMPLEMENTED_NOT_VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The last surface in the chain and the only one whose reader has no account here. Opened with no acting user, so a middleware change putting the public route behind auth fails this. Writing it confirmed a design rather than finding a defect: `LiveReportService` degrades an empty `campaign_ids` to «nothing», never to «everything» — a link that named no campaigns would otherwise widen silently to whatever the project acquires later. §10's ninth surface, the client PORTAL, carries requests, quotes, invoices and messages and no campaign metrics; the client-facing DATA surface is this link |
| LOG-REPEATS-001 | A OPS | The same answer every thirty minutes is said once | `SyncRunLog::collapse` on the account, project and campaign logs | «٤٨ محاولة متطابقة منذ 06:00» instead of forty-eight rows | `SyncRunTruthTest` ×3 — the count, the streak break, and «a run that stored metrics is never folded into one that did not» | **IMPLEMENTED_NOT_VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The live Snapchat account produces an identical `no_data` run every half hour: forty-eight indistinguishable rows a day. The cost is not the noise — it is that the one row which IS different is the one nobody sees. Every run is still recorded; only CONSECUTIVE identical ones are collapsed, so the log stays a chronology rather than a grouping that would put yesterday's failure beside today's |

## §47 — SNAP-BREAKDOWN-001: the live zero was ours (2026-08-19)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SNAP-BREAKDOWN-001 | A PIPELINE | A `breakdown=campaign` response is read where Snapchat puts it | `SnapchatConnector::campaignSeries()` — `timeseries_stat.breakdown_stats.campaign[]`, with the unbroken-down shape still read second; `InsightPayloadRows::snapchat()` corrected the same way | — | `SyncRunTruthTest::test_the_production_snapchat_body_is_followed_from_raw_row_to_stored_figure` — the production body, asserted as numbers at raw / parsed / mapped / stored; `SnapchatReportingWindowTest::series()` rebuilt to the real shape | **IMPLEMENTED_NOT_VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | **The live defect, proven from the platform's own retained body.** The connector read `timeseries_stat.timeseries` and took `timeseries_stat.id` for a campaign. With `breakdown=campaign` Snapchat returns neither: the series IS the ad account (`"type":"AD_ACCOUNT"`) and the campaigns hang under `breakdown_stats.campaign[]`. So the loop read an absent key and produced nothing — **zero rows every thirty minutes, for a body carrying 100.17 USD of spend, 44,396 impressions, 1,171 swipes and two purchases**. The run said «the provider returned no insight rows», which is the one thing that body disproves |
| SNAP-FIXTURE-001 | E TESTING | A provider fixture is the platform's shape, or it tests itself | — | — | `SnapchatReportingWindowTest::series()` now returns the production shape | **IMPLEMENTED_NOT_VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | This is why the defect survived a full suite: the mock invented `{"timeseries_stat":{"id":"cmp-1","type":"CAMPAIGN","timeseries":[…]}}`, the connector was written to match it, and eleven tests agreed with each other about a shape Snapchat never sends. Rebuilding the fixture failed two of them immediately — which is the whole value of a fixture that is not the code's own opinion |
| NO-DATA-PROOF-001 | A OPS | «NO_DATA» is a conclusion from the body, never from a count of zero | `integrations:diagnose --payload` prints the retained body; `integrations:probe` asks the platform and stores nothing; `read()` records URL, HTTP status and the platform's request id | — | `SyncRunTruthTest` ×2 on the receipt | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The pipeline reported `no_data` honestly against what it could see, and what it could see was wrong. Naming that state was correct; **concluding NO_DATA from it would not have been** — the body was the only thing that could tell the two apart, and it is now printable on demand |

## §48 — The Campaigns page had nothing to show, and the money was withheld not zero (2026-08-19)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CAMPAIGNS-ADOPT-001 | B CAMPAIGNS | A campaign discovered BEFORE adoption existed still becomes visible | `external_campaigns.unlinked_at`, stamped by `CampaignLinker::unlink()`; the importer adopts when `unified_campaign_id IS NULL AND unlinked_at IS NULL` | — | `RuntimeClosureEdgeCasesTest` ×4 | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | `CAMPAIGNS-VISIBLE-001` adopted only on FIRST import, because `unified_campaign_id IS NULL` is also what an unlink produces. A row discovered before that shipped is never new again — so on the live Snapchat account, **89 campaigns and 1,056 stored metrics with an empty Campaigns page and nothing to press**. The condition needed a third fact, not a stricter version of the same one |
| CAMPAIGNS-LEGACY-SAFETY-001 | B CAMPAIGNS | A legacy row a PERSON detached is never silently re-adopted | `StampHistoricalUnlinks`, run by the migration, recovers `unlinked_at` from `audit_logs` | — | `RuntimeClosureEdgeCasesTest` — «a legacy row the audit trail shows was unlinked is never re-adopted» and «…with no unlink in the audit trail is adopted» | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | **The dangerous half.** Leaving every existing row unstamped would have the first sweep reverse every real unlink on production, silently. The audit trail is PROOF rather than a hint, and each leg is checkable: `CampaignLinker::unlink()` is the only path that clears the link; its one route has always written `campaign.external_unlinked` — since `280f333`, the commit that introduced external campaigns at all; `audit_logs` is created three days BEFORE `create_campaigns_tables`; and nothing prunes it (`integrations:prune-raw` retains provider payloads, not audits). So every external campaign lived its whole life under unlink auditing, and the absence of an entry is evidence about that row rather than an assumption about all of them. The migration prints how many decisions it recovered, so the deploy log carries the count. No account, project or tenant id appears anywhere in the rule |
| UNLINK-FIXTURE-001 | E TESTING | The unlink test unlinks the way the product does | — | — | `RuntimeClosureEdgeCasesTest::test_a_deliberate_unlink_is_not_undone_by_the_next_sync` now calls `CampaignLinker::unlink()` | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | It wrote the three columns by hand — a state the product cannot produce, because `unlink()` is the only unlink path and it is what records the decision. It passed for the wrong reason and would have gone on passing while every pre-existing campaign stayed invisible. Same lesson as SNAP-FIXTURE-001 |
| FX-WITHHELD-001 | A OPS | Withheld money is not reported as zero | `--downstream` prints the converted total, the platform's own total and the withheld count | — | — | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The first production run printed «spend 0.00» beside «purchases 127». `sum(value)` over withheld rows is 0, and printing that says the account spent nothing — the exact mis-statement FX-001's withholding exists to avoid. The original amount survives on every row, so both are shown and the withheld count says which to read. No USD→SAR rate source is configured (`fx.rates.driver` is null by design), so every money figure on this account is null until one is |

## §49 — The structure sync was being killed, and said nothing (2026-08-19)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| STRUCTURE-TIMEOUT-001 | A PIPELINE | The structure sync gets the time the work needs | `SyncAccountStructureJob::$timeout = 900` | — | — | **IMPLEMENTED_NOT_VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The live account has 89 campaigns, and a sweep reads campaigns, ad squads, ads AND creatives for all of them, paged. Horizon's default is 120 seconds; the work is not. Production showed three attempts at 18:55:02, 18:56:33 and 18:58:04 — killed, retried, killed. Sized to the work rather than to a default: the sweep runs every six hours and `$uniqueFor` already stops it stacking, so a long attempt costs one worker slot |
| STRUCTURE-KILLED-001 | A OPS | A job that DIED closes its run instead of leaving it open | `SyncAccountStructureJob::failed()` marks the open run `failed` with the reason | — | `RuntimeClosureEdgeCasesTest::test_a_killed_structure_job_closes_its_run_instead_of_leaving_it_open` | **IMPLEMENTED_NOT_VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | **This is why nobody saw it.** A killed process never reaches `finish()`, so each attempt left `integration_sync_runs` at `running` — forever. `AccountStructureSyncer` catches every `Throwable` a provider can raise, so `running` cannot mean «the platform refused»; it means the process went away. The pipeline looked busy rather than broken, for hours, while 1,056 metrics sat in a project whose Campaigns page was empty |
| DIAGNOSE-STRUCTURE-001 | A OPS | The diagnosis shows the structure runs, not only the metrics ones | `integrations:diagnose` prints the last four structure runs | — | — | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Two pipelines, two clocks, two tables — and only one of them was reported. The stuck `running` rows were invisible until this was added, which is what turned a week of guessing into a five-line answer |

## §50 — The two campaigns, and the retry loop underneath them (2026-08-19)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SNAP-ADOPT-COUNT-001 | B CAMPAIGNS | «87 of 89» is explained, not accepted | `integrations:diagnose` prints each unadopted campaign with its id, name, status, `unlinked_at`, `linked_at`, unlink-audit count and same-name collisions | — | — | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The two are `sbx-cmp-1` and `sbx-cmp-2` — **sandbox demo rows, not Snapchat campaigns**, first seen 2026-08-17 and predating the guard that now 404s the sandbox connect endpoint in production. Snapchat returns **87**, which the metrics runs corroborate exactly (`raw 87 → parsed 87 → mapped 87`). The real figure is 87 of 87. Each also reports `same-name visible campaigns=1`, and `unified_campaigns` is unique on `(project_id, name)` — so their non-adoption is a name already held, not a Snapchat defect. Removing them is a data decision for the owner |
| SNAP-STRUCTURE-RETRY-001 | A PIPELINE | The structure sync completes instead of exhausting its attempts | — | — | — | **CLOSED — see §51** | `c1764e6` | Production, after the `$timeout = 900` deploy: four consecutive `failed` runs, `records=0`, «has been attempted too many times». `MaxAttemptsExceeded` means the job is re-queued **while still running**, and the measured gaps are ~91 seconds (18:55:02 → 18:56:33 → 18:58:04) — the signature of `retry_after` firing before a long job finishes. Read `config/queue.php` `retry_after` and `config/horizon.php` supervisor `timeout` against that measurement; `retry_after` must exceed the longest job timeout. The §43 `failed()` hook is what made this visible at all — before it, these left `running` rows forever |
| CI-GATE-BOUND-001 | E TESTING | A gate that cannot start its tests fails fast | `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — `timeout-minutes: 120` on the job, `15` on the Browsers step | — | — | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Run `32224855943` sat `in_progress` for **2h40m** on `npx playwright install`, with backend and frontend green and not one test executed; re-running only that job reproduced it at 20 minutes against a healthy ~2. Two attempts, same step, no application code. Bounds sized from measurement — 13.5m + 22.5m + 22.5m of tests, observed job durations 49–70 minutes. The verdict is unchanged: same script, same three projects, `workers: 1`, no `continue-on-error`, no retry wrapper, no test timeout touched |

## §51 — SNAP-STRUCTURE-RETRY-001: the broker was re-delivering a job that had never stopped (2026-08-19)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SNAP-STRUCTURE-RETRY-001 | A PIPELINE | A long job is never handed to a second worker while the first is still running | `config/queue.php` — `retry_after` 90 → **1200** on every connection that has one; `config/horizon.php` — supervisor `timeout` 120 → **900** | — | `QueueRetryContractTest` ×5 | **LIVE_VERIFIED** | `c1764e6` | **The root cause, and it was not in any function.** `retry_after` shipped at Laravel's published default of 90 seconds while `SyncAccountStructureJob` declares `$timeout = 900`. Redis therefore released the job back onto the queue every ninety seconds while it was STILL RUNNING; production shows it exactly — 18:55:02 → 18:56:33 → 18:58:04, **ninety-one seconds apart**, three attempts spent on one sweep that had never stopped, ending in `MaxAttemptsExceeded`. The §49 `$timeout = 900` fix was necessary and not sufficient: it stopped Horizon killing the job at 120s and thereby exposed the second violation underneath. Until this shipped, no structure sweep on this account had ever been allowed to finish, which is why Ad Sets, Ads and Creatives were empty beside metrics that arrived perfectly well. **Closed on production 2026-08-19**: run `32264564608` completed one sweep in 667s with no re-delivery — see STRUCTURE-LIVE-001 |
| QUEUE-CONTRACT-001 | E TESTING | The three timeouts are a contract a build can check | `App\Support\Queue\QueueContract` — discovers every `ShouldQueue` job by reflection, reads every Horizon supervisor including per-environment overrides, and reports violations of `longest job <= worker < retry_after` | — | `QueueRetryContractTest::test_the_shipped_configuration_satisfies_the_queue_timeout_contract`, `…retry_after_is_above_every_worker_and_job_timeout`, `…the_structure_job_cannot_be_redelivered_while_it_is_still_running` | **LIVE_VERIFIED** | `c1764e6` | 2,287 backend tests passed for as long as the defect existed, because every one of them tested behaviour INSIDE a job and none tested the conditions under which a job is allowed to finish. Jobs are discovered rather than listed: a list is a thing somebody forgets, and the failure this guards against is precisely a long job arriving beside a `retry_after` nobody revisited. The test reads the connection from `deploy/backend.production.env.example` rather than `config('queue.default')` — the suite runs on `sync`, which executes inline and can never re-deliver anything, so asserting against the test connection would have asserted nothing at all |
| QUEUE-CONTRACT-FAILFIRST-001 | E TESTING | The check is proven capable of failing | — | — | `QueueRetryContractTest::test_the_contract_rejects_the_configuration_that_was_live`, `…rejects_a_worker_timeout_below_the_longest_job` | **VERIFIED** | `c1764e6` | Both halves of the ordering are asserted against the values production actually held.
