Reverting `retry_after` to 90 in the working tree failed **3 of the 5** tests before the fix was restored — a green suite otherwise proves only that today's numbers happen to be fine |
| QUEUE-MARGIN-001 | A PIPELINE | The margin is derived, then measured | `config/queue.php` header records the derivation | — | — | **LIVE_VERIFIED** | `c1764e6` | A Snapchat structure sweep is four paged endpoint walks. `PlatformHttp` allows 4 attempts per request at a 60s HTTP timeout and honours `Retry-After` up to 120s; the realistic bad day is ~60 requests at a couple of seconds (~120s) plus one full throttle wait on each of the four walks (~480s) — around ten minutes, which is what the 900s job ceiling was sized for. `retry_after` is that ceiling **plus 300s**: the window between the timeout alarm firing and the worker releasing the job, during which `failed()` writes the run row and Horizon records the failure. 1.33× the job timeout, deliberately — a broker that waits an hour to re-deliver genuinely lost work is its own outage. **Measured on production 2026-08-19: 667s** (14:32:02 → 14:43:09, 11,686 upsert operations — see CREATIVE-COUNT-001; not a row count). The estimate came out close, and the measurement settles how impossible the old configuration was — an honest sweep needs 667 seconds, so `retry_after = 90` re-delivered it seven times over during one run, and the 120s supervisor timeout would have killed it at 18 % complete. Headroom now 667 < 900 < 1200, i.e. 1.35× and 1.8× |
| HORIZON-RESTART-001 | A OPS | A worker does not keep serving the configuration it booted with | `scripts/deploy-production.sh` — `queue:contract` in BOTH containers, then `horizon:terminate` | — | — | **LIVE_VERIFIED** | `c1764e6` | `config:cache` rewrites the backend container's cache and says nothing to Horizon. An image change recreates the `queue` container, but an environment-only deploy changes no image — and the master would go on serving the PREVIOUS `retry_after` while the repository, the config cache and the deploy script all agreed on the new one. That is this ticket's failure shape exactly: the number correct everywhere a human looks, and wrong in the one process that uses it. `queue:contract` runs in `backend` AND `queue` because the first dispatches and the second decides when a job may be given to somebody else; it exits non-zero, so a deploy that would reintroduce the defect stops before the restart |
| QUEUE-CONTRACT-VISIBLE-001 | A OPS | The effective contract is printed from the running application | `php artisan queue:contract` — active connection, `retry_after` per connection, every worker timeout, every job's timeout | — | — | **LIVE_VERIFIED** | `c1764e6` | A repository can say 1200 while the container serving production holds 90, because `config:cache`, an environment file and a worker that booted an hour ago are three different answers to the same question. This asks the running application, which is the only one of the three that matters, and puts the answer in the deploy log |
| STRUCTURE-ACCEPT-001 | A OPS | Acceptance watches the run it created, for longer than the job may take | `integrations:accept-structure` — watermark, dispatch, poll to terminal; `StructureAcceptance` holds the criteria; `StructureSweepTargets` is the one definition of the swept set, shared with the scheduled sweep | — | `StructureAcceptanceTest` ×13 | **LIVE_VERIFIED** | `c1764e6` | A fixed wait is not acceptance. The job's ceiling is 900s, so a five-minute sleep reports «still running» for a healthy sweep — and, worse, prints something reassuring for the exact defect it exists to catch, because a job re-queued every ninety seconds always leaves a recent-looking run. Runs are attributed to one invocation by a watermark taken before dispatch, and the command refuses outright if `--observe` does not outlast the job's own timeout. Seven refusals, each a way the sweep actually failed or could fail silently: fewer runs than accounts (a unique-job lock left by a killed attempt), more runs than accounts (re-delivery), `MaxAttemptsExceeded`, still `running` at the window's close, `success` with `records=0` (which the syncer cannot produce), `no_data` over a payload carrying rows (SNAP-BREAKDOWN-001 restated as a check), and any run already open before the check started (the §49 symptom, which accepting on top of would hide) |

## §52 — SNAP-STRUCTURE-RETRY-001 closed live, and the hierarchy counted (2026-08-19)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| STRUCTURE-LIVE-001 | A PIPELINE | One structure sweep starts once, is not re-delivered, and finishes having written something | — | — | production run `32264564608` | **LIVE_VERIFIED** | `c1764e6` | `bd34d613-9a69-4f8a-b9c6-edb3e217499a` — `success`, **11,686 upsert operations** (the run column is named `records`, which CREATIVE-COUNT-001 shows is a miscount), 14:32:02 → 14:43:09, **667s**. The poll printed `1/1 run(s) started` for eleven minutes and never `2/1`; no `MaxAttemptsExceeded`, no stale `running` row, no duplicate. The three `failed` rows still visible at 12:55:01 → 12:56:32 → 12:58:03 are the old configuration's last gasp, ninety-one seconds apart to the second — the same account, the same job, before and after one config change |
| HIERARCHY-COUNTS-001 | A OPS | The four levels are counted, and unplaced rows are named | `integrations:diagnose --hierarchy` — campaigns / ad_squads / ads / creatives for THIS account, plus ads with no ad squad and creatives with no ad | — | `HierarchyCountsTest` ×6 | **LIVE_VERIFIED** | `8ee280e` | «11,686» says nothing about SHAPE: every row could be a campaign, or every ad could be filed under nothing, and the total would read the same. Counts say what was discovered; placement says whether any screen can reach it, because most of them walk the hierarchy downwards from the campaign. A level at 0 is called out explicitly rather than printed quietly |
| HIERARCHY-ORPHAN-TRUTH-001 | A OPS | «orphan ad squads» is not reported as a number the schema makes impossible | — | — | `HierarchyCountsTest::test_an_ad_squad_with_no_campaign_cannot_be_stored_at_all`, `…an_ad_with_no_campaign…` | **VERIFIED** | `8ee280e` | **The first draft of this section had an orphan column per level, and it would have printed 0 for ever.** `external_ad_sets.external_campaign_id` and `external_ads.external_campaign_id` are NOT NULL with cascading foreign keys — a row naming an undiscovered parent is REJECTED at import, not stored detached. Those rows are counted as `skipped` and make the run `partial_mapping`, which is where the number actually lives. Two placements CAN go wrong and are counted: an ad with no ad squad (correct on LinkedIn and Google, a defect on Snapchat, so it is stated and left to be read against the platform) and a creative with no ad. The two tests assert the database refuses the impossible states, which is what stops the reassuring column being added back |
| DIAGNOSE-NO-METRICS-001 | A OPS | A missing METRICS run does not silence the STRUCTURE report | `reportAccount` no longer returns early when `MetricSyncRun` is empty | — | `HierarchyCountsTest` ×4 exercise exactly this path | **VERIFIED** | `8ee280e` | Found while testing the hierarchy section: an account with no metrics run printed one line and then said nothing — no structure runs, no hierarchy, no downstream, however much of all three it held. Structure and metrics are two pipelines on two clocks and this command exists to report them separately; staying silent about one because the other has not run is the same failure in miniature as the one being diagnosed |

## §53 — Phase 2 on production, and what the counts exposed (2026-08-19)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| HIERARCHY-LIVE-001 | A PIPELINE | The four levels are counted on the live account | — | — | production run `32273929086` | **LIVE_VERIFIED** | `8ee280e` | RazzahAvenu Self Service → «رزه افينيو»: **ad_squads 187, ads 5,706, creatives 1,451**, under **87 Snapchat campaigns**. The campaign row reads **89** because it counts `external_campaigns` for the account, and two of those are sandbox residue (`sbx-cmp-1`, `sbx-cmp-2`) that Snapchat never returned — SNAP-ADOPT-COUNT-001. **89 is not Snapchat's campaign count and must not be quoted as one**; the metrics runs corroborate 87 exactly (`raw 87 → parsed 87 → mapped 87`). Read-only; no provider was called |
| HIERARCHY-PLACEMENT-SQUAD-001 | A PIPELINE | Snapchat ads are placed by their ad squad | — | — | production run `32273929086` | **LIVE_VERIFIED** | `8ee280e` | **`ads with no ad squad: 0`**, printed by that run. On Snapchat an ad is placed BY its squad, so anything above 0 would be a defect. This sub-claim is unaffected by CREATIVE-AD-RELATION-001 and stands as measured |
| HIERARCHY-PLACEMENT-ORPHAN-001 | A OPS | An orphaned ad squad or ad cannot be stored | — | — | `HierarchyCountsTest` ×2 | **VERIFIED** | `8ee280e` | Schema-rejected: `external_campaign_id` is NOT NULL with a cascading FK on both tables, so such rows are refused at import and counted as `skipped`. Independent of the creative relation |
| HIERARCHY-PLACEMENT-CREATIVE-001 | A PIPELINE | Creative placement, measured from the canonical relation | `integrations:diagnose --hierarchy` reads `external_ads.creative_id` | — | `HierarchyCountsTest` ×6 | **LIVE_VERIFIED** | `b6cec1a` | **Remeasured on production 2026-08-19, run `32305946281`, against deploy `32305567341` of `b6cec1a`:**<br>`ads with no ad squad: 0` · **`ads with no creative: 0`** · **`creatives referenced by ads: 1451`** (distinct `external_ads.creative_id`) · **`creatives referenced by no ad: 0`**, against levels campaigns 89 / ad_squads 187 / ads 5,706 / creatives 1,451.<br>Three things now hold on live data rather than on fixtures: every one of the **5,706 ads carries a `creative_id`**, every one of the **1,451 creatives is reachable from at least one ad**, and the distinct creatives referenced by ads equals the creative count exactly — so the canonical relation is fully populated with no dangling row on either side. The superseded run `32273929086` printed `creatives with no ad: 0` from `whereNull('external_ad_id')`, which would have read 0 regardless; this measurement comes from the relation itself and is the first that means anything |
| HIERARCHY-ORPHAN-IMPOSSIBLE-001 | A OPS | «orphan ad squads» is not reported as a number the schema forbids | — | — | `HierarchyCountsTest::test_an_ad_squad_with_no_campaign_cannot_be_stored_at_all`, `…an_ad_with_no_campaign…` | **VERIFIED** | `8ee280e` | `external_ad_sets.external_campaign_id` and `external_ads.external_campaign_id` are NOT NULL with cascading foreign keys. A row naming an undiscovered parent is REJECTED at import, counted as `skipped`, and turns the run `partial_mapping` — that is where the number lives. A column for it would have printed 0 for ever whatever happened. The two tests assert the database refuses those states, which is what stops the column being added back |
| CREATIVE-COUNT-001 | A OPS | `records` on a structure run counts UPSERT OPERATIONS, not database rows | `ImportExternalStructure::creativeFor()` — called once per AD, `updateOrCreate` keyed on `(project_id, provider, external_creative_id)`, counter incremented on every call | — | — | **VERIFIED** | `8ee280e` | The levels decompose the run's 11,686 to the unit: **87 campaigns + 187 ad squads + 5,706 ads + 5,706 creative upserts = 11,686**. Creatives are therefore overstated **3.9×** — 5,706 counted against 1,451 that exist. This is the argument for per-level counts in one line: the sweep's headline is not a statement about what the database holds. The command's own wording («stored 11686 record(s)») is wrong and is corrected in the functional PR for CREATIVE-AD-RELATION-001, not here |
| CREATIVE-AD-RELATION-001 | A PIPELINE | An Ad↔Creative relation that can express many ads sharing one creative | `ExternalCreative::ads()`; `CreativeRows` filter + options; `integrations:diagnose --hierarchy` | — | `CreativeAdRelationTest` ×7, `HierarchyCountsTest` ×6 | **PARTIAL — see §55** | `PENDING` | The canonical relation is `external_ads.creative_id` and always was — no association table, no backfill. What was wrong is the reverse column `external_creatives.external_ad_id`, which names whichever ad was imported last. **On this branch `CreativeRows` and `DiagnoseSyncCommand` no longer read it**, leaving `CreativePresenter` (`:65`, `:115`) as the ONLY remaining production reader — that is CREATIVE-PRESENTER-AD-001. Writers remain (`ImportExternalStructure:174`, `DemoCreativeAnalysisSeeder:119`) and so does the schema (`2026_07_27_120000:27`), both until CREATIVE-COLUMN-RETIRE-001. **PARTIAL until the presenter migrates**, and Phase 4/5 stay closed until then |

## §54 — Phase 3: one Snapchat body, followed to the HTTP response (2026-08-19)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SNAP-STRUCTURE-API-001 | A PIPELINE | A Snapchat body becomes ad sets and ads in the structure endpoint | no change — the chain already held | no change | `SnapchatStructureReachesTheApiTest` ×2 | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | Starts at the platform's own envelopes (`adsquads[].adsquad`, `ads[].ad`, `creatives[].creative`) and ends at the JSON a browser receives. The Snapchat-specific claim it protects is the one a generic reader gets wrong: **a Snapchat ad does not name its campaign**, it names an `ad_squad_id`, and the campaign is resolved through the squad. Asserted end to end: the squad's name, provider, status and `daily_budget` 150.00 from 150,000,000 micro-units; the ad reached THROUGH that squad; its creative joined from the account's creative list because the ad names only an id; a paused squad shown rather than filtered; and `ads_without_ad_set = []`, because on Snapchat every ad is placed by its squad |
| SNAP-STRUCTURE-API-002 | E TESTING | The existing coverage could not have proved this | — | — | — | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | `CampaignMetricsTest:441-465` constructs `ExternalAdSet` and `ExternalAd` rows BY HAND, Meta-shaped, and never runs the connector or the importer. It proves the controller reads rows it was given; it cannot prove that what Snapchat sends becomes those rows. «The connector fetches them» is exactly the proof this phase refuses to accept, which is why the new test starts at the provider body |
| PHASE3-NO-DEFECT-001 | A PIPELINE | The reason Ad Sets and Ads were invisible was Phase 1, not this layer | — | — | — | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The probe was written fail-first and the product passed it. **Both failures were the test's own**: the controller orders ad sets `orderBy('name')` so «Jeddah» precedes «Riyadh» and index 0 is `sq-2` (now keyed by `external_id`, never position, ads included); and `150.0` serialises to JSON as `150`, so a strict float assertion was testing PHP's encoder rather than the connector's arithmetic. Layers 4-7 were never broken — the tables were EMPTY because the sweep never finished, which is SNAP-STRUCTURE-RETRY-001. The blast radius was read off the code first: `CampaignStructureController` and `RelatedEntitiesController` read `ExternalAdSet` by `external_campaign_id` and `ExternalAd` by `external_ad_set_id`, and all four models carry `BelongsToProject` + `BelongsToTenant` |
| PHASE3-SCREEN-001 | C UI | The Campaign Structure SCREEN carries them on production | — | `CampaignStructureTab` at `tab === 'structure'`, `CampaignDetailPage:372` | — | **BLOCKED_OPERATIONAL_EVIDENCE** | — | Production holds the rows (187 ad squads, 5,706 ads — HIERARCHY-LIVE-001) and the API contract is asserted from a provider body. **Neither proves a screen rendered.** That needs an authenticated production session, which is not available here, and no Demo user may be created to fake one. Deliberately NOT claimed as LIVE_VERIFIED |

## §55 — CREATIVE-AD-RELATION-001: the relation already existed; the reverse column was the lie (2026-08-19)

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CREATIVE-AD-SHAPE-001 | A PIPELINE | The Ad↔Creative shape is what SNAPCHAT sends | — | — | `CreativeAdRelationTest::test_four_ads_sharing_one_creative_are_all_recorded_against_it` | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | **The investigation changed the fix.** **Proven for Snapchat only:** its ads name a single `creative_id` and many ads share it, and the fail-first test passed immediately — four ads, one creative row, all four recorded through `external_ads.creative_id`, which has modelled this per ad since `2026_07_29_000500` with `ExternalAd::creative()` as a real `belongsTo`. **Not claimed for any other platform.** What IS verifiable for the rest is narrower and is about our adapters, not their APIs: every connector emits at most one `creative` per ad row (`MetaConnector:244`, `TikTokConnector:243`, `XConnector:176`), and `GoogleAdsConnector:285` and `LinkedInConnector:178` emit none at all — so the importer can only ever write one `creative_id` per ad. Whether Meta, TikTok, X, LinkedIn or Google Ads permit more in their own models is **unverified** and needs their API contracts read before any claim. **No association table was built and no backfill was needed** — an association table would model something no connector here sends |
| CREATIVE-AD-REVERSE-001 | A PIPELINE | The reverse column is not mistaken for a relation | `ExternalCreative::ads()` — `hasMany(ExternalAd, 'creative_id')` | — | `CreativeAdRelationTest::test_the_reverse_column_remembers_only_the_last_ad_and_is_not_a_relation`, `…can_name_every_ad_that_carries_it` | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | `external_creatives.external_ad_id` is a reverse denormalisation of the same fact, rewritten by `creativeFor()` on every upsert, so it holds whichever ad was imported last. The test pins it at `ad-4` of four — asserted deliberately, so the column can never again be read as a relation. `ads()` is the honest inverse and is what readers now use |
| CREATIVE-FILTER-001 | A OPS | Filtering by an ad finds every creative that ad carries | `CreativeRows` — `ad_ids` filtered through `whereHas('ads', …)` | — | `CreativeAdRelationTest` ×7 — incl. `test_filtering_by_each_of_the_four_ads_returns_the_shared_creative` and `…do_not_leak_into_this_projects_filter_or_options` | **IMPLEMENTED_NOT_VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The filter read `whereIn('external_ad_id', $ids)`. Filtering by any ad other than the last one imported for a creative returned NOTHING, and filtering by the last returned it — with nothing in the result to tell those two apart. Four ads share a creative on the live account, so three of every four ad filters were silently empty |
| CREATIVE-OPTIONS-001 | A OPS | The ads filter offers the ads that exist | `CreativeRows::adExternalIds()` reads `external_ads` through `creative_id` | — | `CreativeAdRelationTest::test_the_ads_filter_offers_all_four_ad_ids` | **IMPLEMENTED_NOT_VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | `distinct('external_ad_id')` was really a count of CREATIVES wearing an ad's name: **1,451 options on the live account where 5,706 ads exist**, with every ad but the last of each creative unselectable. Same arithmetic as CREATIVE-COUNT-001, surfacing in a dropdown instead of a total |
| CREATIVE-PRESENTER-AD-001 | C UI | A creative's payload names every ad that carries it, not one | — | `CreativePresenter` still emits a singular `ad_id` from the legacy column; `CreativePulseSection:684-691` builds its drill-down link from it | — | **OPEN — next defect** | — | **Deliberately not fixed here.** A creative has many ads, so a singular `ad_id` is wrong by construction, and the drill-down currently links to whichever ad was imported last. Correcting it changes the API payload (`api.ts:112`), the component and its tests — a frontend contract change that belongs in its own PR rather than riding on a backend relation fix. The backend now has `ads()` to serve it truthfully |

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CREATIVE-DIAGNOSIS-001 | A OPS | The hierarchy diagnosis measures the CANONICAL relation | `integrations:diagnose --hierarchy` — ads with `creative_id = null`, distinct creatives referenced by ads, creatives referenced by zero ads; scoped to the account's own campaigns | — | `HierarchyCountsTest` ×6 | **IMPLEMENTED_NOT_VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | It read `whereNull('external_ad_id')` and called the result «creatives with no ad». That column is rewritten on every upsert, so the query actually answered «has this creative ever been touched by an import» — a different question wearing the same words, and one that reads 0 whatever the relation looks like. Now read from `external_ads.creative_id`. «ads with no creative» is REPORTED for every provider and called a defect for none. The first draft warned on everything except Google Ads and LinkedIn, reasoning that the others emit a `creative` key so one must be expected — which does not follow. Our adapters emitting at most one creative per ad row says what our code can produce, not whether a platform requires an ad to have one; an ad in review, a deleted creative or a draft is a number, not a proven fault. The threshold for warning is a verified platform contract, and none has been read |
| CREATIVE-READER-EVIDENCE-001 | E TESTING | Reader behaviour is proved by tests that execute the readers | — | — | `CreativeAdRelationTest` — 3 direct `CreativeRows` cases | **VERIFIED** | `PENDING` | The four relation-shape tests execute none of `CreativeRows`, so they were not evidence for it. The filter and the options list are separate code paths and now have their own cases: each of `ad-1..ad-4` finds the shared creative; the options list contains all four; a colliding provider ad id from another project selects nothing and appears in no option list. **Fail-first proved by reverting both readers to `external_ad_id` — 2 failed, then 7 passed restored** |
| CREATIVE-COLUMN-RETIRE-001 | A PIPELINE | `external_creatives.external_ad_id` has no legitimate purpose left | — | — | — | **NOT_STARTED** | — | Repo-wide audit, after this PR. On this branch `CreativeRows` and `DiagnoseSyncCommand` no longer read it, so the remaining production READER is `CreativePresenter` alone (`:65`, `:115`) — that is CREATIVE-PRESENTER-AD-001, the next PR. WRITERS: `ImportExternalStructure:174` and `DemoCreativeAnalysisSeeder:119`. SCHEMA: `2026_07_27_120000:27`. Once the presenter migrates, nothing reads it and the writer plus the column should be removed in a migration rather than left as a field that looks like a relation and is not. **This is a precondition for opening Phase 4** |

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CREATIVE-CAMPAIGN-RELATION-001 | A PIPELINE | Whether one creative can reach ads in more than one campaign | — | — | — | **BLOCKED_OPERATIONAL_EVIDENCE** | — | **Recorded as a question, not asserted as a defect.** `creativeFor()` canonicalises a creative by `(project_id, provider, external_creative_id)` — deliberately, so one creative stays one row — but writes `campaign_id` and `external_campaign_id` on every upsert, from whichever ad it is processing. If a creative is only ever used inside one campaign those writes are harmless repetition. If a creative can be used by ads in two campaigns, they are the same last-writer shape as `external_ad_id`, and `CreativeRows`' campaign filter — which reads `creative.campaign_id` directly — would inherit it. **Prove the shape first**, from retained Snapchat structure payloads or a live read: does any `external_creative_id` appear on ads belonging to more than one campaign? If it does, campaign filtering must derive through the ads exactly as `ad_ids` now does. If it does not, nothing needs changing and the columns are merely redundant. **Precondition for opening Phase 4** |

| Req ID | Area | Requirement | Backend | Frontend | Tests | Status | Commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CREATIVE-ACCOUNT-IDENTITY-001 | A PIPELINE | Whether a creative's identity key is account-scoped | — | — | — | **BLOCKED_OPERATIONAL_EVIDENCE** | — | **A question raised by an asymmetry in the schema, not a defect claim.** `external_creatives` is unique on `(project_id, provider, external_creative_id)` and carries **no `external_account_id`** (`2026_07_27_120000:42`); `ImportExternalStructure::creativeFor()` upserts on that same triple. But `external_campaigns` DOES carry `external_account_id`, so campaigns, ad squads and ads are account-scoped while creatives are not — and a project may hold several accounts of one provider, because the binding is unique on `(project_id, external_account_id, purpose)` (`2026_07_21_140000:106`). **The unresolved question: is `external_creative_id` unique across a provider, or only inside an ad account?** If it is account-scoped, two accounts in one project sharing a creative id collapse into one row, and the canonical key must include account identity — a migration and backfill that belong in their own functional PR. If it is provider-global, nothing needs changing. **Probe before Phase 4**: look in retained or live Snapchat structure for the same `external_creative_id` under distinct `external_account_id` values, and read the provider's identity contract if it states one. A same-project / same-provider / different-account collision test is written **only if** account-scoped identity is established — writing it first would be asserting the answer. The live account has ONE active binding, so production cannot exhibit the collision today; that makes it a latent question, not an active fault |
